| CON-48 | Backlog Health shall show, for In older Sprints and Unassigned, the columns ID, Work Item Type, Title, Area Path, Iteration Path, State, Created By, Updated By (System.ChangedBy), Created On and Updated On (System.ChangedDate), in the table and in Export CSV; Features without DoD shall show the Description as plain text (HTML removed) in the table and its CSV. Every tab shall offer a person filter: Assigned To on In older Sprints and Features without DoD, Created By or Updated By on Unassigned; "Everyone" shows all. | T | M | UC-21 |
| CON-49 | Each Backlog Health tab shall let the viewer choose and reorder its columns from ID, Work Item Type, Title, Area Path, Iteration Path, State, Assigned To, Created By, Updated By, Created On, Updated On, Sprint end, Parent, Epic, Feature, User Story, Tags and Description (ID always shown; saved per tab in this browser; Reset defaults restores the tab's default, which includes Assigned To on every tab), sort by any shown column from its header (ascending, descending, list order; empty values last), and filter by State and Iteration Path (the values present in the tab). Export CSV shall follow the shown rows, their order and columns, plus the URL. | T | S | UC-21 |
| CON-50 | Every work item the app creates (Features, User Stories, Tasks, including the review and Time Writing stories of a new Feature and the month Time Writing story) shall be assigned: to the person chosen in the confirmation, else to the person creating it. The browser and the Windows app shall refuse a create request without an assignee email before sending it. | T | M | UC-06, UC-07 |
| CON-51 | A role right "Create work items for other people" (default: Admin, Product Owner, People Manager, Scrum Master) shall let the confirmation of Bulk actions → month tasks, Backlogs → new Feature / Complete children, and Backlogs / My Tasks [Board] → ＋ User Story (under a Feature, the New User Story form, BKL-19) / ＋ Task (under a User Story, with title and Iteration Path) choose the assignee from everyone in the backlog with an email; without it the assignee is the person creating it. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-52 | Settings → Feature setup (every user; see CON-58) shall keep per Epic the optional User Stories (title, Description, order) and the default Original Estimate (hours) of a new month Task — its only Time Writing setting (BKL-28) — in the Git data repository as feature-setup.json, written clean (blank stories dropped) and with the loaded version (concurrent change refused). Story titles shall accept plain text and the placeholders {feature}, {program}, {service}, {version}, {month}, with a preview. Older files with Time Writing story or Task templates still load; those fields are ignored. | T | M | UC-14 |
| CON-53 | Creating a Feature under an Epic with a setup shall offer its optional User Stories all ticked (completing one: none ticked, BKL-38) and create the ticked ones, then the Time Writing story and month Task by the one rule of every page (BKL-28), with the setup's default hours on a newly created Task; existing optional User Stories with the same title shall be reused; a placeholder the Feature title cannot fill shall stop creation with a message. An Epic without a setup shall keep the PU / Full Release milestones. | T | M | UC-07 |
| CON-54 | Bulk actions shall list State checks for the shown person's Features and User Stories (absence Epic and Removed children ignored): a Feature whose User Stories are all Closed (should be Closed), a User Story whose Tasks are all Closed (should be Closed), and an Active Feature without an Active User Story (needs one); each with what is off and, for the first two, the fix after confirmation (a New Feature is set to Active first). | T | M | UC-06 |
| CON-55 | Settings → Storage shall offer "Load everything from Git": planning data, connection setup, on-prem view data, Windchill exports, access roles and Feature setup, each loaded on its own and reported (loaded, or failed with the reason); older Git data never replaces newer data here. | T | M | UC-12 |
| CON-56 | The release view's "Create Feature" shall confirm with the person picker of CON-51, defaulting to the person creating it. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-57 | *(Retired — see CON-66)* Feature setup per Epic shall also configure the Time Writing User Story that holds the month Task: title (placeholders allowed; default “{feature} - Time Writing”), Description, Assigned to (default: the Feature's assignee), Area Path (default: the Feature's) and Iteration Path (default: the Sprint chosen when creating; when set it wins). Setup files without it keep the default. | T | S | UC-06 |
| CON-58 | Settings → Feature setup shall be open to every user and keep two layers in Git: each person's own setup (feature-setup/<email>.json, the email of their Time Writing connection) and the shared setup (feature-setup.json). When a person creates a Feature, their own setup of the Epic shall be used before the shared one, the New Feature form shall say which one is used and offer the other when both exist. "Enable for everyone" shall copy the person's setup of an Epic into the shared setup, replacing that Epic only. Saving either layer shall need a Git token that can write. | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-59 | Settings → Feature setup shall make the mapping to an Epic explicit: a "Go to Epic #" box opens any Epic by number, also one not in the fetched data; for the chosen Epic a panel shall list, in order, my setup, the shared setup and the built-in milestones, mark which one applies when I create a Feature, and say when a change is not saved yet; an Epic without a setup in the shown layer shall offer "Use built-in Full Release (PRD, SFV, DT, IQOQ)" and "Use built-in PU (SFV, DT, IQOQ)", giving the same titles as the built-in creation; "Copy this setup to Epic #" shall copy the shown Epic's setup to another Epic in the same layer, asking before replacing one. Saving stays as in CON-58. | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-60 | The Time Writing connection shall require the work email: the form field "Your work email (required)" (as Azure DevOps shows it; it finds the user's work and is assigned to the Features they create) shall be required, and saving without it shall be refused with "Enter your work email: the Time Writing connection uses it to find your work and to assign the Features you create."; an invalid one with "Enter a valid work email, e.g. name@company.com.". The Stream mapping row of a saved Time Writing connection without an email shall say "Work email missing: Edit to add it". In the connection form every field title shall fit on one line, with its explanation below the input, so the inputs of a row line up. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-61 | *(Retired — see EPS-08)* Settings → Feature setup shall list all shared setups at once ("All shared setups", n Epics; "as last loaded in this browser" without Git), by Epic number: the Epic and its title, the optional User Stories in order (or None), the month Task's default hours (or None), a note when the user's own setup of that Epic replaces it for them, and "Open", which shows that Epic's shared setup in the Feature setup pop-up. Without a shared setup it shall say that every Epic uses the built-in milestones unless someone has a setup of their own. | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-62 | *(Retired — see EPS-08)* With a Git token that can save (not with sample data), each row of "All shared setups" shall offer Edit, which turns the row into fields in place: the Feature DoD (CON-63), the optional User Story titles (change, add, remove, move up / down) and the month Task's default hours; the story descriptions are kept and stay editable with "All fields…" (the pop-up). Delete (CON-64) shall drop an Epic's shared setup after confirmation. Changed rows shall say "Changed · not yet in Git"; one bar with a change description, Discard and Save to Git shall save all changed rows as one commit of feature-setup.json (default description naming the Epics), refused when the file changed in Git since it was loaded. Unsaved list changes and unsaved pop-up changes shall not exist at the same time (switching asks to discard), nor survive Reload from Git without confirmation. Without write access the list stays read-only and says why. | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-63 | A Feature setup of an Epic (personal or shared) shall include a mandatory "Feature DoD (Definition of Done) — required" — the DoD a new Feature under the Epic starts with (BKL-13); the built-in PU / Full Release stories bring their default DoD. A new or changed setup without a DoD shall not be saved: Save to Git (in the pop-up and in the list) stays disabled with "The Feature DoD is mandatory: add it for Epic #… to save."; "Enable for everyone" stays disabled for a personal setup without one. Unchanged older setups without a DoD do not block saving others; the list shows their DoD as "Missing" and the summary as "Feature DoD missing". | T | M | UC-07 |
| CON-64 | A Feature setup shall be deletable: "Delete setup" next to the chosen Epic's summary deletes my / the shared setup of that Epic in Git at once (one commit, after confirmation; the shared setup, else the built-in milestones, then apply), each row of "All shared setups" has Delete (also while editing; saved with the list's Save to Git), and the pop-up offers "Delete my / the shared setup of this Epic". Only with a Git token that can save. | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-65 | In Bulk actions every listed work item — in Move to current PI + Sprint, the month Time Writing tasks and State checks — shall show its number as a link to the item in Azure DevOps ("#ID", opening in a new tab, tooltip "Open {type} #ID in Azure DevOps"); following the link shall not tick or untick the item. Items without a known address show the number as plain text. | T | S | UC-06 |
| CON-66 | *(Retired — see BKL-28)* Feature setup per Epic shall configure the Time Writing User Story that holds the month Task: title (placeholders allowed; default "{feature} - Time Writing"), Description, Assigned to (default: the Feature's assignee), Area Path (empty: the Feature's) and Iteration Path (empty: the project root; never the PI or Sprint chosen when creating — BKL-14). Area Path and Iteration Path shall be editable in the setup pop-up and, while editing a row, in "All shared setups", whose rows show them ("Area: …", "Iteration: …", or the defaults). Setup files without them keep the defaults. | T | S | UC-06 |
| CON-67 | A Feature setup without a Feature DoD shall get a default DoD as a pre-fill (never saved without the user): the Full Release DoD ("IQOQ finished and Checklist uploaded into the Feature.") when the setup has a PRD story, else the PU DoD ("DT finished. This is a PU release."). "All shared setups" shall show it under "Missing" ("Default: …"), offer "Fill missing DoDs with the default" (n setups; all marked changed, saved with Save to Git) when the token can save, and pre-fill it when a row without one is edited; the setup pop-up shall pre-fill it on "Edit setup…" and offer "Use the default: …"; the New Feature form shall pre-fill it for an Epic whose setup has no DoD. "All fields…" shall explain itself on hover (the full editor with Descriptions, Assigned to and Area / Iteration Paths). | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-68 | A PAT shall never follow a connection to another server: when the views synced from Git (at every start) or restored by hand move a connection's URL to a different host, the connection's own PAT is not carried over — the connection is restored without a PAT (reported under "no PAT"), the old server keeps its PAT and the new host gets none unless a PAT is entered for the restore or another connection already holds one for that server. | T | M | UC-01 |
| CON-69 | Every CSV export (query views, Backlog Health, Windchill views, effort export) shall neutralise cells a spreadsheet would run as a formula: a value starting with =, @, a tab or a carriage return, or with + or − when it is not a plain number, is prefixed with an apostrophe before quoting, so Excel shows it as text. Numbers such as "-3" and "+1.5" stay as they are. The header row (column names) shall be neutralised and quoted in the same way as the cells. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-70 | The app shall not go blank on bad data: a numeric HTML / XML entity outside the Unicode range (e.g. &amp;#1114112;) stays as written instead of failing the render; a render error in a view shows an error page naming the failure with Reload and "Try to continue" instead of an empty screen; an .xlsx entry that unpacks to more than 100 MB is refused with a message; in the desktop app the main window only ever shows the bundled app (an outside link opens in the browser, redirects elsewhere are blocked) and the Windchill sign-in window blocks non-https redirects. A Windchill snapshot from Git whose rows are not plain objects shall have those rows dropped at parse time instead of failing the view. | T | M | UC-01, UC-08 |
| CON-71 | A Windchill export page shall list the export's **unique releases** read from the document names, which hold the release name, the release number and then the document name, with any delimiters, and may be **clubbed** over several releases (name + number pairs one after the other: "Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2_Test Plan", "Viewer 1.6 & 1.7 SRS"). Every version in the name (two or more numbers joined by dots, optionally after v, ver, version, R, Rel or Release; a single number only after one of those words; a version in brackets such as "(v2)" is the document's revision and is skipped) ends a part whose name is the text since the previous version — a version with no name before it keeps the previous name — and the text after the last version is the document name; separators (space, -, _, \|, /, :, ;, comma, +, &, "and", brackets) are dropped. A clubbed name is **one release**, kept as written ("Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2", number "1 + 2"), counted once; spacing and delimiter variants of the same clubbed text are the same release; the number of clubbed releases is shown. Each release (name ignoring case and spaces, number ignoring trailing zeros: 3.1 = 3.1.0) is listed once with its document count (expandable to the document names) and the latest date of a date column (e.g. Last Modified); the count reads "n releases · m of k documents"; the name column ("Name", else the first column naming a name) can be changed; documents without a release number in the name are listed apart; the releases export to CSV. Pulse Check's Windchill exports table shows the number of unique releases per stream. The releases shall be listed **newest first by the latest date** of the date column, the date read as the export writes it (ISO, 27-Oct-2026, 27 Oct 2026 09:15, Oct 27, 2026, 27.10.2026, 27/10/2026 or 10/27/2026 with an optional time; a part above 12 is the day, else month/day/year is assumed), releases without a readable date last, then by name. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| CON-72 | The release source of a Windchill export page shall be, by preference, the **Location** column (the document's folder path; "Location" if present, else the first column naming a location, folder or path): the releases are read from the deepest folder that holds a version — the name and number in one folder ("Remote Diagnostics 1.6"), a **clubbed** folder ("Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2", "Viewer 1.6 & 1.7": one release as written, number "Rel1 + Rel2", counted once), or a version-only folder under the name folder ("Remote Diagnostics/1.7", "CareLink/R3.2"), the folders after it ignored. The release number read from a folder shall be kept **exactly as written** ("R3.2", "rel1", "1.6.0"); numbers written differently are different releases. The document names (CON-71) are the fallback when the export has no such column; "Read from" shall let the viewer choose Automatic, Location or Document name, and the column for each. From Location the page shall list the **unique locations** (each distinct value, its document count and the release read from it) and offer "Export locations CSV" and, with Git storage connected and writable, **"Save locations to Git"**, which writes `windchill-analysis/<export id>-locations.json` (schema 1, kind `windchill-locations`: export, column, generated time, document count, unique locations, unique releases with their folders; no document rows, no credentials) to the Git **data** repository for analysis of the folder naming. The count shall say which source was used. | T | M | UC-08, UC-12 |
| CON-73 | A Windchill export page shall show the header month picker, and its Releases section shall by default list only the releases with **a document dated in the chosen month** (the date column of any of the release's documents — a release counts in every month one of its documents is dated in, not only the month of its newest document), with the count "n of N releases with a document {date column} in {Month YYYY} · d documents"; "Show: All months" (the period control in the page's source bar, QV-62) lists every release. The list shall be sortable by every column (Release name, Release, Latest date, Documents, Locations; a click toggles the direction; newest / most first by default, name A→Z), with the sort shown on the heading, and its column widths shall be draggable (kept per export in this browser, "Reset widths"). The CSV export shall follow the shown list and order. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-74 | In Settings → Access, a member shall be added by choosing the person by **name** from a list of the people in the Time Writing backlog (QMS-0025: everyone Assigned To an item there, with the email Azure DevOps gives for them; same names told apart by their email); the app shall store that email. "Other (enter an email)…" shall still allow a person who is not in that backlog, and without the backlog loaded the email field shall be shown with a hint. The members table shall show the name first and the email under it, sorted by name. Since v6.0.0 a person is added with every mapping on People & access → People (CAP-49). | D | S | UC-12 |
| CON-75 | *(Retired — see EPS-07)* A Feature setup of an Epic shall optionally name up to 5 title fields — the parts of a Feature title for that Epic (e.g. DP: Program, Service, Release; CP: Platform, Release Name, Number; ECP: Project Code, Release Name, Release Number) — each with an optional on-prem field (reference or plain name, e.g. SourceProgram). Names are unique (letter case and spaces ignored) and not {feature} or {month}; blank or repeated names are dropped on save; a setup without fields is saved as before. Each field is a placeholder {Field name} (any letter case, spaces ignored) in the setup's titles. | T | S | UC-06 |
| CON-76 | The built-in default of the DP stream ("DP [Completed Releases]", same ID as in the shared setup) shall read the DHP Release work items — project DHP, type Release, not Removed, outside the Recycle Bin — with ID, SourceProgram, PsName, Release, Title, State, ReleaseIntent and the DDT, DRfH, DRPC and DRPE dates and statuses, newest DDT Date first, because the DP page filters by DDT Date and Create Feature reads SourceProgram, PsName and Release. The approved DHFI documents (DHP.Vault) shall be their own default, "DHFI [Approved]" (reference, hidden from the menu), which feeds DP's DHFI data set. A DP connection loaded from Git keeps the team's own query. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-77 | On-prem view data from Git (at start in the web app, Load from Git, and the fallback of a fetch that cannot run) shall be applied only when it was fetched with the query the connection has here (ignoring spaces and letter case); data fetched with another query (e.g. by a desktop app still following an older shared setup) shall not replace the data here, and Load from Git shall say so and that the connection setup should be saved to Git. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-78 | A connection's query shall be any work-item query as pasted from Azure DevOps (Edit query → WIQL): a **flat list** (SELECT … FROM WorkItems WHERE …, paged by ID as before) or a **tree** or **direct-links** query (SELECT … FROM WorkItemLinks WHERE … MODE (Recursive \| MustContain \| MayContain \| DoesNotContain)). A tree or links query is sent read-only as written (with SELECT [System.Id]) in one call of up to 20,000 links, and every work item at either end of its links is read, each once in the order met (parents before their children); the view's columns and order come from its SELECT and ORDER BY. 20,000 links or more, a historical (ASOF) query or an unknown MODE is refused with a message; the desktop proxy accepts only SELECT [System.Id] FROM WorkItems or WorkItemLinks. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-79 | The **stream mapping** in the shared setup (`azure-views.json` in Git) — the connections of DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations and DP's data sets (server, project, query, view name, Show in menu) — shall be **enforced** for every user: at start (Git storage connected) a user's own change to one of these connections is replaced by the one in Git (the PAT stays), and a stream's connection from Git is used before one set up only on this computer. Time Writing stays personal and other views keep a local change, as before (CON-26). A user with **Manage shared settings** saves every change to the stream mapping (Save, Set up) to Git right away, overwriting the file ("Saved to the shared setup in Git; it applies to every user at their next start"), and has **Save stream mapping to Git** to write it again at any time; a change that cannot be saved (no Git storage, read-only token, error) is kept on that computer — not undone by the next start — until it is saved, and says so. Others see that the stream mapping is set by their admin and that their changes are replaced. | T | M | UC-01, UC-02 |
| CON-80 | The **work email** of a Time Writing connection is personal: it decides who "me" is (the access role, My Tasks [Board] / My Work, the assignee of new Features). It shall never be written to the shared connection setup in Git (`azure-views.json`); an email found in that file (written by an older version) shall be ignored when the setup is loaded, restored or synced, and a sync or restore that updates a connection shall keep the email already entered on this computer. Once per computer, a Time Writing connection that came from the shared setup with an email taken from the file shall lose it, so the app asks the user for their own work email (the start message says so); a connection set up only on that computer, and any email entered afterwards, are never touched. | T | M | UC-01, UC-17 |
| CON-81 | Every work item read from Azure DevOps (on-premises or dev.azure.com, read-only) shall keep the IDs of its **Successors** (link type `System.LinkTypes.Dependency-Forward`) and **Predecessors** (`System.LinkTypes.Dependency-Reverse`) from the links returned with it, each set once and in ascending order ("501, 502"); other links (Parent, Child, Related, attachments) are not dependencies. A view of its own — a connection that is neither a stream nor a stream's data set — shall show them as the last two columns of its query view, **Successor ID** and **Predecessor ID**, when any of its items has such a link: sortable, searchable, in the CSV and saved to Git with the view's data. Stream views (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations) and their data sets are unchanged. | T | S | UC-09 |
| CON-82 | **Your work email** shall be a setting of its own on each computer — Settings → Azure DevOps → *Your work email* — and not part of any connection, the stream mapping or the shared setup in Git: it decides who "me" is (the access role, My Tasks [Board] / My Work, the Time Writing prompts, the assignee of new Features). Saving, editing or selecting a connection, a sync or restore from Git, and an app update (Force Update) shall not change it; the Time Writing connection form shall no longer ask for it. On the first start with this setting, the email kept on the Time Writing connection becomes the setting, but only once the CON-80 clean-up has run on that computer (so an email inherited from the shared setup is never taken over); otherwise the app asks for it (Get started, *Work email missing* in Stream mapping). | T | M | UC-01, UC-17 |
| CON-83 | **ECP** (formerly QRE) shall be a default Windchill stream after S&S IT (listed under Release Details, NAV-52; an export named like "ECP" — or still "QRE", read as ECP; one named ECP comes first), read like S&S IT except for what a release is: a Location reads "Parent folder/Project number Project name/Phase/…", and each release is a **project**: the **second folder** of the Location path split on "/" (leading "/" not counted), **whatever its name**, and every document anywhere under that folder belongs to it ("Projects/123456 Patient Monitor/Design" → 123456, Patient Monitor; "/Projects/PJ-1234 Name/DHF" → PJ-1234, Name; "Projects/Lab Automation/Design" → "Lab Automation"); the project number is the folder's first word when it holds a digit ("pj_77", "PJ 77" read as PJ-77), the rest is the name; without a Location, a document named "PJ-<number> <name>" names its project; a project is counted once with its documents, in every month one of its documents is dated in (CON-73), Pulse Check's Supported line and the Monthly pack list it as "<number> <name>", and its QMS-0025 match and Create Feature use the title "ECP \| <number> <name>" (never "v<number>"). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-84 | **BRITE** shall have a place at the root of Release Details (above the list of streams, NAV-52) that opens a page saying it is not set up yet, until its exports and release rules are known; it counts nothing and is not part of Pulse Check or the Monthly pack. | D | C | UC-09 |
| CON-85 | A default Windchill stream not imported yet ("＋ ECP", "＋ S&S IT" under Release Details) shall open the import **named after that stream** (Name prefilled "ECP", title "Import the ECP export"), so that the export becomes that stream's page — and Pulse Check's and the Monthly pack's — whatever the file is called; the plain "Import Windchill export" keeps the file name as default. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-86 | *(Retired — see QV-62)* On ECP's Windchill export page the release table (and its CSV) shall name its columns **Project No** and **Project Name** instead of Release and Release name, since an ECP release is a project (CON-83); S&S IT keeps Release name / Release. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-87 | There may be **several ECP exports**. The Windchill import shall have a **Logic** dropdown — Standard (releases, S&S IT's rules) or **ECP setup** (projects) — and for ECP a **Project details at** choice of the Location folder that holds "Project number Project name" (2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th or 6th; 2nd by default), then the export's **Name** (e.g. "ECP - Type 1"); "＋ ECP" opens it with ECP chosen. The logic and folder are saved with the export (this browser and its Git snapshot; unknown values dropped) and can be changed on its page. Every ECP export is listed **on its own** — its own entry under Release Details ("＋ ECP export" after the list of streams adds another, NAV-52), its own line in Pulse Check (Supported and the Windchill exports table) and in the Monthly pack, under its name — while Create Feature still titles its projects "ECP \| <number> <name>". An export without a setup keeps CON-83 (named ECP / QRE: ECP at the 2nd folder). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-88 | **Back up and clean up** (Settings → Storage → Shared setup in Git, roles with "Manage shared settings") shall offer **Download backup** — every connection (name, view name, role, server, project, query; never a PAT or the work email) as a dated JSON file in the views-file format — and **Back up and remove all connections…**, which after a confirmation (1) downloads that backup and saves it to Git as a new dated file (`azure-views-backups/azure-views-<UTC time>.json`; queued, GIT-17 — two backups in the same second keep the connections of both, by connection id), stopping with nothing removed when that save fails; (2) writes a reset marker (`azure-views-reset.json`), empties the shared connection setup (`azure-views.json`) and removes every on-prem view's data in Git (`azure-data/`); (3) removes every connection here with its saved data. PATs shall stay per server (a PAT saved only on a connection is first kept for its server). At its next start every other computer with Git storage shall apply a reset marker it has not applied yet — remove all its connections (also ones it changed itself) with their data, once — before taking the connections now in Git; until then it shall not save its old on-prem view data to Git. The correct connections are then added again (Stream mapping, Other views). Before saving or deleting backup or reset data, the app shall verify that the configured Git repository is private. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-89 | People with the **Admin** role in the access policy shall have **View as…** (sidebar footer) to see the app as a chosen **person** or **role**; no one else has it, and without an access policy it is not offered. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-90 | Viewing as a **person** shall apply the roles of their work email in the access policy (the default role when they have none or no email is known) and show their own work: My dashboard, My Tasks [Board], Monthly Time Writing and Bulk actions as "me", and their personal views (sections, menu, filters). People come from the fetched Azure DevOps data (Assigned To, with the email when known) and the access policy's members. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-91 | Viewing as a **role** shall apply that role's areas and actions only; "me" stays the Admin. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-92 | View as shall be **read only** and for this page load only: a banner names who or which role is viewed with **Change** and **Exit View as**; while it is on, every write to Azure DevOps or to Git storage is refused with "Read only while viewing as …", before anything is sent; it is never stored, and a reload ends it. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-93 | The **stream mapping and its query logic** — which server, project and query feed DP, CP, IOCC, Content and Tool Validations, their data sets (Pedestal, DHFI, CP names), the shared release naming patterns, and saving the mapping to the shared setup in Git — shall be changed only by people with the action **Change the stream mapping** (`streams.manage`), held by the app access role **Tool Owner** (any number of members, set in Role rights; nobody hard-coded). Admins no longer hold it by default. While no member (and no default role) holds it, people who may manage access may change the mapping, so nobody is locked out. Only someone who may change the stream mapping may change who holds it, and a policy that has a Tool Owner must keep one. Everyone else sees the mapping read-only and may still add their own views and enter their PATs. Without access.json there are no restrictions, as before. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-94 | **Role rights as a permission matrix**: every menu area and every action is a permission of its own, in groups (Menu areas; Work items in Azure DevOps — create, assign to others, edit fields, change state / close Features, move to a Sprint or PI, enter hours, comment, bulk create; Release data — Windchill import, release counts, baselines, export tables (QE Mapping removed, CON-117); Streams — the stream mapping (CON-93), stream options; Setup — the shared Feature setup; Capacity and time — capacity and absence, bucket rules and thresholds, holidays and common activities, period control and locking figures, historical hours; People and access — edit people, capacity roles, role rights, View as; Reports and sharing — Monthly pack text and setup, table / chart / page defaults, shared setup in Git, master data, audit, KPI tracker; On-Demand — set up the pages, edit their rows, CON-103; manage pages and menu groups, CON-106; build the dashboards, CON-107; and, in Work items in Azure DevOps, post the PI review of a Feature, CON-113; in Setup, set up the PI review form, CON-114). Each cell is **Allow**, **Deny** or **Not set** per role. A person gets the union of what their roles allow, less anything any of their roles denies (a denial wins); Not set is not allowed unless another role allows it. The app hides or disables each control without its permission, and refuses a change in Azure DevOps before anything is sent unless every permission it needs is allowed. The matrix can be searched, and **Check access** shows for a person and a permission whether it is allowed and which roles allow or deny it. An access.json written before the split keeps every person's rights: each new permission is allowed where the action it came from was (or for everyone, where the app had no check). Denials are saved in access.json. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-95 | The Feature setup shall work in **layers, field by field**: Built in → **All Epics** → the Epic's shared setup → Mine. For the **Feature DoD**, the **optional User Stories** and the **default Original Estimate** of the month Task, an Epic's setup (shared or mine) may set the field or **inherit** it; a new Feature under the Epic gets each from the nearest layer that sets it (Built in: no DoD — typed in New Feature — no optional stories, no estimate; DPF-09). **All Epics** (Admin → Feature setup, in feature-setup.json) holds the defaults; roles allowed "Edit the shared Feature setup" change it. An Epic without a setup of its own uses All Epics when All Epics has anything set (CON-97, v7.37.0), else nothing (DPF-09: no built-in milestone stories). A setup that inherits its Feature DoD does not need one of its own; Enable for everyone keeps the All Epics defaults; files without layers read as before (every field set). | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-96 | *(Retired — see CON-97)* The Feature setup editor of an Epic shall show, for each of those fields, whether it is **Set here** or **Inherited from** my setup / the Epic's shared setup / All Epics / Built in, show the inherited value read-only, and offer **Override here** (copies the inherited value to edit) and **Revert to inherited** (the tooltip names the value it falls back to); "Which setup applies" lists All Epics. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-97 | Admin → Epic & Feature setup shall edit an Epic's whole setup on **one screen (Setup per Epic)**, next to a searchable list of Epics — **All Epics (defaults)** first, then every Epic on the board or with a setup, each marked *Uses defaults* or *n of 4 customized* (and *my version*). For the chosen Epic: a **preview** of a new Feature under it (title from its fields and pattern, Description with the DoD and field lines, User Stories, month Task hours), then four sections in the order a Feature is built — **Fields & title** (the Epic setup's fields and title pattern, Copy to Epics), **Definition of Done**, **User Stories** (for the DP and IOCC bulk Epics in two tabs, Release and PU, with the QE page and its columns, EPS-09) and **Month Task** (default estimate) — each with one **Customize for this Epic** switch: off shows what All Epics gives (or none), on edits the Epic's own value, starting from what applied. All Epics edits the defaults directly. **Use my own version (only me)** edits the person's own copy of the Epic (Fields stay shared), with *Share my version with everyone* and *Remove my own version*; *Reset this Epic to the defaults* removes the Epic's shared setup. **One Save to Git** writes the Epic setup and the Feature setup together (a customized DoD may not be empty); *Discard* drops every change. Value lists stay a section of their own. An Epic without a setup of its own uses All Epics when All Epics has anything set. Older links to the Feature setup, Epic setup or All Epic setups open Setup per Epic. The Epic list shall be at most 600 px tall (or the window's height), with its own scroll bar, the search box kept on top and the list kept in view while the setup scrolls (at most 320 px on a narrow screen). | T | M | UC-02, UC-34 |
| CON-98 | Saving the shared connection setup to Git (the stream mapping, Save to Git, Use as Time Writing, duplicate clean-up) shall **merge** this computer's connections into the setup in Git instead of replacing it: connections in Git that this computer does not have stay, this computer's version wins for the same connection, and only connections removed on purpose (the extra Time Writing connections, duplicates, and the views the Tool Owner removed for everyone, CON-110) leave Git. Personal views are never written; since CON-119 only the Stream mapping is written (each person's Other views go to their own file). An admin's clean-up (CON-88) still empties the setup on purpose. Before saving the shared setup, the app shall verify that the configured Git repository is private. | T | M | UC-08 |

The views the Tool Owner removed for everyone are listed first, and their Restore takes the removal away (CON-112). | T | M | UC-08 |

This holds while access.json is loading, when it cannot be read and when Git storage is not connected. **Bootstrap:** when Git storage is connected and the read confirmed that no access.json exists (the read succeeded and the file is absent), the viewer may only set up the first roles file ("Manage access", Admin → People & roles → Role rights → Set up roles), keeping Manage access for themselves (CON-44); nothing else is allowed until it is saved. | T | M | UC-22 |

Then (1) a backup of the view's definition (views-file format, never a PAT or work email) is saved to Git as a new file (`azure-views-backups/azure-views-<UTC time>-removed-<id>.json`), (2) a **removal marker** `{ id, name, by, at, mode, backup, view }` is added to `azure-views-removed.json` (a file of its own, so an older app that rewrites `azure-views.json` cannot drop it), (3) `azure-views.json` is written without the view, and the view is removed here with its saved data. Every other connection (custom queries, backlog views, background sources) is an **Other view** and shall be kept in Git in a file of its creator's own, `other-views/<work email>.json` (the email lower-cased; characters other than letters, digits and `._@+-` replaced), next to `azure-views.json`, each view recording its creator (`createdBy`, the work email of whoever added it). Adding, editing, cloning or Use as Time Writing in **Other views** shall save the person's file at once (merged with what is there, so an admin's Assign in between is kept); without Git, a token that can save or a work email the view stays here and is saved at the next start that can. The on-prem data of an Other view in Git (`azure-data/<id>.json`, which stays where it is) shall never add that view on another computer. The first sync from another repository (GIT-26) only adds the person's views there. An Other views file shall be accepted only when its recorded owner matches the active work email. An incoming Other view shall never replace a local My view or Stream mapping that has the same ID. Saves and retries shall require verified private repository visibility. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08, UC-12 |
| CON-121 | **Migration to an unassigned list**: at the first start of this version with a Git token that can save (and again whenever an older app writes Other views back), the app shall move the Other views found in `azure-views.json` to a shared list `other-views/unassigned.json` and take them out of `azure-views.json` in the same flow — the list first, then the setup, so a failure part-way never loses a view. A view already in the list, assigned before (the list records assigned ids) or marked removed for everyone (CON-110) is only taken out, never added again. Each write uses the version just read; a write that lost a race re-reads and tries again, so two computers moving at once end with one list, each view once (idempotent); a network or server failure is tried once more (GIT-27). The custom streams are read fresh first, so a custom stream's connection is never moved; when they cannot be read nothing is moved that time. Until moved (a read-only token, or nobody with write rights has started yet), the Other views still in `azure-views.json` are treated as unassigned. Both migration writes and any retries shall require verified private repository visibility. | T | M | UC-02, UC-12 |
| CON-122 | **Assign an unassigned Other view** (admins: "Shared setup in Git, master data, audit, KPI tracker", `storage.shared` — Tool Owner and Admin by default): the **Unassigned Other views** list shall offer, per view, an **Assign to** work email (the people of the access roles offered, any work email accepted) and **Assign…**, which opens a confirmation pop-up (`OTHER VIEWS · SHARED IN GIT`) naming the view, the person and their file. **Assign** shall write the view into that person's file first (its creator: that person; `assignedBy` and `assignedAt` recorded) and then take it out of the unassigned list with its id recorded as assigned; from their next start only that person sees it. A view no longer in the list (assigned by someone else meanwhile) is refused with the reason; nothing is written while viewing as someone else, with the synthetic sample or a read-only token (the reason shown, CON-102 style). Both assignment writes and any retries shall require verified private repository visibility. | T | M | UC-02, UC-14 |

| CON-124 | **Remove one of my Other views**: **Remove** on a row of **Other views** shall open a confirmation pop-up (`OTHER VIEWS · YOURS IN GIT`) and then remove the view here, from the person's file in Git and — by a removal marker of CON-110 (mode remove, with its backup) — on every other computer that still has it, an older app included, which never writes it back; its data file in Git is kept. The Tool Owner's **Remove / Delete for everyone** (CON-109, CON-110) of one of their own Other views also takes it out of their file. **Restore** in Removed connections (CON-112) of an Other view puts it back as the restorer's own (their file), never into the shared setup. Removing or restoring an Other view and any retry shall require verified private repository visibility. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |

| CON-102 | Every button that starts a change in Azure DevOps — create (＋ New Feature, ＋ Feature, ＋ User Story, ＋ Task, ＋ Time Writing Task, Complete children, Create Feature, ＋ Add a new activity and its Add / Save buttons), Edit, a state change or Close, Move (Sprint check), saving hours, Add comment, and bulk Create Features — shall be **disabled** when the viewer lacks the action it needs (CON-94), including the read-only state before the roles file is loaded (CON-101), so no form opens and no send is attempted; a **visible line** next to the button, or once above a table of row buttons (each also with the reason as tooltip), shall say why: "Read only: the access roles are not loaded yet, so nothing can be changed in Azure DevOps." or which permission the role lacks and where an Admin changes it. The write guard before sending stays as the last safety net; nothing changes for a viewer who holds the action. | T | M | UC-22 |

### 5.3 Fetching and saving Azure DevOps data (FET) — UC-03

*Group rationale: the user asked that "every time a Fetch happens, it should save automatically", and that Save means save and refresh.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FET-01 | When a time-tracking connection is fetched without a query, the app shall read the team's Epic backlog: the team's area paths, every work item in them, and their parents. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-02 | When a time-tracking connection is fetched with a query, the app shall read the query's results and their parent chain. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-03 | The app shall read work items in batches of at most 200 IDs. If any batch fails, it shall import nothing from that fetch. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-04 | When a time-tracking fetch returns one or more items, the app shall replace the saved Azure work items with the result and write an audit entry with action "Sync". | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-05 | When a time-tracking fetch returns no items and saved items exist, the app shall keep the saved items and hold the result as a preview, with "Azure returned no work items, so the saved data was kept. Check the team or query; save the empty preview only if that is expected." | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-06 | "Save preview — replace Azure cache" shall save the preview only when its scope equals the current connection. Otherwise it shall show "The source changed after fetching. Refresh the selected backlog before saving." | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-07 | When a backlog-view, documents or reference connection is fetched, the app shall save its items under that connection's ID in `azureSources`, with the fetch time. | T | M | UC-03, UC-08 |
| FET-08 | When a release-source connection is fetched, the app shall save its items as `releaseSource`, with the fetch time. | T | M | UC-03, UC-08 |
| FET-09 | "Fetch all" shall fetch every connection, one after another. | D | M | UC-03 |
| FET-10 | The header refresh button (↻) shall run "Fetch all" when connections exist. Otherwise it shall refresh the active configuration. It shall be disabled while a fetch is running. | D | M | UC-01, UC-03 |
| FET-11 | The app shall refuse a second Time Writing fetch while one is running, with "A Time Writing fetch is already running." | T | S | UC-03 |
| FET-12 | The data-status chip shall show exactly one of: "Refreshing…", "Unsaved result", "Sample data", "{n} items · {relative time}", "No data". | D | M | UC-01 |
| FET-13 | When a preview exists, the app shall show the preview notice with the buttons "Save preview — replace Azure cache" and "Discard". | D | M | UC-03 |
| FET-14 | When no work items are saved and no fetch is running, the app shall show "No source data loaded yet." with a link to fetch and a "Load synthetic sample" button. | D | S | UC-03, UC-18 |
| FET-15 | When a fetch fails, the app shall show the error followed by "Previously loaded data has been retained." and keep the saved data unchanged. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-16 | When saved work items lack a verified scope for the active team backlog, the app shall hide them and show the scoped-refresh notice. | T | S | UC-03 |
| FET-17 | Fetching shall not write to Azure DevOps. | I, T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-18 | When a connection's query selects fields, the app shall store those field values per item, with identities reduced to their display name. | T | M | UC-08 |
| FET-19 | When a query is fetched, the app shall read Azure DevOps field names through the read-only `fields` operation. On failure it shall fall back to readable reference names. | T | S | UC-08 |
| FET-20 | At every start (opening the app, Force Update, reload), once the shared connection setup from Git is in place, the app shall fetch the active Time Writing connection and the read-only cloud (Azure DevOps Services) connections that have a PAT in this browser, one after another, with the same saving and failure rules as a manual fetch. On-prem connections shall not be fetched at start. Sample data shall not trigger it. A Time Writing fetch requested while one is running shall join the running fetch and report its result, not fail. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-21 | The start-up fetch (FET-20) shall wait only for the connection setup from Git — not for the on-prem views' data, which is read after it — and at most 10 seconds for Git, then fetch with the connections here. It shall run as a background job ("Fetch Azure DevOps at start") shown in the top bar with its result, or why the Time Writing connection was not fetched (no connection, no PAT in this browser, on-premises); with no connection set up nothing is shown. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-22 | The top bar shall offer **⟳ Time Writing** next to ↻ Refresh: it fetches only the Time Writing connection in use, now — the same fetch as its Fetch in Admin → Streams — waiting first for a fetch already running, and says when it is saved. ↻ Refresh shall fetch the Time Writing connection **first**, then the read-only connections, so a slow or failing view (an on-prem server without VPN) never holds it up. | T | M | UC-02 |
| FET-23 | A work item shall count as **finished** when its State is **Closed, Resolved, Completed or Done** — one list for every page (Pulse Check, the Monthly pack, Planning Overview, Insights, My Tasks, query views, the Edit form). Removed and Cut are not finished; pages that list open or pending work leave them out separately (owner decision 10). | T | M | UC-02, UC-05 |
| FET-24 | A finished item **without a Closed Date** shall take its close date from the item: its **State Change Date** (the day it entered the finished state), else its Resolved Date; when neither is there, from its **history** — the last change that moved it into a finished state — read once per item version (one read-only GET of the item's updates, at most 200 items per fetch, kept in this browser). Only when nothing tells shall its last-changed date be used (owner decision 10). | T | M | UC-02, UC-05 |

### 5.4 Time Writing naming rules (TWN) — UC-04, UC-05

*Group rationale: story names are free text ("what I wrote randomly"). The Month Task is the anchor.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TWN-01 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title starts with M, given as a full or 3-letter English month name (case-insensitive, optional ".") followed by a 4-digit year, with any of the separators space `,` `.` `_` `/` `-` or none. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-02 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title starts with `YYYY-MM`, `YYYY/MM` or `YYYY.MM` (month 1–12, optional leading zero) not followed by a digit. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-03 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title ends with M in the forms of TWN-01 or TWN-02. Trailing spaces, `.`, `)` or `]` are allowed. A letter shall not come directly before the month name. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-04 | When a title both starts and ends with a month, the leading month shall be used. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-05 | The app shall not treat a month that appears only in the middle of a title as the Task's month. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-06 | The app shall not treat a word that merely begins with a month name (e.g. "Octopus 2026", "Mayday 2026") as a month. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-07 | The app shall take a Month Task's Feature and Epic from its parent chain, whatever the parent story is called. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-08 | The app shall identify Time Writing stories per the definition in §1.4. | T | M | UC-05 |
| TWN-09 | When placing a new Month Task under a Feature, the app shall choose, in this order: (1) an existing live Task of mine for that month, which is reused rather than created; (2) the one live story titled exactly "{Feature} - Time Writing"; (3) the one story holding my Month Tasks; (4) the Feature's only Time Writing story; (5) a new story. When more than one candidate exists at step 2, 3 or 5, the result shall be a conflict and nothing shall be created. | T | M | UC-06 |

### 5.5 Monthly Time Writing (MON) — UC-04, UC-05

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MON-01 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall provide, in the page header, a Team/Individual switch labelled "Time writing for", defaulting to Individual. The choice shall be remembered in localStorage `demand-planning-monthly-view-v1`. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-02 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode the page shall show only the chosen person's Tasks. In Team mode it shall show every person's Tasks. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-03 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode with no person chosen and no Me, the page shall show "Choose whose time writing to show, or set your Azure email on the Time Writing connection to make "Me" the default." and list no Tasks. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-04 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show the metrics "Tasks", "Original Estimate", "Completed Work" and "Staged changes" for the selected month. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-05 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall list each Month Task of the selected month with: Epic, Feature (the nearest Feature above the Task), Month Task, State, Original Estimate, Completed Work, Tags. Epic, Feature and Task shall link to Azure DevOps. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-06 | Planning for month M shall be locked when it is locked early for M in Settings → Period control (MON-23), or once its plan lock date has ended: the date set for M, else the default planning lock day of M (5th unless changed, MON-21); the lock date ends at midnight UTC (NAV-51), the same moment for every viewer. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-07 | Actuals for month M shall be locked when they are locked early for M, or once M's actuals lock date has ended: the date set for M, else the default actuals lock day of M+1 (4th unless changed, MON-21); the lock date ends at midnight UTC (NAV-51). Both lock dates belong to M's own entry. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-08 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show the pills "Planning open"/"Planning locked" and "Actuals open"/"Actuals locked". | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-09 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The Original Estimate input shall be disabled while planning is locked, when the Task is tagged `ad-hoc`, or while saving. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-10 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The Completed Work input shall be disabled while actuals are locked or while saving. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-11 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* Edits shall be held as staged changes until "Save {n} staged task(s) and refresh" is pressed. "Discard staged changes" shall clear them. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-12 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The app shall reject the whole save, writing nothing, when any staged value is empty, not a finite number, or negative, with "Original Estimate and Completed Work must be non-negative numbers." | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-13 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The app shall reject the whole save, writing nothing, when any staged change falls in a locked field, with "A staged change is in a locked field. Unlock the period in Settings before saving." | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-14 | Saving shall write only Original Estimate and/or Completed Work (and Tags when MON-15 applies) to each changed Task, then read the Task back and verify it. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-15 | When planning is locked and a Task with no Original Estimate first receives Completed Work, the save shall add the tag `ad-hoc` to that Task. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-16 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* After a successful save, the app shall clear the staged changes and refresh the Time Writing data from Azure DevOps (FET-04). | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-17 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The save button shall be disabled when there are no staged changes, while saving, or when the active connection has no PAT. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-18 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show a check panel, titled "Not picked up for {Month YYYY} ({n})" or "Everything picked up for {Month YYYY}". It shall list: (a) open Time Writing stories with no Month Task for the month; (b) Tasks under a Time Writing story with no month in the title; (c) Month Tasks with no Feature above them, with the parent shown. | T | M | UC-05 |
| MON-19 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode, the check panel shall list only the chosen person's stories: those assigned to them, or holding one of their Tasks. | T | S | UC-05 |
| MON-20 | A month Time Writing Task newly created after its month's planning has locked (the MON-06 / MON-07 rule with the Settings periods) — by New Feature, Complete Feature children or Bulk actions → Create month tasks — shall be created with the tag `ad-hoc` and read back with it; the Feature, the User Stories and an existing Task that is reused shall not be tagged. The form and the confirmation shall say so before Save, and the result shall name it. Both transports shall accept only this one tag, only on a new Task. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-21 | Settings → Capacity & periods → Period control shall set the default lock days for every month without dates of its own — **Last day to update the plan** (1–28 of the month, default 5) and **Last day to update actuals** (1–28 of the next month, default 4); the day entered is still open and the month locks from the next day — so the November plan can be updated until 5 November (locked from 6 November) and November actuals until 4 December (locked from 5 December). | T | M | UC-04, UC-14 |
| MON-22 | The locks shall be worked out from the current time on every page (Time Writing, the Edit form, ad-hoc tagging of new Tasks), checked again at least every minute, so a new month starts open when the month rolls over and the previous month locks on its own dates. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-23 | Period control shall show, for the header month, planning and actuals each with Open / Locked, its **last day to update** (the month's own date or the default), a date field to set that month's own last day (a later date reopens it), **Use default**, and **Lock now** / **Undo early lock**, with an optional reason; each change is in the audit list with who and why. | T | M | UC-14 |
| MON-24 | The default days and each month's dates, early locks and reason shall be kept with the capacity settings in the Git planning data, so every computer applies the same locks; read back, only valid months (YYYY-MM), dates and days 1–28 are kept. Lock records kept only in a browser by earlier versions are no longer used. | T | M | UC-14 |

For a ticked Feature the month Tasks of each later month up to the Target month (at most 24) shall be created like the month's own (ATM-11: re-read, never duplicated); every newly created Task shall get the entered hours as its Original Estimate, except this month's when planning has locked (it is ad-hoc, MON-20). Such a Feature is marked "Shared · owner {name}" in the list, the user's Task goes under its Time Writing story in the user's name, and My Tasks [Board] shows a notice naming the shared Features without the user's month Task, with a link to Bulk actions. | T | M | UC-04 |

On a Feature shared by several people who each keep their own User Story, each one is offered the Feature, and their month Task goes under **their own** Time Writing story when they have one (two of their own are a conflict to resolve), never under another person's; with none, the existing rules apply (the story titled "{Feature} - Time Writing", else one holding their month Tasks, else a new one). A Feature named after one type ("<Name> - PTO", "Training") offers only that type, and its stories without a type of their own are of that type. | T | M | UC-04 |

The row names both PIs and offers **Move Feature to <PI>**, which sets the Feature's Iteration Path to that PI after a confirmation (its state stays); expanding it lists the Active User Stories in that PI. Not available with sample data or without a PAT. | T | M | UC-06 |

### 5.7 Time Writing backlog view (BKL) — UC-07

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BKL-01 | The Time Writing view shall show the saved work items as an expandable Epic → Feature → User Story → Task tree, with the view name as its heading. | D | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-02 | The tree shall exclude items in the state Removed. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-03 | The tree shall remember the column preferences in this browser. | T | S | UC-07 |
| BKL-04 | The New Feature title shall be **free text** (1–255 characters; empty: "Enter the Feature title."). When the Epic's Feature setup has title fields (BKL-18) they build the title instead, and a setup whose titles use {program}, {service} or {version} needs a "Program \| Service \| Release" title. Without a setup the built-in milestone stories are named after the service and release of a "Program \| Service \| Release" title ("SFV : Review of Deliverables (Core 5.2)"), else after the whole title. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-05 | *(Retired — see BKL-13)* The Feature form shall offer "Release classification" PU or Full Release. It shall set the description to "Feature DoD: DT finished. This is a PU release." or "Feature DoD: IQOQ finished and Checklist uploaded into the Feature." accordingly. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-06 | *(Retired — see DPF-09)* Without an Epic setup the form shall offer the optional milestone User Stories PRD, SFV, DT and IQOQ (SFV, DT and IQOQ ticked by default) with Select all / Deselect all; ticking PRD makes the Feature a Full Release (its milestones and default DoD), otherwise it is a PU. There is no separate PU / Full Release field. They are titled "{milestone} : Review of Deliverables ({Service} {Release})". | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-07 | *(Retired — see BKL-14)* Creation shall always add a "{Feature} - Time Writing" story in the selected Sprint and a "{Month YYYY} \| {Feature}" Task. The Feature shall be assigned to the creator email, and the children shall not be assigned. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-08 | Creation shall require a dated PI → Sprint selection, and shall refuse if the selection is no longer current, with "The selected PI/Sprint is no longer current. Reload the schedule and review it before creating. No write was attempted." | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-09 | Creation shall be refused without a creator email, with "Configure the Feature creator email in Azure DevOps settings before creating a Feature." | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-10 | "Complete Feature children" shall add only the missing children of an existing Feature, without recreating the Feature. It shall be offered (on the board and on My Tasks [Board]) only where its Epic offers it (BKL-38) and only for a Feature with no optional User Story: no User Story under it other than its Time Writing story (Removed ones ignored). | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-11 | After creation, the app shall read every created item back and report "Hierarchy complete: {n} milestone stories, Time Writing story and {month} task verified." | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-12 | The view shall offer the "Source details and dates" table — with search, counts by type, and at most 100 rows — as its own **Source details (n)** tab next to the board tab (named after the view, e.g. **QMS-0025**), not as a collapsible section under the board. The board tab opens first; the choice is kept per view in the browser tab. | D | C | UC-07 |
| BKL-13 | The Feature DoD (Definition of Done) shall be mandatory for a new Feature. The New Feature form ("New Feature", also from My Tasks [Board]) shall have a required field "Feature DoD (Definition of Done) — required", pre-filled from the Feature setup of the Epic (CON-63), else empty to type (DPF-09: no built-in PU / Full Release DoD), and "Create Feature and children" shall stay disabled while it is empty ("Enter the Feature DoD first"). The release view's "Create Feature" shall have the same required field (empty at first) and stay disabled without it. The Feature's Description shall start with "Feature DoD: {DoD}" (the prefix is not doubled when typed), followed by the optional Description. Creating a Feature whose Description has no DoD text shall be refused before anything is sent to Azure DevOps, with "Enter the Feature DoD (Definition of Done): a Feature is not created without it." Completing the children of an existing Feature does not ask for it. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-14 | Creation shall always add the Time Writing User Story "{Feature} - Time Writing" and a "{Month YYYY} \| {Feature}" Task under it, by the one rule of every page (BKL-28). The Time Writing story is not tied to a PI, Sprint or year: its Iteration Path is the project root (e.g. "QMS") — never the PI or Sprint chosen when creating (only the optional User Stories go to that Sprint); its Area Path and the Task's are the Feature's. The same applies when completing children. An existing Time Writing story is used wherever it sits (not refused for another path, not duplicated), and Bulk actions → Move to current PI + Sprint never moves Time Writing stories. The Feature is assigned to the chosen person (default the creator email). | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-15 | The New Feature form shall offer the built-in User Story "Review of Deliverables - {Feature}" (the whole Feature title) as an optional User Story, with or without an Epic setup, next to the optional User Stories or milestone stories. It and those stories exclude each other: ticking it clears them, ticking any of them clears it, and Deselect all clears it too. By default the stories are ticked and it is not; it is ticked by default only when there are no stories to offer. Ticked, it is created under the Feature in the Sprint chosen in the form (the current Sprint by default; the Feature's iteration when none is chosen), assigned like the other children; unticked, it is not created. It is not part of the editable setup. Completing children with it ticked adds it when missing; an existing one with that title is reused wherever it sits. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-16 | The New Feature form shall follow the Azure DevOps work-item layout: the title on top (a FEATURE marker) with, above it, side by side and as wide as the title, the Parent Epic and the Release Classification (BKL-17); a header strip with Assigned to, State (New), Area (read-only, the Epic's) and Iteration (the PI → Sprint, chosen automatically when one current pair exists, with ↻ Reload); the Feature DoD as the body with Description and acceptance criteria folded under it; a side column with Planning (target date) and the month's Time Writing hours; the child work items always shown, headed "n will be created or checked", to tick them; and the PAT field only when no token is saved ("Use another token" shows it). The primary button reads Save. What is created and every rule of BKL-04 to BKL-15 stay unchanged. | T | S | UC-07 |
| BKL-17 | *(Retired — see BKL-33)* The New Feature form shall offer, for every parent Epic (with or without a Feature setup), an optional "Release Classification" drop-down: blank (not set), Major, Minor, MLD, Platform Update, Service Pack. On creation the Feature's Description shall carry the line "Release Classification : {value}" directly below "Feature DoD: …" and above the optional Description; left blank, the line is still written as "Release Classification :". It does not change which children are created. Completing the children of an existing Feature and the release view's Create Feature do not ask for it. | T | S | UC-07 |
| BKL-18 | *(Retired — see EPS-07)* When the Epic's Feature setup has title fields (CON-75), the New Feature form shall show one input per field under the title and build the title from the filled ones joined with " \| " (typing in the title takes over until a field is changed again). On creation the Description shall carry one line per field, "{Field} : {value}" — also for a blank one — below the Release Classification line; the setup's {Field} placeholders shall take the typed values. Opened from Release Details (Create Feature on a release or a DP row), the fields shall be pre-filled from the release's on-prem work items (per field the first item with its on-prem field filled), else from a title of as many " \| " parts. Completing children fills the placeholders from the Feature title's parts. When the setup the form uses (mine or the shared one) has no title fields but the other setup of the Epic has, the form shall say so and offer to switch to it. | T | S | UC-07 |
| BKL-19 | A Feature row on Backlogs and on My Tasks [Board] shall offer "＋ User Story", opening a minimal "New User Story" form: the title; "Under Feature" (the row's Feature, can be changed); one row with Assigned to (CON-50/51), a required current or future Sprint, Story Points (0–1,000, optional) and Priority (1–4, default 2); Description and Acceptance criteria; and, folded under "More fields", Risk (blank, 1 - High, 2 - Medium, 3 - Low) and Value area (Business, default, or Architectural). The story's Area Path shall always be the Feature's (re-read at Save) and its State New: neither shown nor editable. Save shall run as a background job (NAV-36), re-check the Sprint, re-read the Feature, create the story with these values only and read it back to verify them. Both transports shall accept these four fields only on a new User Story and only with these values. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-20 | A Feature, User Story or Task row on Backlogs and on My Tasks [Board] shall offer "Edit", opening a form that first reads the item fresh (with its revision) and then lets the user change: the title; Assigned to; State (New, Active, Resolved, Closed; Task: New, Active, Closed); Iteration (Feature: a PI of the current or future Sprints; User Story / Task: a current or future Sprint); Description and Acceptance criteria; on a Feature the Feature DoD (required) and Target date; on a User Story Story Points, Priority, Risk and Value area; on a Task Original Estimate and Completed Work. Locked and never written: Work Item Type, Parent and Area Path (always the parent's); the Time Writing story and a month Task keep their iteration; a month Task keeps the month in its title; a month Task's hours follow MON-09/MON-13/MON-15 (estimate locked after the planning lock or when tagged ad-hoc, Completed Work after the actuals lock, ad-hoc tag on late Completed Work without an estimate). Save shall send only the changed fields as a background job, with a test of the revision so a change made in Azure DevOps meanwhile is never overwritten, then read the item back and verify it. When a Feature moves to another PI, the form shall offer to move its open User Stories (not the Time Writing story) to a Sprint of that PI. The form shall show the Discussion (every page of comments up to 1,000, newest first, as text — a mention as "@Name", an image as "[image]", PIR-05) and add a comment (plain text). Both transports shall accept only these fields and the comment, and write to dev.azure.com only. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-21 | The QMS-0025 backlog (Backlogs) shall open filtered to the person browsing — the items assigned to them (by their work email, CON-82, or their name), every item above them for context and every item below them — shown as **Me** ticked in the **Assigned to** filter (BKL-46); choosing **All** or other people shows their items instead. The choice lasts for that visit of the app; a new start filters to the person browsing again. The State filter and search apply on top. |  T | M | UC-03 |

A month the person already has a Task for (under the Feature) says so: it is kept and gets only the hours typed. **Add n months** creates them one after another by the one Task rule (BKL-28: under the Feature, the person's own Time Writing story first, ad-hoc after that month's planning lock) and reports them; a failure keeps the Tasks already made. | T | M | UC-07 |

A month the chosen person **already has a Time Writing Task** for under the Feature shall be marked on its row — "There is already a Time Writing Task for {Month} (#id, its hours). The check runs whenever a Feature, User Story or Task is saved in the Edit form and whenever a month Task's hours are saved, re-reads the Feature and its children from Azure DevOps first, and changes each title at the revision just read. Where it is off, the action is not offered on that Epic's Features (Backlogs and My Tasks [Board]); single User Stories are still added with ＋ User Story. Where it is on (and BKL-10 allows it), the **Complete Feature children pop-up** shall list the Epic's User Stories **none ticked**, so the person decides which to add, and offer **Your own User Story** as in New Feature; the Time Writing story and Task follow BKL-28. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-39 | **Close Feature** (Time Writing → Close Features; Close on a Feature row of My Tasks [Board] and Sprint check) and **Close User Story** (Close on a User Story row there) shall open a **pop-up listing the items that would close**: the Feature with every open User Story and Task under it; for a User Story, the story alone — unless it is the **last open User Story** of its open Feature (Time Writing stories not counted), when the Feature, its Time Writing story and their open Tasks are listed too. Each row shows a **tick box**, its type (Time Writing story and Task named as such), the **#ID opening it in Azure DevOps**, the title, the owner and the state; the person's own items (and the item asked about) start ticked, other people's do not. A parent closes only with everything open below it: ticking an item ticks what is below it, unticking one unticks what is above it. When a month Time Writing Task is ticked, Close waits for **“I have updated the hours…”**. **Close n items** closes the ticked ones children first (New → Active → Closed), each read back; an item above a failure stays open; the result is reported. | BKL-40 | In the **Edit** form, when no PAT is saved for the server, the PAT field shall stay on screen while the person types in it, and "Reading #… from Azure DevOps…" shall show only while a read is running; the item is read when **Load** is pressed. | T | M | UC-04 |
| BKL-41 | Entering hours (Original Estimate or Completed Work above 0) on a month **Time Writing Task** — on Time Writing (Save, also for a new activity with typed hours) or in the Edit form — shall set the Task and its **Time Writing story** to **Active** when they are not: from New (or another state), and a **Closed** one is **reopened** to Active. The person is told which item changed and from which state; a Removed item stays Removed and is named. The Epic setup's default hours on a new Task do not count as entered, and in the Edit form a State chosen in the form wins. | T | M | UC-04, UC-06 |

Both open the Close pop-up (BKL-39) listing the Task, with the question whether its hours are entered; closed Tasks become read-only on Time Writing. Only the person's own Tasks are closed from Time Writing (choose "Me" first). | T | M | UC-04, UC-06 |
| BKL-43 | When a Feature closes **before its last Time Writing month** (Close Feature, or Close on its last open User Story), the Close pop-up shall also list the **later months' Time Writing Tasks** (months after the current month in UTC, NAV-51) of **everyone**, ticked, marked "→ Removed, 0 h": on Close each is first set to **0 h Original Estimate** and then to State **Removed**, before anything is closed. The editor is never assigned by default. | T | M | UC-04 |
| BKL-45 | The QMS-0025 board shall have **one** scroll bar: the board table fills the screen down to the footer, so the page itself does not scroll and the table scrolls (vertically and sideways, headers staying visible). It re-fits when the window or the content above it changes, and takes off the table any page overflow the measurement missed (fonts, display scaling). The table is never shorter than 120 px (about three rows); only a window too short for that scrolls the page as well. On a phone (700 px wide or less) the page scrolls instead and the table only sideways. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-46 | The QMS-0025 board shall filter by **State** and **Assigned to** with **multi-select drop-downs** (the shared MultiSelect: tick any number of values; nothing ticked = all). An item matching both is shown with the items above it for context; Assigned to also keeps the items below the people chosen (not below Unassigned items, so an unassigned Epic does not bring everything). **Reset filters** returns to the defaults and clears the search. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-47 | **Column Options** of the QMS-0025 board shall offer **Save view for everyone** (before v10.6 "Save for everyone") to a person with the right "Table, chart and page defaults" (held by the Tool Owner; Admin → People & roles → Role rights), with Git storage connected and no sample data: the chosen columns and order become **the default for everyone**, saved in the shared views defaults in Git (`personal-views-defaults.json`, read fresh first so its other defaults are kept). A person who has not saved their own order sees the team default; **Apply** keeps a person's own order in their browser, which wins; **Reset to the team default** goes back to it (Reset defaults when none is set). It shall be **ticked by default** (one Task per month up to the Target date, BKL-32) and **unticked by default on a DP release** (DPF-15).
