| BKL-50 | On **My Tasks [Board]** a Feature (and an item with items under it) shall open with a **+** and close with a **−**, in a small box, in place of the ▸ / ▾ arrows; a row with nothing under it shows no box (product owner, 26 Nov 2026: "For Expanding the Features - Give a Plus Symbol"). | T | S | UC-07 |

### 5.8 Read-only query views and Releases (QV) — UC-08

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QV-01 | A read-only view shall show its connection name, organization/project, item count (behind its ⓘ Info, QV-62) and fetch time, plus "Refresh from Azure" (or "Fetch from Azure") and "Connection settings". | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-02 | Before the first fetch, the view shall show ""{name}" has not been fetched yet." and state that nothing is written to Azure DevOps. | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-03 | The view's columns shall be the query's SELECT fields, in query order. Each entry shall be resolved against the project's field list, whether written as a reference name (any letter case, with or without square brackets) or as a field name such as Title or Assigned To; common field names shall resolve even when the field list cannot be read. Entries that are not fields of the project shall be named in a notice. When the saved data was fetched with other columns than the connection's current query, the view shall show the current query's columns and ask for a refresh. When the query selects only `System.Id`, or has no query, the columns shall be ID, Work Item Type, Title, State, Assigned To, Iteration Path. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-04 | The column headings shall use Azure DevOps field names when read (FET-19), else built-in names, else the reference name split into words. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-05 | Rows shall initially follow the query's ORDER BY, including DESC. Clicking a heading shall sort by that column, and clicking it again shall reverse the order. Empty values shall sort last. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-06 | The view shall show a summary strip with counts by State, by Work Item Type and by person. It shall also show the count of overdue items (open, target date before today) and of items changed in the last 7 days. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-07 | Clicking a State or Type chip shall filter the rows by that value, and clicking it again shall clear the filter. | D | S | UC-08 |
| QV-08 | The search box shall filter the rows to those where any shown column contains the text (case-insensitive). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-09 | "Export CSV" shall download the filtered rows with the shown columns. Values containing `"`, `,`, CR or LF shall be quoted. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-10 | The table shall show at most 500 rows, with a note when more match. | D | C | UC-08 |
| QV-11 | Read-only view items shall not count in Dashboard, Monthly Time Writing or My Tasks [Board]. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-12 | Every read-only Azure DevOps view shall be its own sidebar page with no tabs inside it. A release-source view shall show, on one page, the Releases list (QV-13) above the documents (QV-14). | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-13 | The Releases list shall be one flat list with no grouping by stream: one row per Name + Release, where a path segment "Release x.y.z" gives the release: when the Iteration Path has such a segment, the release shall come from it and the name from the Area Path (its last segment; the segment above a release segment if the Area Path has one; the segment above the release in the Iteration Path if the Area Path is the project alone); otherwise both shall come from the Area Path, the name being the segment above the release segment. Each row shall show the approval date, the document count, and "Tracked · {n}" or "Not tracked" depending on whether a Feature, User Story or Task in the Time Writing backlog has a title naming both the name and the exact release number. Each row shall also show Planned (h) and Booked (h): the sum of Original Estimate and of Completed Work, over all months, on the Time Writing Tasks (month Tasks, or Tasks under a Time Writing story) under the Features that track the release, plus any tracking Task itself; Removed Tasks are excluded, negative values count as 0, each Task is counted once, and a release with no such Task shows "—". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-14 | The documents shall be shown as a query view (QV-03 to QV-10). |  D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-15 | When any item of a read-only view has a release in its paths (QV-13), the view shall show the columns Name and Release after the ID. The QMS-0025 Time Writing backlog shall not use this. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-16 | Every read-only Azure DevOps view (not QMS-0025) shall show the header month picker and, by default, only the items whose month (UTC, NAV-51) is the selected month: the Author Approval Date (configured field, else any approval-date field), else the Changed Date. On a release-source view this filters both the Releases list and the documents; planned and booked hours stay all-months. The period control **Show: Selected month / All months** in the page's source bar (QV-62) shall turn the filter off, and a month with no items shall say so (the totals are in the view's ⓘ Info). | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-17 | Every read-only connection, release sources included, shall keep its own fetched snapshot (by connection ID), so each view shows only the results of its own query. A release snapshot saved before this (one shared snapshot) shall be shown only for the connection whose query it was fetched with, or when there is one release source, and shall be dropped at the next release-source fetch. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-18 | Dates of work items (Author Approval Date, Target Date, query date columns) are stored in UTC; they shall be shown as long dates in the viewer's time zone (e.g. "30 September 2026"), while the UTC date decides the month for the ADS month filter, the Releases list and the Dashboard counts (NAV-51). The header month shall start on the current UTC month (NAV-11). A filtered view shall say how many items have no approval date (their Changed Date is used). | T | M | UC-08 |
| OV-10 | Pulse Check (Data Insights; formerly the Dashboard's "Releases" tab) shall start with the summary: one line per read-only view whose documents carry releases in their paths, as "{view} [{count}] : {name} {release}, …" (releases with documents approved in the month, by Author Approval Date in UTC, NAV-51; "[0] : —" when none). Below it a 2×2 section: one quadrant per view with its release and document counts and the Title (from the query) of each document approved in the month, linked, with its release and approval date, and a last quadrant with releases and documents per view and in total. A separate query (e.g. Content) is its own view. | T | S | UC-01 |
| QV-19 | The Releases list shall show, for each release, the QMS-0025 Feature(s) tracking it as links (#ID title); "Not tracked" when none. A Feature tracks a release when its title, in any order and format and with any extra words (e.g. an Art), contains the release name as whole words and the exact version, optionally preceded by "v", "v.", "Rel" or "Release" (e.g. "Art-A, Dose Planning, v.1.1.0.1", "Dose Planning 1.1.0.1"); a version with other digits around it (1.1.0.10, 11.1.0.1) or another version (1.1.0.2) does not count. Versions that differ only by trailing zero parts are the same (3 = 3.0.0.0, 3.1 = 3.1.0.0, 3.1.1 = 3.1.1.0), both for this match and for grouping documents into releases. A Feature matching several releases counts for the one with the longest name. Stories and tasks do not attach releases to Features. Planned and booked hours come from the Time Writing Tasks under the tracking Features. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-20 | *(Retired — see QV-31)* A release that is not tracked shall offer "Create Feature": a QMS-0025 Feature with an editable title, pre-filled as "{view} \| {name} \| v{release}" (the mapping from the Release work item fields is still to be supplied); Create is allowed only while the title still names the release and its version (QV-19), under the Epic named like the view (or one chosen), assigned to the Time Writing creator email, validated by Azure DevOps before saving and verified after; after creation the release shows it as its Feature. Disabled with sample data or without the Time Writing PAT or creator email. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-21 | *(Retired — see QV-31)* The Create Feature title shall be built from three parts that match the Epic setup placeholders ({program} \| {service} \| v{version}): for DP, Program (default DP) · Service (the release name) · Release; for CP, Platform · Name (the release name) · Release, the platform chosen from a list starting with Platform 1.0 and Platform 2.0 and extended with "Add…" (new entries kept in this browser); for other streams, Stream · Name · Release. Changing a part rebuilds the title; the whole title shall stay editable and must still map back to the release. | D | S | UC-02 |
| QV-22 | Release Details → DP shall show only the DP connection's own query as a table: exactly the query's columns in query order (no Name / Release read from the paths), with the query view's summary, search, sorting, month filter and CSV export (QV-03…QV-10, QV-16) — no Releases list, no QMS-0025 Feature match, no Create Feature, no Pedestal or DHFI data. "Columns (n of m)" shall open a chooser listing every column of the query (name and reference name) to tick the ones shown, with "Show all" and "Only the first"; at least one column stays shown, the choice is remembered per view in this browser, and the CSV holds the shown columns. CP, IOCC, Content and Tool Validations are unchanged. The DP Feature matching and DHFI linking are in the backlog (OI-14). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-23 | A query column written as a bare field name ("[SourceProgram]") whose field has a prefixed reference name ("Custom.SourceProgram") shall show that field's values: when the name is neither a reference name nor a display name of the project, the one field whose reference name ends with ".{name}" is used, both when resolving the query's columns and when keeping the fetched values (also when the field list cannot be read). With several such fields the column stays as written and is not guessed; a written prefix is never replaced. Data fetched before this needs one "Refresh from Azure". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-24 | Release Details → DP shall filter its query's rows by **DDT Date** (in UTC, NAV-51) for the header month — "Month filter: by DDT Date in UTC" (in the view's ⓘ Info, QV-28), rows without a DDT Date only with "Show: All months" — and show the counts of the month above the tabs (QV-62: Releases in the month, Completed, Supported): **Completed Releases** = the releases whose DDTStatus is Done with the DDT Date in the month; **Supported Releases** = the **unique releases** with at least one milestone Done with its own date in the month — DDT (DDTDate / DDTStatus), DRfH, DRPC or DRPE — each release counted once however many of its milestones fell in the month; a release is its PsName + Release (two rows naming the same release count once), else its work item. The Supported hint shows how many milestones were Done and the split per milestone; folded under the counts, "Supported releases counted" lists each release once with its milestones and their dates, the latest date, ID and Title. "Done" is matched ignoring case and spaces; the fields may carry a prefix (Custom.DDTDate). With no DDT Date in the month the counts stay above "No release has its DDT Date in …"; the counting rules are behind ⓘ "What was counted". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-25 | Pulse Check and the Dashboard shall count DP from the DP connection's query in the same way: its releases are the Completed Releases and, in place of documents, the Supported Releases ("n completed" / "n supported" in the stream quadrant, the comparison with the previous month and the totals table; "· n supported" per month and in total in the Dashboard's delivery table), each supported release listed once as its titles with the milestones it passed by their team names ("SFV · DT Done", QV-26); the Dashboard's total over a period counts a release supported in several months once. Other streams keep releases and documents. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-26 | The DP milestones shall be shown by the DP team's names while the query fields keep theirs: DT = Design Transfer (DDT), SFV = Start of Verification / Start of Hardening (DRfH), PRD = PRD Milestone (DRPC), RPE = Release Planning Event (DRPE) — in the Supported Releases split ("DT n · SFV n · PRD n · RPE n"), the "Supported releases counted" table (short name with the long name), a legend in the view's ⓘ Info (QV-28), the Pulse Check / Dashboard titles ("DT Done") and the info texts. The counts depend only on the field names (…Date / …Status, prefix allowed), so a changed DP query keeps working as long as it returns these fields; the app adds no project condition, so every row the query returns — from any project of the collection — is shown and counted. | T | S | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-27 | A query view cell whose value is HTML (rich-text fields such as Acceptance Criteria or Description) shall show its text only: tags removed, entities decoded (&amp;nbsp;, &amp;amp;, &amp;lt;, numeric), each paragraph, list item or line break joined with " · "; the full text also shows on hover, the CSV export holds the same text, and search matches it. Values that are not HTML (e.g. "a < b") stay as they are. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-28 | A Release Details / on-prem view shall keep its top short: information about the view — the month filter note (DDT Date for DP, Author Approval Date otherwise, with the count of undated rows), the DP milestone legend, "loaded the data saved to Git…" and the Git save note ("Saved to Git (…)") — sits behind one ⓘ Info next to the view's name, not as banners. Only errors (fetch or Git save failed) and warnings (data fetched with other columns) stay as banners. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-29 | The DP table shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column: for each row, the Feature and the User Story on the Time Writing board (QMS-0025 items in this browser; removed items and Time Writing stories left out) whose title matches the row's **SourceProgram + Title** — or the Title alone, since not every board title carries the SourceProgram — by fuzzy match at the match threshold (80% by default). The Title holds the name and the version and **both must match** (the NAV-26 score: name 60%, version 40% — the exact version full, the same major.minor a quarter, the same major a tenth); the version is the one in the Title, else the Release field, so a board title without the version or with another one stays below 80%. The search also **combines PsName and Release** (with and without the SourceProgram) and tries both the Title's version and the Release field's, and a board title equal to the one Create Feature gives the row (QV-31; spaces, case and punctuation ignored) is a 100% match, so a Feature created from the row is always found even when the Title differs from the PsName. Shown as "Feature id n%" / "Story id n%" linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold ("closest Feature id · n%") or "no match" / "board not loaded", and **Create Feature** (QV-31). The column is searched and exported to CSV ("Feature 10 (92%); User Story 11 (85%)" or "No match (closest …)"). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-30 | The DP view's columns shall be rearrangeable: drag a column heading onto another to put it in that place, or in **Columns** drag a column in the list or use ← → to move it one step; the QMS-0025 match column moves and hides like the query's columns. The order is remembered per view in this browser, survives a refresh (a column added to the query later goes right after the column it follows in the query) and is used by the CSV export; **Query order** goes back to the query's order. Sorting by a click on a heading is unchanged. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-31 | Create Feature from Release Details — a DP row without a QMS-0025 match (QV-29) or an untracked release of CP, IOCC, Content or Tool Validations — shall always open the one default Create Feature form ("New Feature", as on the QMS-0025 board and My Tasks [Board]: Parent Epic, Feature title, Area Path, PI / Sprint, Feature DoD, User Stories and the Time Writing task from the Epic's setup, assignee, PAT), never a separate form. It starts with the title "Program \| Service \| Release" — DP: Program = SourceProgram (else DP), Service = **PsName**, Release = **Release** (each falling back to the Title and its version); CP: the release name \| Release (QV-45); other streams: the stream \| the release name \| Release — and the Epic named like the stream (else the first Epic); both stay editable. When it cannot open, the button is disabled and says why (sample data; no Time Writing connection; no Epic loaded). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-32 | Pulse Check shall show a **Supported Releases** section under the release quadrants, on both the previous-month and the current-month release tabs, **folded by default**: its title and count are shown, and **Show list** opens the list (**Hide list** folds it again; the choice is remembered per viewer in the browser). Opened, for the DP stream it lists each supported release of that month once (QV-24) with its name and release, the milestones it passed in the month (DT, SFV, PRD, RPE, each with its date), "Completed (DT)" when DT is among them, the latest date, a link to the work item and its Title; the count reads "n releases · m milestones" (also while folded); opened with none, "No release passed a milestone in …", or "The DP view has not been fetched yet". | T | M | UC-09 |
| QV-33 | Pulse Check's Releases tab shall show, next to the Releases summary ("Stream [n] : release, release, …"), a **Supported** summary in the same format: per stream the supported releases of the month — DP: its Supported Releases (QV-32); S&S IT: the unique releases of its Windchill export with a document dated in the month (CON-73), "name number" — with the total "{Month YYYY} · n supported releases". It shall be shown also when no Azure DevOps Server view is set up, as long as a stream has supported releases. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-34 | **Tool Validations** shall be counted per **tool**, not per document: a validation has several documents (plan, protocol, report, …) for the same tool, so Pulse Check's Releases line, the Supported summary (QV-33), the quadrant and the Dashboard shall list each tool once for the month, with its document count. Every line follows the page's month filter: the Releases tab shows the month before the header month and the Current month releases tab the header month, for the Releases and the Supported summaries alike. The tool is the Area Path's deepest segment below the project that is neither a release segment nor the stream's own folder ("Tool Validations", "Validation"); without one, the Title before its version, else the Title before its first document word (Validation, Plan, Protocol, Report, Summary, Record, Checklist, IQ, OQ, PQ, Test, Assessment); the version is the Iteration Path's "Release x" or the Title's version when there is one. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-35 | The Tool Validations view (Release Details) shall show its query table without the summary strip — no State or Type filter chips and no People row; search, sort, column widths and CSV export stay. | D | S | UC-08 |
| QV-36 | **IOCC** shall be counted like DP, each release once per month: a **Completed Release** is one whose **Release Notes** or **Verification Report** is signed (Author Approval Date) in the month, and a **Supported Release** one whose **Release Plan**, Release Notes or Verification Report is signed in the month (so, as for DP, the Completed Releases are also Supported). The document type is read from the Title; a release is its Type of Release (Area Path after IOCC), its Title without the version and the document words, and its version, so that the plan, notes and report of one release count once. Pulse Check's Releases line, quadrant (completed / supported), Supported summary and the Dashboard delivery table shall use these counts; unsigned documents and other document types are not counted. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-37 | Release Details → **IOCC** shall group its releases hierarchically: "Group by" (default **Type of Release**), then "then by" for each further level, among Type of Release, Release name and Release (up to three levels, each column once). Each group row shall show its level, value ("(blank)" last) and release count, indented by its level, and collapse on its own; "Collapse all" / "Expand all" shall act on every level, each disabled when it has nothing to do; "No grouping" shows the flat list. A release's name shall leave out the document words (Release Notes, Verification Report, Release Plan, …) so that a release's documents are one release (QV-36). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-38 | Every table with draggable column widths (the query views, the release tables incl. IOCC and the Windchill releases) shall be as wide as its columns — no fixed overall width — so that widening one column widens the table (scrolling sideways) instead of narrowing the others; columns without a dragged width shall fit their content. A **Wrap text** option on each such table (kept per table in this browser, off by default) shall let long cell text wrap so that the table fits the page; off, every cell stays on one line. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-39 | A Windchill export page's **Releases** table (S&S IT, and any other export shown) shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column, searched as on the DP table (QV-29): the release name — and each part of a clubbed release — with and without the stream name, against the release number(s), name 60% and version 40% at the match threshold (80% by default); a board title equal to the Create Feature title is 100%. It shows "Feature id n%" / "Story id n%" linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold (or "no match" / "board not loaded") and **Create Feature**, which opens the default Create Feature form (QV-31) titled "{stream} \| {release name} \| v{release}" (a clubbed release: "{stream} \| {its name as written}") under the Epic named like the stream (else the first Epic). The column is sortable and in the releases CSV. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-40 | *(Retired — see QV-41…QV-45)* **CP** releases shall be read from each asset's **Title**, written "[Release name] [Release No] [Asset name]" with any delimiters: the release name is the Title before its first version and the release number is that version — a dotted number (3.1, 3.1.0), optionally written v3.1, R3.2 or Rel 4.0, or a whole number written with v, R or Rel (v5); the asset name after it is dropped, so all assets of a release are **one release** (name and number, case and trailing zeros ignored). The other naming, "[Asset name] [Release No] ([Release Name])", shall be read too: when the Title ends with a release name in brackets right after the number (e.g. "Test Report 3.1 (Care Pathways)", "SRS - v3.2 (CareLink)", "Labeling 5 (Eclipse)"), the release name is the bracketed text and the number the one before it; brackets elsewhere keep the first naming. Assets of one release in either naming are one release. A Title without a version falls back to the general path rule. Release Details → CP shall list these releases with their asset count, the QMS-0025 Feature found by **fuzzy match** (name 60%, version 40%) at the match threshold, its planned and booked hours, and — without a Feature — the closest one and **Create Feature** in the default form (QV-31) titled with the **release name and number only**, "release name \| vRelease" (e.g. "Care Pathways \| v3.2", no stream in front), under the Epic named CP. Pulse Check shall count CP's releases of the month from the same rule, and its **Supported** summary shall list CP's unique releases with an asset in the month ("CP [n] : release, …"). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-41 | **CP** shall be reported from its **release-folder tree** (the connection named CP — or starting with CP — whose query is a tree / links query, else the first CP connection; it is listed under Release Details → CP even when its Show in menu is off; with the CP_Release_Folder tree query: Release containers, folders and documents, with the approver slots): a release is a **Release container**, and every Artifact or Document belongs to its nearest Release ancestor (Parent → ID). Release containers are never grouped by codename, version, asset or title, so twelve asset folders under one release are one release. A document whose Parent is not in the data set, a Parent loop, or a document without a Release above it is listed as a data issue with its IDs and not counted. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-42 | A **qualifying QA/QE signature** shall be an approver slot of an Artifact or Document — ApproverNameN, ApproverFunctionN and ApproverApprovalDateN of the same slot number, slots found in the data (1–20 today, more without a change) — with a name, a valid date and a role on the QA/QE list (QAQE for Software, Quality Engineer, QA, QE, QA/QE, QE Software; trimmed, case ignored, no substring match; built in). The document's State and Author Approval Date never filter (a QA/QE signature on an InReview document counts). A dated slot without a name or role is a data issue; a dated QA/QE approval on another work-item type (a Folder) is an exception; an Artifact Review is not counted again. The signature's month is its date in the viewer's time zone. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-43 | For the header month, CP shall count **Supported** = the Release containers with at least one qualifying QA/QE signature in the month on a document anywhere under them, and **Completed** = those whose **main Release Notes** (Release container → Reports or Report folder → a document of Type Release Notes) has a qualifying QA/QE signature in the month; an asset's Release Notes does not complete the release. Each release counts once per month however many documents, signatures or approvers it has; completed releases are part of the supported ones (never added). A main Release Notes also signed in another month is flagged for review; several main Release Notes in one release are a data issue. **Shared containers** (built in: 614437 Clinical Platform Global) are reported apart, not in the counts. Completion history across months is not kept (KNOWN-ISSUES LIM-12). Pulse Check and the Dashboard count CP this way (Completed as releases, Supported in the Supported summary). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-44 | CP release **names** shall come from an optional second CP connection (the CP_Releases data: Release and Content — the other CP connection whose rows carry Content, else one with a Release field; its name and record counts are shown behind ⓘ, and a names connection without any Content is a data issue): the release version is read from the container title (a leading version after an optional "Release", else one right after "Clinical Platform"; never a version in brackets), matched to the Release field (as written, else the first version in it, e.g. "v26.2.0.0 (asset A)") with omitted trailing segments as zero (26.2 = 26.2.0.0, never 26.2.0.1), and the distinct non-blank Content values give the name: one → "Name Version" (blank rows warned), several → conflicting names, none → no name; without the data set, a capitalised word after the version in a "Release <version> <Name> … Clinical Platform" title is a candidate pending review. Names never change the counts and their status is shown. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-45 | Release Details → **CP** shall show, for the header month: the counts Releases in the month, Completed and Supported (QV-62); a **Releases** table of the supported releases with "n releases · m QA/QE-signed documents" (Release name with its name status and warnings, Release = the version, Release Notes signed, Documents = the QA/QE-signed documents, Container ID linking to Azure DevOps, Completed, the matched Feature's **Time Writing Feature**, **Planned (h)** and **Booked (h)** (QV-65), and the **QMS-0025 match** with **Create Feature** titled "release name \| vVersion"), each row opening its signature evidence (document, Type, State, approver, role, slot, date, folder path); the shared activity apart (Container ▸ opening its signature evidence, Why apart, Signed documents right-aligned); the data issues; and **Export evidence CSV**. The whole tree is read (no month filter on the items); the query's rows stay below. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-46 | The release table of every other Release Details view — **IOCC**, **Content** and **Tool Validations** — shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column, as on the DP table (QV-29), the Windchill releases (QV-39) and CP (QV-45), so that every stream offers Create Feature the same way: per release, the Feature and the User Story on the QMS-0025 board matched by the same search (the release name — for IOCC also its Type of Release with the name — with its release number, the stream as the program; name 60% and version 40% at the match threshold, 80% by default; a board title equal to the Create Feature title is 100%), linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold (or "no match" / "board not loaded") and **Create Feature**, which opens the default Create Feature form (QV-31) titled "{stream} \| {release name} \| v{release}". The "Time Writing Feature" column keeps its match and the planned and booked hours, without a Create Feature button of its own. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-47 | Every Release Details page with a consolidated result shall show it and the raw data as **two tabs** of the page instead of the raw rows at the bottom: every stream's page — DP, CP, IOCC, Content and each Windchill export or search (S&S IT, ECP) — shows **Releases \| Source data (n)** (QV-62; Tool Validations has its own tabs instead — no Releases table, the query rows on its **Tool List [TFS]** and **Validation Documents [TFS]** tabs, TVL-14): the Releases table (DP: the release list with its QMS-0025 match; CP: the month's releases with their evidence; Windchill: the consolidation, ECP's projects included) \| the query's rows or the imported rows with their search and Export. The consolidated tab opens first; the raw tab shows its row count; the choice is kept per page for the browser tab. A view with no consolidation (an own query) keeps its single table. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-48 | The actions of a Release Details page shall take little room: the main action as a **small icon button** with its name as tooltip — **↻** Refresh from Azure (Fetch on a page not fetched yet; the progress shown beside it), **↻** Refresh from Windchill, **↻** Import newer export — and the others in a **⋯ menu** (Load from Git, Connection settings, Remove this export… — Delete this export… on an export under Other views, CON-118), closed by a choice, a click outside or Escape; the period control **Show: Selected month / All months** stays beside them (on CP too since QV-72: All months lists every release container signed in any month; its counts stay the header month's). | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-49 | A CP release container titled in the **Platform** pattern "PF1.0 \<Name\>-\<Number\>" or "PF2.0 \<Name\>-\<Number\>" shall take its name — with the variant, e.g. "PF2.0 Carpo" — and its number straight from the title: the number is the part after the **last hyphen** and may hold letters ("A12"); the name may contain spaces and hyphens. Such a release needs no CP_Releases lookup; its Create Feature title is "PF2.0 Carpo \| A12". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-50 | Pulse Check shall offer a **Period** — Month (the monthly tabs as before), **Quarter**, **Half-year** or **Year** — in calendar terms (Q1 = January–March, H1 = January–June, the year January–December), with a choice of the latest eight periods up to the one holding the month before the header month. For a quarter, half-year or year it shall show, per stream, the releases of all its months — each release once however many months it appears in, DP's Supported Releases once each, documents added up, the Windchill streams' releases by their dates — against the previous period of the same length (Q1 against Q4 of the year before), with the change, the release list per stream and a total; and the Features and User Stories opened and closed in the period per stream. A period still running says how many of its months have passed. | T | M | UC-26 |
| QV-51 | Pulse Check shall open without recalculating what has not changed: the CP release tree, the release names, each Windchill export's releases and each month's stream releases shall be worked out once per fetched or loaded data set and reused for every month, period and visit; a fetch, import or load from Git recalculates. Reading a release tree of 4,000 documents shall take well under a second. | T | M | UC-26 |
| QV-52 | *(Release names by the patterns of QV-55 first.)* **Content** shall name its releases by the document **Title**, not by a release name and number read from the paths: every document with a Title is a release, one per unique Title (spaces tidied), with its documents and approval date. This applies on the Content Release Details page (the Releases table shows the Title in its **Release name** column and no Release column, QV-62; the query output has no Name / Release columns added), in Pulse Check (Releases summary, the Content quadrant, period totals and comparisons), in the Monthly pack lines fed by Content and wherever the stream's releases are counted. A Feature whose title holds the Title tracks it (Time Writing Feature and its hours). | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-53 | The **DP** table (Release Details → DP) shall show, after the query's columns, **Month & Year** — the month of the release's DDT Date in UTC (NAV-51; e.g. "Oct 2026"), which the list filters on, or "No DDT Date" (never a Completed Release; listed by its planned milestones per QV-54, else only with *All months*) — and **Milestones** — for DT, SFV, PRD and RPE the month of its date (or "no date") and its status (or "(no status)"), with ✓ when it is Done with a date and so counts as Supported in that month (DT ✓ also as Completed). Both are searched and exported with the table, so a release missing from a month or a count can be traced to its dates and statuses. | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-54 | A DP release **without a DDT Date** shall be listed in the DP table in each month in which one of its milestones (SFV, PRD, RPE) has its date with the status **Planned** or **ToBePlanned** (case and spaces ignored), its Month & Year reading e.g. "Oct 2026 (SFV Planned) · planned, not counted"; it shall **not count** as a Supported or Completed Release (those need a milestone Done with its date in the month). A release with a DDT Date stays listed by the DDT Date's month only; one with no DDT Date and no planned milestone date is listed only with *All months*. | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-55 | Release names shall follow **naming patterns** per stream, edited in Settings → Azure DevOps → Stream mapping → **Release naming patterns** (roles with Manage shared settings; applied to the shared release-source settings and saved to Git with the planning data, GIT-14; audited): each pattern is matched against a document's Title ignoring case — `*` any text, `{…}` the release name (it may hold `*` and text), spaces match any spaces — with an optional label as its group; the first that fits names the release, the rest of the Title being the document; a Title none fits keeps the stream's own rule. Built in: **Content** `{*_RELEASE}*` (the name is the Title up to and including _RELEASE, e.g. "ABC_Labeling_RELEASE_IFU_v2" → release "ABC_Labeling_RELEASE", document "IFU_v2"; a Title without _RELEASE stays a release of its own, QV-52); **IOCC** `*Conformance Verification {*}` (label Conformance Verification) and `*Test Tools {*}` (label Test Tools), other IOCC documents keep the IOCC rule. The documents of a release are grouped under it on the release page (one row per release, its documents under it), and Pulse Check, its counts and the Monthly pack use the same names. A pattern is checked as typed (one {…}, at most 200 characters, at most 10 per stream), "Try a Title" shows the release, group and document it gives, and Reset to built-in removes the saved ones. | T | S | UC-26 |

### 5.9 Dashboard (OVR) — UC-09, UC-10

OVR-01 to OVR-08 describe the earlier Overview (Team / Individual switch, "This month" and "Trends" tabs). They are **retired**: the Dashboard rebuild replaced them with OV-11 to OV-14 (§5.9), and the month capacity view moved to Pulse Check → Last month. OVR-09 still applies (Dashboard → Streams). The rows are kept for traceability.

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OVR-01 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* The Dashboard shall provide, in the page header, a Team/Individual switch labelled "Dashboard for", defaulting to Team. The choice shall be remembered in localStorage `demand-planning-overview-view-v1`. | D | M | UC-09 |
| OVR-02 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* In Individual mode the Dashboard shall use only the chosen person's Tasks, while still applying regional holidays by region. With no person chosen it shall show "Choose whose overview to show." | T | M | UC-09 |
| OVR-03 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* The Dashboard shall show the metrics: utilization (team, or "{person} · utilization"), "Overloaded", "Has capacity" and "Absence". | D | M | UC-09 |
| OVR-04 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* The Dashboard shall provide the tabs "This month · {Month YYYY}" and "Trends", remembering the selection in localStorage `demand-planning-overview-tab-v1`. | D | M | UC-09, UC-10 |
| OVR-05 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* "This month" shall show: "Who has capacity" (person, status, available, planned, booked, utilization, main stream); "Streams of work"; "Hours by Epic"; the "Funded hours" and "Absence" donuts; "Hours by person and stream" (top 8 streams, the rest as Other); and a collapsed "How utilization is calculated". | D | M | UC-09 |
| OVR-06 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* "Trends" shall offer "6 months" or "12 months". It shall show "Hours by month", "Utilization by month", "Utilization by person and month", "Epic mix by month", and Pareto charts for "Overrun hours" and "Ad-hoc hours", each grouped by Feature or Epic. | D | M | UC-10 |
| OVR-07 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* Clicking a month in a Trends chart shall set the selected month. | D | S | UC-10 |
| OVR-08 | *(Retired — see OV-11…OV-14.)* An Epic's colour shall not change when switching between 6 and 12 months. | T | S | UC-10 |
| OVR-09 | Overrun hours shall be Completed Work above Original Estimate on Tasks that have an estimate. Ad-hoc hours shall be Completed Work on Tasks tagged `ad-hoc`. | T | M | UC-10 |

### 5.10 Capacity model (CAP) — UC-09, UC-14

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-01 | Working days of a month shall be its Monday–Friday dates. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-02 | Funded (contractual) hours per person shall be working days × **Working hours per day** (Settings → Capacity & periods → Capacity and absence; default 8). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-03 | Absence shall be the Completed Work on Month Tasks under the Absence Epic, split into PTO, Sick Leave, Unplanned Leave and public holidays by Feature and Task titles. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-04 | A public holiday booked under a Feature titled "Public Holidays - {India\|Netherlands\|US}" under the Absence Epic shall count as absence for every person mapped to that region, unless the Feature (or an Epic above it) is mapped under CAP-10, which then decides. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-05 | *(Retired — see CAP-38)* Available hours shall be funded − absence − reserved time (common activities, CAP-11). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-06 | *(Retired — see CAP-38)* Utilization shall be booked hours (Completed Work on Month Tasks outside the Absence Epic) ÷ available × 100. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-07 | Status shall be: "No bookings" when utilization is undefined; "Overloaded" when strictly above (>) the overloaded threshold (default 100%; exactly at it is not overloaded — owner decision 9); "Has capacity" when < the capacity threshold (default 80%); otherwise "Balanced". | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-08 | Streams shall be the Epics above the booked Month Tasks, excluding the Absence Epic. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-09 | People shall be the assignees of Month Tasks, excluding "Unassigned". | T | M | UC-09, UC-14 |
| CAP-10 | Settings → Capacity shall offer "Holidays and common activities": map an Epic or Feature by ID (also one not in the fetched data) as Holidays (absence) or Common activity (reserved time), for Everyone or Everyone in a region (India, Netherlands, US), with an optional name. Each mapping shall list, for the header month, the hours per person, the number of Tasks and of people in scope; name-based holiday Features found automatically shall be listed too. Mappings shall be saved with the capacity settings, audited, kept when the capacity numbers are saved, and shared through the Git planning file (GIT-03) so everyone who loads it gets them. | T | S | UC-14 |
| CAP-11 | Every Month Task under a mapped Epic or Feature shall apply to each person in its scope (people in the month mapped to that region in Settings → Resources, or everyone) instead of to its assignee: Holidays as absence (Public holiday), lowering available capacity; Common activities as reserved time, which counts as utilized hours (CAP-38), not as project work. Hours per Task: Original Estimate, else Completed Work, else one working day. The nearest mapped item above a Task decides. | T | S | UC-09, UC-14 |
| CAP-12 | Region and person views shall keep shared-time Tasks whose scope is everyone or the chosen region; the month capacity view shall show common activities per person, in the Absence metric and as their own part of the funded-hours ring; Dashboard definitions and heatmap tooltips shall name them. | D | S | UC-09 |
| CAP-13 | **NPRA** (non-project related activities: internal meetings such as stand-up, special projects and town hall; trainings such as white belt, AI and Cornerstone) shall be the Time Writing Tasks that carry, themselves or on a Story / Feature / Epic above them, a tag starting with "NPRA" (e.g. "NPRA", "NPRA Hours"; the absence Epic and mapped shared time take precedence). Their hours are not project work: they count in no stream or plan line. They are split into **Trainings** and **Other NPRA** (CAP-38), are not deducted from the available hours and count as utilized hours. They are tracked against an **NPRA maximum** per person per month (Settings → Capacity, default 20 h); hours over it are flagged; 0 tracks without a maximum. The month view shall show an NPRA metric (booked hours, the maximum, how many people are over it), per person "NPRA booked / maximum" with the hours over the maximum flagged, and an NPRA table (booked, planned, maximum, left, status). Unplanned sick leave is not NPRA: it is booked under the absence Epic by the end of the month at the latest and deducted from the available hours (CAP-02); the view says so. | T | M | UC-09, UC-14 |
| CAP-14 | Settings → Resources shall take a **Time tracking started** date (formerly *Joined on*: the date time tracking started for the person, not their real joining date — owner decision 5) and a **Left on** date per person (both days worked; a leaving date before the joining date is dropped), saved with the resource profiles. A person's funded hours in a month shall be the working days (Monday–Friday) of the month within those dates × hours per day; in a month with no such day the person shall have no capacity, not be counted in the head-count or the totals, and the hours booked on their month Tasks shall be listed to be checked. The month capacity view shall name the people counted for part of the month. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-15 | Settings → Resources shall have a **Default Time tracking started** (formerly Default Joined on) (1 May 2026 unless changed; it can be changed or cleared — in People's ⋯ menu, CAP-61), saved with the capacity settings and kept when the other capacity settings are saved. It shall apply as the Joined on (CAP-14) of everyone without their own date, so months before it give them no capacity. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-16 | The Epic configured as the absence Epic (Settings → Capacity & periods, same ID) is the **Team Holidays and Training** Epic: under it, a month Task that is training (CAP-32) shall count as **NPRA** (Trainings, CAP-38) — utilized hours, not deducted, against the NPRA maximum (CAP-13), not project work and not absence — and everything else under it stays **absence** (PTO, Sick Leave, Unplanned Leave and the holidays mapped as shared time). The Epic's own title is not read for this. Leave and training under the Epic are offered in Bulk actions' Leave and training list (ATM-24, ATM-27), not as missing work. | T | M | UC-05, UC-06 |
| CAP-29 | Settings → People & access shall list the people from Azure DevOps (CAP-51): the members of the Time Writing connection's team and everyone Assigned To fetched work (with Time Writing or, as "no Time Writing yet", without); people with settings who are not in Azure DevOps (historical hours, added by name before v7.0.0) are kept with their settings and shown with the Status **Not in Azure DevOps** (CAP-58; before, a separate "Not in Azure DevOps" list under the table; before v7.0.0 everyone with a profile was in the main list). | T | S | UC-14 |
| CAP-30 | The members of the Time Writing connection's team shall be read from Azure DevOps (read only, names and emails; groups left out) **automatically** when People & access opens and after every refresh of the Time Writing data, and again with **↻ Read the team again**; also once at start for the co-owner lists (PGB-21, quietly); the PAT needs Project and Team (Read), and a refusal is shown with that hint (v7.0.0; before, an "Add team members from Azure DevOps" button stored them). | T | S | UC-14 |
| CAP-31 | *(Retired — see CAP-51: people are no longer added by name; they come from Azure DevOps.)* Add a person by name shall list anyone not found any other way; people added by name or from the team can be removed from the list again. | T | S | UC-14 |
| CAP-32 | A month Task shall be **training** when it or its User Story has "training" in its title (each person's "<Name> \| Training \| Time Writing" story) or carries a **Training** or **NPRA** tag; the Feature's and Epic's titles and tags under the absence Epic are not read, because leave and training share one Feature per person. A Training tag counts as NPRA anywhere, like an NPRA tag (CAP-13). | T | M | UC-05, UC-09 |
| CAP-33 | Settings → Resources shall offer **Remove** on every person (after confirmation): the person is hidden from the list and left out of **every capacity figure** — capacity, utilization, team totals, Pulse Check, Team overview, My dashboard — their funded and booked hours included, on every page; Pulse Check names them as "Removed in Settings → Resources, not counted". They are listed with the Status **Not part of the team** (People's Status filter, CAP-58), and **Restore** in their drawer brings them back (CAP-59; before, a separate "Removed people (n)" list). Saved in the person's profile in Git (`removed: true`), so it applies to everyone. Since v6.0.0 Remove first asks whether the person left the team or is not part of it (CAP-48). | T | M | UC-14 |
| CAP-34 | Admin → Capacity & time → **Capacity and absence** (moved there from People, CAP-61) shall show **who counts in the month's capacity**: "Capacity in <Month>: n people, X h available = C h contractual − A h absence" (CAP-38), with an Info listing each counted person and their available hours, and a column **Available <month> (h)** ("not counted" for people without a month Time Writing Task, not employed that month or removed); Unassigned Tasks are named as not in the total. The Team overview Utilization tile and its Capacity and workload analysis (Workload by person) shall show the number of people and their total capacity. | T | M | UC-14 |
| CAP-35 | Team overview shall have an **Old Calculation** tab: for one chosen month (a month of the period, the header month by default) one row per person with **Net Capacity** = working days of the month the person is employed × funded hours per day (Settings → Capacity); **Planned Hours** = every planned hour (Original Estimate) — work, absence, holidays, training, NPRA and the shared time that applies to the person; **Planned Utilisation** = Planned ÷ Net Capacity; **Available Hours** = Net Capacity − Planned (negative when planned above capacity); **Status** from the Planned Utilisation in the Settings bands (Overloaded / Balanced / Has capacity / No bookings); **Actual Hours** = every hour recorded (Completed Work), absence, holidays and NPRA included; **Actual Utilisation** = Actual ÷ Net Capacity; and a Total row. Region, role and team member filters apply; removed people (CAP-33) are not counted. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-36 | The Old Calculation (CAP-35) shall deduct **Standard Hours** (32 h by default) from every person's Net Capacity: Net Capacity = working days employed × funded hours per day − Standard Hours, never below 0. The tab shall show the Standard Hours and their **breakup** (lines of label and hours, 0–744 h each, at most 20); roles that manage shared settings can edit the breakup there (Edit Standard Hours: add, change or remove lines; Save), and the total is the sum of the lines. One set applies to everyone and every month and is kept with the capacity settings in the planning data (shared through Git); each change is recorded in the audit log. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-37 | The Old Calculation (CAP-35) shall offer a tick box per shared-time group — **All** (everyone) and **All-<Region>** (All-India, All-Netherlands, All-US): the holidays and common activities shared by everyone or a region (imported history rows for a group and Settings → Capacity rules), each with its hours of the month. A ticked group is counted in every person's Planned and Actual Hours; an unticked group is left out of both. All are ticked by default; roles that manage shared settings change them, kept with the capacity settings in Git with an audit entry. Other pages keep counting shared time as before. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-38 | **Utilization** shall be, per person and month, **Utilized hours ÷ (Contractual hours − Absence hours) × 100**, on every page that shows utilization or capacity (Team overview, Pulse Check, Settings → Resources, My dashboard, Time Writing, Monthly pack): **Contractual hours** = working days employed × hours per day (CAP-02, CAP-14); **Absence hours** = PTO + Sick Leave + Unplanned Leave + Public holidays (incl. shared holidays, CAP-11) + Other absence (Completed Work); available = contractual − absence, never below 0; **Utilized hours** = Work + **Trainings** + **Other NPRA** + **Common activities** — recorded (Completed Work; common activities spread by CAP-11 count their Completed Work) for Utilization, planned (Original Estimate; common activities their CAP-11 hours) for Planned utilization. Trainings are NPRA that is training (CAP-32, or a Training tag on the Task or above it); the rest is Other NPRA. Status (CAP-07) follows this Utilization. The month view shall show per person Contractual hours, Absence hours, Available hours, Project work, Training, Other NPRA, Common activities, Total planned, Total actual and Actual utilization (names per CAP-45); team figures add up the people. The Old Calculation (CAP-35) is unchanged. | T | M | UC-09, UC-14 |
| CAP-39 | Team overview shall show the CAP-38 split: (a) **Capacity and workload → Workload by person** shall list per person over the period Contractual hours, Absence hours, Available hours, Project work, Training, Other NPRA, Common activities, Total planned, Total actual, Planned and Actual utilization and Actual − plan (with the stream filter, only that stream's work counts: no trainings, other NPRA or common activities); (b) a **Utilization** tab shall show, for one chosen month of the period (the header month by default), the same per-person table as Pulse Check → Last month (CAP-38) and the team figure "Σ utilized ÷ Σ available" (and planned), for the people left by the region, role and team member filters. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CAP-40 | In the Old Calculation (CAP-35), an absence Task (PTO, Sick Leave, Unplanned Leave, public holiday or other absence, including a person's own holiday row) with no Original Estimate shall count its Completed Work in **Planned Hours**, so booked leave that was never planned is planned too. With a typed formula, **Utilization** uses the recorded hours (Completed Work) and **Planned utilization** the planned source chosen per figure, Absence as deducted from capacity in both, Funded Hours the profile's capacity base; blank when none of its hour figures is booked or it divides by 0; the status follows it. Unsaved edits ask before the drawer closes or moves to another person (Keep editing / Discard changes). ### 5.11 Baselines (BAS) — UC-11

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BAS-01 | A release shall be a Feature, not Removed, whose parent Epic title equals a rule's stream (trimmed, case-insensitive). | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-02 | Every stream shall use the same formula: baseline hours = hours per unit × (fixed units per release + units per driver × driver count). When a maximum is set and the result exceeds it, the baseline shall equal the maximum. There shall be no release types. | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-03 | Actual hours for a release shall be the Completed Work (negatives treated as 0) on its Month Tasks, and on Tasks under its Time Writing stories, rolled up to the nearest Feature. | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-04 | The table shall show, per release: stream, release, scope, baseline, actual, variance (actual − current baseline), and scope growth (current baseline − first baseline). | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-05 | "Set scope" / "Re-baseline" shall require the driver count (when the rule has units per driver > 0) before "Save baseline" is enabled. | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-06 | Saving a scope shall append a version {time, driver count, reason} to the release's history and write an audit entry "Baseline" or "Re-baseline". | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-07 | When a scope is identical to the current one, the form shall warn "Same scope as the current baseline; saving adds a version with no change." and still allow saving. | D | C | UC-11 |
| BAS-08 | The rules editor shall maintain one row per stream with: hours per unit, fixed units per release, driver, units per driver and maximum hours, and shall show the resulting formula. It shall warn about streams whose hours per unit is 0. | D | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-09 | Saving rules shall drop empty or duplicate stream names, replace negative or non-finite numbers with 0, and drop a maximum of 0. Rules saved in the earlier shape (release types and line items) shall be read as the uniform formula: the first line item is kept, and a types-only rule becomes 0h per document. Saving shall write an audit "Update"/"BaselineRules". | T | M | UC-11 |
| BAS-10 | "Load example values" shall load the example rules into the draft without saving. | D | C | UC-11 |
| BAS-11 | The "Task plan check · this month" panel shall flag a Month Task with ">25%" when Original Estimate > 0 and Completed Work > 1.25 × Original Estimate. See OI-07. | T | S | UC-11 |

### 5.12 Git storage (GIT) — UC-12

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GIT-01 | The app shall accept an owner and repository matching `^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,100}$` (a trailing `.git` is removed), a branch matching `^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]{1,200}$` without `..`, and a path ending in `.json` without empty or `..` segments. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-02 | The app shall keep the GitHub token in localStorage `demand-planning-git-token-v1` only when "Remember token in this browser" is ticked, and otherwise in memory for the session. | T | M | UC-12 |

| GIT-04 | "Connect & load" and "Load latest" shall replace those fields in the app with the file's content (the capacity settings only when the file has them). When the file does not exist and the branch exists, the app shall report that saving will create it. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-05 | The app shall reject a file that is not JSON, or is not schema 1, loading nothing. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-06 | "Save to Git" shall write the file with the loaded version id (sha) and the entered change description as the commit message. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-07 | When GitHub reports a conflict (HTTP 409 or 422), the app shall show "The data file changed in Git since it was loaded here. Load the latest version, then make your change again." and set the status "Conflict". | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-08 | The sync bar shall show "Unsaved changes — not yet in Git" whenever the current fields differ from the last synced file; it shall also be shown on Settings → Capacity. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-09 | In sample mode the app shall refuse to save to Git. | T | M | UC-12, UC-18 |
| GIT-10 | "Disconnect" shall remove the Git config and token from this browser, leaving app data and the repository unchanged. | T | S | UC-12 |
| GIT-11 | The app shall map HTTP 401, 403 and 404 to the specific messages listed in the code (`gitStorage.ts`). | T | S | UC-12 |
| GIT-12 | When Git is connected and a token is available, the app shall load the Git file automatically once at start, and check it again while the app is open (GIT-15). | D | S | UC-12 |
| GIT-13 | The Connect Git storage form shall start with the team's data repository prefilled — owner `philips-internal`, repository `InD_DemandPlanningStorage`, branch `main`, data file `planning-data.json` — so that a user adds only the token; the values stay editable, and a saved connection shows its own values instead. The Get started guide shall say so. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-14 | The shared planning file (planning-data.json) shall also hold **Settings → Table defaults** (UI-12) and the **release-source settings** (fuzzy match threshold and approval date field of Stream mapping), so that Save to Git shares them with everyone: each written once set, checked when read (unknown values and an invalid threshold dropped), and an older file without them leaves the browser's own values alone. A changed match threshold shows the Git save bar on Stream mapping. Before v7.7.0 both stayed in the browser where they were changed. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-15 | *(Retired — see GIT-18…GIT-20)* While the app is open it shall pick up what others save to Git: every **5 minutes**, and when the window comes back (at most once a minute), it lists the folders of the shared files it has read or saved (planning data, Access, Feature setup, QE Mapping, the On-Demand pages, the connection setup, the Monthly pack setup and month text, default and own views, historical hours) — a folder listing, no file contents — and compares their versions with the ones it has. **Newer planning data** is loaded at once (quietly, without reloading other areas) unless this browser has unsaved planning changes; the Storage message says "Updated from Git at …". Every other newer file, and planning data with unsaved changes, is named in a **"Newer data in Git" banner** with **Reload** (which says when reloading drops unsaved planning changes). Files saved from this browser do not count; no check with the synthetic sample or without Git storage. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-16 | Loading the planning data from Git (**Load latest**, **Update & load**, **Load latest from Git** on an error or a save conflict, and Data & sharing → Load everything) shall first ask for confirmation when this browser has changes not yet saved to Git, naming what is replaced (baselines, release scope, the region / role mapping, the capacity settings, table defaults) and, on a conflict, that someone else saved first; declined, nothing is loaded and the changes stay. Without unsaved changes it loads without asking. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-17 | Saves to the shared Git files that are merged rather than refused — historical hours (HIS-04), default views (MYD-09), the KPI tracker copy (KPT-03), connection backups (CON-88), the On-Demand pages (OND-28, OND-30; a page of row files: each row's file, its page.json and its counter, OND-33), the Tool Validations master list (TVL-02) and the Monthly pack template (PCK-08) — shall be **queued with their time (to the second)** and written one after another in that order, each as **its own commit** whose message ends with `[saved <UTC time>]` (followed by who saved it, GIT-22). Before each write the file shall be read again from Git; when someone else changed it after it was read here, the change shall be **merged** into the latest content by the file's entries (rows, pages, sheets, connections), keeping both changes and, for an entry both changed, the one with the later time (from the other commit's `[saved …]`, else its commit date); a change wins over a removal (for the On-Demand pages the later time wins there too, OND-28). The template is binary and not merged: the latest time wins, and an older save is not written. A conflict at write time shall read, merge and try again, at most **3 times**; then the save shall fail with "… kept changing in Git while saving (3 tries). Your change is kept here and not lost: choose Retry to save it again." and stay in the queue until **Retry** (or the page is closed). Where the save happens the app shall show **"Waiting to save (N in queue)"** while more than one save of the file waits, **"Saved to Git — merged with a change by someone else"** when merged, and the kept changes with Retry. Access, Epic / Feature setup, QE Mapping, the Monthly pack setup and month text and the planning data keep refusing a conflict (GIT-07). (Design defaults review, decision 8.) | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-18 | While the app is open it shall pick up what others save to Git: every **5 minutes**, and when the window comes back (at most once a minute), it lists the folders of the shared files it has read or saved — a folder listing, no file contents — and compares their versions with the ones it has (GIT-19). **Newer planning data** is loaded at once (quietly) unless this browser has unsaved planning changes; the Storage message says "Updated from Git at …". Every other newer file is **remembered per file** with who saved it and when (the app user its latest commit names, GIT-22 — else, for a commit made outside the app, its GitHub author — and its date) and shown **only on the page in use that uses it**, as a page-level **Notice** (warning, UI-STANDARDS §8 — on the page, not a pop-up, never a Background jobs entry): "**Newer data in Git** for {page}: {what} — saved by {who} on {when}" with **Load the newer data**. The files map to their pages: an On-Demand page's file → that page, every On-Demand Dashboard whose blocks or filters read it, and Admin → Pages & menu; a dashboard's file → that dashboard; qe-mapping.json → no page (no longer read, GIT-28); Feature / Epic setup → the pages that create Features and Admin → Epic & Feature setup; custom streams → the Release pages, Home and Admin → Streams; the connection setup → Admin → Streams; the Monthly pack setup and month text → Pulse Check and Admin → Reports; historical hours → the report pages and Admin; the KPI tracker copy → KPI tracker; planning data with unsaved changes → Baselines and the Admin pages; Access → Admin → People & roles only (loaded quietly on every other page, GIT-23); a file the build does not know → every page. The **menu layout** (the menu is refreshed), the **default and own views** and, away from People & roles, the **access roles** (GIT-23) are loaded quietly, with no notice. A change to another page is kept and shown when that page is opened, unless it was loaded meanwhile. No check with the synthetic sample or without Git storage. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-19 | Saves of **this window** shall never be shown as newer data (KB-82): every version this window saved (planning data, a queued GIT-17 save and its retries, a save that refuses a conflict, a removal) **and the version each save replaced** counts as its own, and a file with a save of this window on its way is left to the next check; a version a read, a listing or another check reports later is newer only when it is none of these. A version saved in **another window of the same browser** (same repository) is shown as "saved by you in another window of this browser". | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-20 | **Load the newer data** shall read again **only the files of the page in use** (an On-Demand page's own file, the area's file — Feature setup, Access …) **without reloading the app**; a page that holds what it shows (an added page with its drawer and pop-ups, a dashboard, the KPI tracker) opens afresh, any other page shows the data just loaded and keeps its open forms; only a file the build cannot load by itself (not mapped) reloads the app, and the notice says so. When the page holds edits that loading drops — a row being edited in its drawer, the columns or the name being set up, a dashboard block, tabs and filters being edited, unsaved planning changes — a pop-up names them and asks first (**Cancel**, **Load the newer data**). A file whose newer version the next save merges anyway (GIT-17) is **not** loaded silently on the page in use, so rows do not change under the viewer's eyes; the menu layout and the views are the exceptions (GIT-18). | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-21 | When the open-app check (GIT-18) finds a newer **menu layout** (loaded quietly), the app shall also list the On-Demand folder and read **only the pages it does not know yet** (single-file pages and folders of row files alike; an On-Demand Dashboard's blocks are read when it is opened), so a page someone else added appears in the menu and on Home without ↻ Reload from Git or a restart; a page **deleted** by someone else leaves the list (a page **hidden** by someone else leaves the menu with the layout, PGB-19) — unless it is the page in use with edits not saved here: it stays open with its "Newer data in Git" notice. Quietly, no notice; pages already loaded are not read again. | T | M | UC-12, UC-38 |
| GIT-22 | Every commit the app writes to the data repository (a file saved, created or deleted, a queued save of GIT-17, a commit of many files, a row lock) shall name **the app user who saved it** only after GitHub confirms the destination repository is private, at the end of its message, after any `[saved …]` stamp: `[by {display name} <{work email}>]` — the name Azure DevOps gives for the work email (when known) and the work email of My settings (CON-82), only `[by {work email}]` or `[by {name}]` when one is unknown, nothing while viewing as someone else (no saves then) — never a token, a PAT or anything of the GitHub account; brackets in a name are dropped, so the tag cannot be forged by the name. Public, internal and unverified repositories receive no app author tag. The "Newer data in Git" notice (GIT-18) and the access line (GIT-23) shall name that person (the name, else the work email); a commit made **outside the app** (no tag) is named by its GitHub author as before. Commits made before this version carry no tag and show their GitHub author. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-23 | A newer **access.json** found by the open-app check (GIT-18) shall be **loaded quietly** on every page — the rights change in place, the menu and the buttons follow at once — instead of raising the notice everywhere; the "Newer data in Git" notice for Access shall be shown **only on Admin → People & roles** (where someone may be editing the roles; leaving that page loads it quietly). When the viewer's **own rights** change by it, a short **info line** shall be shown once on the page in use — "Your access was changed by {who}: {what changed}", e.g. "you can now edit On-Demand data (rows)", "“Set up On-Demand pages (pages, columns, options)” removed", "you now see QE Mapping", "QE Mapping no longer shown" (at most four, then "and n more"), {who} as GIT-22 — with **Dismiss**; it goes when another page is opened. Not a pop-up, never a Background jobs entry. Access still fails closed (CON-101): until the newer file is read the rights loaded before stay in force, and a file that cannot be read keeps them. | T | M | UC-12, UC-22 |
| GIT-24 | When the saved Git storage connection still names a repository the team's data has **moved away from** (`ponkritz/DemandPlanningStorage`), the app shall show a warning notice on every page naming the new repository (`philips-internal/InD_DemandPlanningStorage`, GIT-13) and the old one, with **Switch to …**, which connects to the new repository with the same branch, data file and token (asking first when there are unsaved changes, as Change connection does) and loads from it; without a token the notice shall open Git storage instead. The saved connection shall not be changed without that click; owner and repository names are compared without letter case. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-25 | A read of a shared file from the Git data repository that GitHub answers with **404** shall count as **"file missing"** only when the repository and branch are readable with the token: GitHub also answers 404 for a repository the token cannot see (a private repository, a token without access to the organisation, a fine-grained token not yet approved, a wrong branch). The app shall then probe the branch **once per repository, branch and token** and reuse the answer for every read of the session (a successful file read counts as readable; an unreadable answer is asked again after a minute; a token or rate-limit refusal is reported as such). When the repository cannot be read, every such read (planning data, access roles, custom streams, the connection setup and its clean-up marker, Feature / Epic setup, the On-Demand pages and folders, the Windchill exports, the on-prem view data, the Monthly pack) shall **fail with "Repository {owner}/{repo} or branch {branch} was not found, or the token cannot access it."** and **nothing this browser has shall be cleared or replaced** because of it — the cached custom streams, the connections and their PATs, the links, the cached access roles, the Windchill views. The read-only notice shall say that **the data repository cannot be read** (not "no access roles yet") with **Open Git storage**, and **Set up roles** (CON-101 bootstrap) shall be offered only when the repository is readable and access.json is really missing. A failed start-up sync of the connection setup shall say why on Admin → Data & sharing (Git storage, and Shared setup in Git). (KB-92) | T | M | UC-12, UC-22 |
| GIT-26 | The first sync of the **connection setup** from a repository other than the one this browser last synced with (Git storage **Change connection…**, **Switch to …** of GIT-24; a browser from before this version with connections from Git counts as another one) shall **change nothing here**: it adds the connections of the new repository that are not here (with the PAT of their server), **removes and replaces none** (a connection that differs is kept as it is here) and **does not apply a clean-up marker** (azure-views-reset.json, CON-88) **or a removal marker** (azure-views-removed.json, CON-111) found there (both marked as seen; a view marked removed there is not added); connections and PATs in this browser are never removed because the new repository's setup differs. It shall say what differs on Admin → Data & sharing (Git storage, and Shared setup in Git): "Git storage now uses another repository ({owner}/{repo}). The connection setup there has N connections that were not here (added: …) and lacks M that are here (kept here: …); K connections differ from here (kept as here: …). … Nothing here was removed or replaced, and your PATs are kept", pointing to the existing actions (Save connections to Git, Restore from Git, remove in Settings). From then on the connections that came from the old repository count as this browser's own, so a later sync with the new repository never removes them. A connection removed by a later ordinary sync (removed in Git) keeps its PAT as its server's PAT. (KB-92) | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-27 | **A save GitHub did not answer, or answered with a server error**, shall be said in plain words and tried once more by itself (product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "Not saved to Git … — Failed to fetch", then HTTP 500 on Retry): a queued save of GIT-17 whose read or write gets **no answer** (the browser's "Failed to fetch", "NetworkError …", "Load failed": offline, DNS, VPN or proxy, firewall, or a gateway error the browser had to drop) or **HTTP 500, 502, 503 or 504** shall start again from the read **once**, after 1–2 s (with jitter) — safe, because every write sends the version it read: a first write that did reach Git leaves the file already the same (nothing written twice), one over a version that changed meanwhile is refused and merged. When it fails again the change is **kept** (the page's kept change with Retry and Discard, OND-28; the GIT-17 queue's Retry for the others) with **"GitHub could not be reached (network, VPN or proxy): {file} is not saved. Your change is kept here — check the connection and press Retry. (Browser: Failed to fetch)"** or **"GitHub had a problem saving {file} (HTTP 500). Your change is kept here — press Retry in a minute. If it keeps failing, the page file is {n} KB — a page with many rows saves faster as row files (the page's ⋯ → Convert to row files…). (GitHub: …)"** (for another file "the file is {n} KB"); Retry reads the current version again and writes the change. Any other answer (401, 403, 404, a conflict) is reported as before (GIT-07, GIT-25). | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-28 | **Delete qe-mapping.json from Git** (QE Mapping removed, NAV-54): the app shall no longer use or save `qe-mapping.json`, and a change to it never raises the newer-data notice (GIT-18). While the file **exists** in the Git data repository, Admin → Data & sharing → **Shared setup in Git** (people with "Shared setup in Git, master data, audit, KPI tracker": Tool Owner and Admin by default) shall show a panel **Data no longer used** with the notice "qe-mapping.json is no longer used by the app — Delete it from Git", **Download a copy** (the file as it is in Git, saved as `qe-mapping-copy-YYYY-MM-DD.json`) and **Delete it from Git…**, disabled with the reason until a copy is downloaded in this visit, without Git storage, in **View as**, with the **synthetic sample**, with a **read-only Git token** and for anyone who does not hold the **Tool Owner** role (an Admin sees the notice). Delete it from Git… opens a **confirmation pop-up** (eyebrow "GIT · SHARED SETUP", Cancel, Delete from Git) naming the file and the commit — and, when a DP or IOCC bulk Epic's Feature setup still names only the old QE Mapping columns, the line "Set the QE page in each bulk Epic's Feature setup first: {Epics} still use(s) the old QE Mapping columns only." (the delete is not blocked); Delete from Git verifies that the configured repository is private and makes **one commit** that deletes only that file, at the version downloaded ("Delete qe-mapping.json: no longer used by the app (QE Mapping removed)"). If the file changed since the copy, nothing is deleted and the panel asks for a new copy. Afterwards the notice is gone and says the file was deleted; its history stays in GitHub. Only the files the app lists as retired can be read or deleted this way. Older app versions that still show QE Mapping then find no file and offer an empty mapping (LIM-130). | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-29 | **Forced update from Git** (product owner, 2026-11-25: "Force Users to Update this once from GIT — show Load everything from GIT as a Pop Up. A write of this computer to the file that replaced the version loaded here (a Stream mapping save, the move of CON-121, a removal for everyone) counts as loaded; a write over a version not loaded here does not. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-30 | The forced pop-up shall be the shared `Modal` (eyebrow `GIT · STREAMS CHANGED`, title "Load everything from Git") made **blocking** — no ×, Escape and a click beside it do nothing, it stays above every other pop-up — whose **only action** is **Load everything from Git**, running the same load as Admin → Data & sharing → Load everything (planning data, connection setup with the person's Other views, on-prem view data, Windchill exports, access roles, Feature setup) as a background job (GIT-33); when every part loads, the version is recorded (GIT-29). It shall **not** show while viewing as someone else, with the synthetic sample, without Git storage, when the file is missing, before the start sync has read the setup, or while this computer is saving the file. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-31 | The failed reload dialog shall offer Retry and allow dismissal without retry only in the outage case of GIT-32 (product owner, 2026-11-26: "No, don't show that. Add the connection setup there in the pop up itself."): when the load fails in any part it shall name each part that failed with GitHub's reason (e.g. "Connection setup: GitHub could not read azure-views-reset.json (HTTP 500)"), and show inside the pop-up the **Git storage connection setup** — the same form as Admin → Data & sharing → Git storage (owner, repository, branch, data file whose folder holds the shared files, GitHub token; the token kept in this browser or desktop app only, never in Git) — and **Retry** (Load everything from Git), which loads with the connection as saved there. The setup is shown for every failure, also one the setup cannot fix (a GitHub server error, no network): Retry starts the background load (GIT-33). The failure dialog blocks the app until Retry starts that job or the user takes the session-only continuation allowed by GIT-32 (LIM-155). | T | M | UC-12 |
