A failure the setup can fix (a token refused, a repository or branch not found) shall offer no skip (product owner, 2026-11-26: "Is there a know about a GIT Outage? And if that is the case add Continue Without Loading"). | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-33 | **Load everything from Git as a background job** (product owner, 2026-11-26: "Make the Loading as a Backend Process.. move it into Jobs Done"): pressing **Load everything from Git** in the forced pop-up (GIT-30) — and **Retry** after a failure — shall **close the pop-up at once** and run the load as a background job **"Load everything from Git"** in the top bar's Background jobs list (Jobs done): **Running** with the part being loaded ("Loading connection setup…"), then **Done** with what was loaded, or **Needs a look** naming each part that failed; the app can be used meanwhile. **Admin → Data & sharing → Load from Git → Load everything from Git** shall start the same job; the panel shows that it runs in the background and, when it ends, its result as before. A job that loaded every part records the version of the Streams file (GIT-29). | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-34 | **One load at a time**: while the Load everything job runs, no second one shall start — the forced pop-up is not shown and the Admin button is disabled, reading "Loading in the background…" with a line pointing to Background jobs. | T | M | UC-12 |
| GIT-35 | While the Load everything job runs the forced pop-up shall **stay closed** — also when Git storage reloads the planning data or this computer saves the Streams file during the load (a write of this computer during the job counts as loaded, GIT-29). View as, the synthetic sample and no Git storage still show no pop-up (GIT-30). | T | M | UC-12 |

### 5.13 Snapshots (SNP) — UC-13

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNP-01 | *(Retired — moved to the backlog: SharePoint is not functional; see PROJECT-PLAN.md → Backlog)* The SharePoint connection shall require an Application (client) ID. It shall sign in with an MSAL popup (scopes `User.Read`, `Sites.ReadWrite.All`; cache in sessionStorage). | D | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-02 | *(Retired — moved to the backlog: SharePoint is not functional; see PROJECT-PLAN.md → Backlog)* When connected, the app shall save the full app state to the central SharePoint file 900 ms after the last change, using the file's etag. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-03 | *(Retired — moved to the backlog: SharePoint is not functional; see PROJECT-PLAN.md → Backlog)* When SharePoint rejects a save because the etag changed (HTTP 412), the app shall show "Another user updated the central data. Pull the latest version before editing again." | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-04 | The snapshot buttons "Save snapshot to Git" (LTE) and "Save PI snapshot to Git" shall be disabled unless Git storage is connected with a token that can write, the synthetic sample is not loaded and no save is running; the page names the destination (repository and folder) and warns before it is closed while a save runs. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-05 | A snapshot shall contain its format, version, id, title, type, month, creation time, creator, build, and **all the data present at the moment** (owner decision 11; format version 2): schemaVersion, people, activities, efforts, baselines, periods, releases, audits, masters, the work items with their hours (azureWorkItems), azureBacklogScope, setupLinks, capacitySettings, baselineRules, releaseScopes, resourceProfiles, the release data (releaseSource, releasePlans, windchillSources, azureSources), tableDefaults and releaseSourceSettings — never earlier snapshots, the data of My views or anything outside the app data. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-06 | A snapshot shall be saved to the Git data repository as "{planning data folder}/lte-snapshots/{month}-{type}-{id}.json", one commit per snapshot, as a **new file only** (an existing file is never replaced). It shall be read back and compared byte for byte before success is reported. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-07 | The app shall reject a snapshot larger than 50 MB, and shall never store a snapshot in the browser. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-08 | Snapshot history shall list only matching snapshot files of the lte-snapshots folder in Git, newest month first, each with a link to the file in GitHub, its month and type. | T | M | UC-13 |
| SNP-09 | The Snapshots page shall offer CSV and JSON exports. | D | S | UC-15 |

### 5.14 Settings (SET) — UC-14

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SET-01 | Settings shall provide section links: Azure DevOps, Storage, Capacity & periods, Resources, Master data, Audit. | D | S | UC-14 |
| SET-02 | Capacity settings shall provide: Absence Epic ID (default 37778), funded hours per working day (default 8), overloaded above % (default 100), has capacity below % (default 80). | D | M | UC-14 |
| SET-03 | When availableBelow ≥ overloadedAt, the app shall store the defaults 80 and 100. An invalid value shall be replaced by its default. See OI-05. | T | M | UC-14 |
| SET-04 | Saving capacity settings shall write an audit "Update"/"CapacitySettings". | T | S | UC-14 |
| SET-05 | Period control shall lock or unlock planning and actuals separately for the selected month, with a reason. Each toggle shall be audited ("Lock planning", "Unlock planning", "Lock actuals", "Unlock actuals"). | T | M | UC-14 |
| SET-06 | "Extend until" shall set the planning or actuals extension to the end of the chosen date in UTC (NAV-51). See OI-01. | T | M | UC-14 |
| SET-07 | Resources shall list the people per CAP-09 and allow setting Region (India, Netherlands, US), Role (QE, QMS, CAPA, Lead, Manager), People manager and Reports to. Each change shall be audited. | T | M | UC-14 |
| SET-08 | The app shall not allow a person to report to themself, and shall report circular reporting lines as "Circular reporting line, not shown above: {names}." | T | M | UC-14 |
| SET-09 | The Resources section shall list the regional holidays of the selected month, and the people without a region. | D | S | UC-14 |
| SET-10 | *(Retired — see EPS-01)* Master data shall allow adding a value to: Release Type, Activity Type, Status, Resource Band, Complexity. See OI-06. | D | C | UC-14 |
| SET-11 | The Audit section shall show the 8 most recent entries (action, entity, detail, time). | D | M | UC-14 |
| SET-12 | "Reset synthetic MVP data" shall replace the app state with the seed state. See OI-09. | D | C | UC-14 |
| SET-13 | "Reports to" shall also accept a manager who has no Time Writing Tasks (not on the board): "Other (type a name)…" takes a name; a name typed in this way shall be offered to everyone under "Not on the board", and the reporting lines shall show such a manager at the top, marked "not on the board", with the people who report to them. | T | S | UC-14 |
| SET-14 | *(Retired — see SET-15)* Settings shall be a menu group of its own with **one page per area** — Azure DevOps, Storage, Access, Feature setup, and for roles with "Manage shared settings" also Capacity & periods, Resources, Master data, Monthly pack, Table defaults (UI-12) and Audit — instead of tabs on one page; the header shall name the page. A page with several parts shall show them as **sections, one at a time**, chosen in a row of section buttons: Azure DevOps — Servers and PATs, Stream mapping, Other views, Read-only sources (when there are any), Synthetic sample; Storage — Git storage, Load from Git (when Git is connected), Shared setup in Git (shared roles), SharePoint; Capacity & periods — Capacity and absence, Holidays and common activities, Period control. A page with one part shows it without the row. The last page and the last section of each page shall be remembered in this browser; links into Settings (Get started, a stream not set up, Baselines) shall open the page and the section they are about. | T | S | UC-01, UC-14 |
| SET-15 | The settings shall be split into **My settings** and **Admin**. **My settings** — what each person sets for themselves: Your work email, Servers and PATs, My views (SET-17), My naming patterns (QV-60), My page usage (NAV-45) — shall open from the **profile at the top right** (NAV-48; as User settings in Azure DevOps), for everyone. The menu group **Admin** shall hold the shared setup, one page per area, each shown only to people allowed at least one of its permissions (CON-94): **Overview** (ADM-01), **Streams** (the stream mapping, CON-93), **Stream options** (Epics to match Features in, Bulk creation Epics, Release counts), **Epic & Feature setup** (EPS-08), **PI Review Setup** (the PI Review form, for those allowed to set it up, PIR-29), **People & roles** (People, Capacity roles, Role rights), **Capacity & time** (Capacity and absence, Calculation profiles, Bucket rules, Insight thresholds, Holidays and common activities, Period control, Historical hours), **Reports** (Monthly pack, Table and chart defaults), **Data & sharing** (Git storage, Load from Git, Shared setup in Git, Other views, Read-only sources, Synthetic sample), **History** (the audit). The header shall name the page. A page with several parts shall show them as sections, one at a time, without a row when there is only one; the last page and section shall be remembered in this browser. Links and remembered choices to the former pages and sections (Azure DevOps → Servers and PATs, Your work email, Other views…; Access; Table defaults; Historical hours) shall open where they moved. | T | S | UC-01, UC-14 |
| SET-16 | Every settings section shall show a **scope chip** saying whom a change affects, before it is made: **Only you · This computer** (My settings), **This computer** (Git storage, Load from Git, Read-only sources, Synthetic sample), **Everyone · Shared in Git** (shared setup), **Yours or everyone's** (sections with a personal and a shared layer: Feature setup, Bulk creation Epics, Other views) and **Everyone · Tool Owner only** (Streams, Shared setup in Git); its tooltip explains it. | I | S | UC-14 |
| SET-17 | **My views** (My settings) shall let a person add their own **read-only** views: a name, a server they already have a PAT for (the server's PAT is used; on-premises servers stay read-only), a project and a WIQL query. A view shall be listed under **Other views** in that person's menu and fetched like other views, and shall be kept in this browser or desktop app only: never in the shared setup in Git, its data never saved to Git or a JSON backup, kept when an admin resets the shared connection setup or the Tool Owner removes shared views for everyone (CON-111), and not listed in Data & sharing → Other views. A view shall never replace or change a stream: a name that names a stream (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations…) is refused, and a view is never matched to a stream or data set. A duplicate of another view (same server, project and query) is refused. | T | S | UC-14 |
| SET-18 | The settings of how the app looks shall be on **one page, Look & feel**, opened from the profile menu, with a tab each: **Page layout** (NAV-50), **Theme and zoom** (Follow the computer / Light / Dark, in step with the top-bar theme button; the desktop zoom stays in the footer), **Start view and menu** (Tiles or Classic, NAV-46; Customize menu) — these three only for the person on this computer — and **Table and chart defaults** (UI-12, UI-17; shared with everyone, only for roles allowed "Table, chart and page defaults"), which moves here from Admin → Reports; an older link to it opens here. | T | S | UC-01, UC-14 |
| SET-19 | **Delete a view of your own from its page** (product owner, 2026-11-25): the page of a view of the person's own (My views, SET-17), listed under **Other views** in the menu, shall offer **Delete this view…** as the last item of its ⋯ menu (danger colour). A confirmation pop-up (`MY VIEWS · THIS COMPUTER`) shall name the view and its server and project and say what is deleted — its definition (name, server, project, query), the data fetched for it here and its menu entry — and that only this browser or desktop app changes: never the shared setup in Git, other people's views, the server's PAT or Azure DevOps. **Delete view** shall delete it with the same code and the same pop-up as My settings → My views (whose row action becomes **Delete…** with this pop-up instead of a browser confirm). The item shall be disabled with the reason as its tooltip while viewing as someone else (View as is read-only); Git storage, the sample data and the viewer's role do not matter for it. After the deletion the app shall open the start page (Home, else the first page the viewer may open) — never a page for the missing view — with a dismissible confirmation line naming the view, and record it in Background jobs. | T | S | UC-14 |
| SET-20 | **Admin → Data & sharing → Other views** (everyone) shall say that the views listed are **the person's own** — kept in Git in their file (named) and seen only by them — and for admins (`storage.shared`, not while viewing as someone else) show below it the panel **Unassigned Other views** (CON-122, CON-123): each view's name, use, server · project and query, the Assign to email and Assign…, ↻ Reload, and "No unassigned Other views." when the list is empty. | T | S | UC-14 |

### 5.15 Local storage, backup and recovery (STO) — UC-15

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STO-01 | The app shall save the app state to IndexedDB 200 ms after the last change, with saves queued in order. A save shall count as done only when its transaction completes. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-02 | On start, the app shall load IndexedDB. Otherwise it shall migrate the legacy localStorage copy `demand-planning-mvp-v1` (keeping it), and otherwise it shall use the seed state. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-03 | When saved data cannot be read, the app shall not fall back to the seed state and shall not overwrite the saved data. It shall show "Saved data could not be loaded. Nothing was overwritten." and a "Retry loading saved data" button. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-04 | When a save fails, the app shall show a global error with "Export JSON backup now" and "Retry browser save". | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-05 | While a local save or a snapshot save is pending, the app shall warn before the page is unloaded. | D | S | UC-15 |
| STO-06 | "Export current JSON backup" shall download the full app state as `demand-planning-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.json`. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-07 | Restore shall reject: a file over 200 MB; a file that is not an object; schemaVersion ≠ 1; required arrays missing; optional fields of the wrong shape; and Azure work items missing required fields. In each case the existing data shall be left unchanged. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-08 | Restore shall require the confirmation checkbox. | T | M | UC-15 |
| STO-09 | PATs, connection profiles and the Git token shall never be part of the app state, a JSON backup, a snapshot or a Git file. | I, T | M | UC-15 |

### 5.16 Web update (UPD) — UC-16

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UPD-01 | The build shall emit `build-info.json` with app, formatVersion, version, buildId, builtAt, revision and buildNumber, and inject `<meta name="app-build-id">` into the HTML. | T, I | M | UC-16, UC-19 |
| UPD-02 | "↻ Force Update" shall open a dialog showing the running version, build number, build ID, built time and revision, and the deployed build. | D | M | UC-16 |
| UPD-03 | The reload shall be blocked, with the first applicable reason shown, while: a fetch is running; a save is pending; a failed save or an unsaved preview exists without a JSON export; or the acknowledgement is not ticked. | T | M | UC-16 |
| UPD-04 | The app shall reload only when the fetched HTML's build id equals the deployed manifest's build id. Otherwise it shall show "The deployment is still propagating, or the server returned an older page. Please retry shortly. Nothing was reloaded." | T | M | UC-16 |
| UPD-05 | The update check shall use no-store, no credentials, no redirects and a 15 s timeout. | T | M | UC-16 |
| UPD-06 | Force Update shall not clear IndexedDB data, PAT settings or column preferences. | T | M | UC-16 |
| UPD-07 | When a view cannot load because its code is no longer on the server — a newer build was deployed while the page was open ("Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" and the other browsers' wording) — the app shall say **"A newer version of the app was published"** instead of the data message, and **Load the new version** shall load the app from a fresh address (a new `_dp_refresh`), never the browser's cached copy of the old page; data in this browser and in Git stays as it is (product owner, 26 Nov 2026: "I keep getting this error"). | T | M | UC-16 |

### 5.17 Desktop app and updater (DSK) — UC-17

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DSK-01 | The web app shall offer "⬇ Desktop app" with the Windows (.exe) and macOS (.dmg, universal) downloads from the `desktop-latest` release, recommending the detected OS. | D | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-02 | The desktop window shall run with contextIsolation on, nodeIntegration off and sandbox on. It shall deny new windows, opening https links in the system browser. | I | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-03 | The desktop app shall accept IPC requests only from its own main frame (the bundled index.html, or the dev server origin). | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-04 | The desktop Azure DevOps proxy shall allow only the read operations areas, backlog-epics, iterations, iteration-tree, fields, query, items, team-members, comments and updates (one work item's history, FET-24), plus the constrained writes create-feature, create-child, update-time-writing-effort and update-work-item. | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-05 | The desktop proxy shall try API versions 7.1, 6.0 and 5.0 on a version error, and shall remember the working one per collection. | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-06 | The desktop proxy shall not follow redirects. It shall report a redirect as a sign-in problem. | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-07 | "Check for updates" shall read `latest.json` from `https://github.com/neurolooom-eng/Test2/releases/download/desktop-latest/` and report an update only when its buildNumber is greater than the installed one. | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-08 | The desktop updater shall download only the installer named for this OS from that fixed address. It shall install it only when its size and SHA-256 equal `latest.json`, and shall delete it otherwise. | T | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-09 | On Windows the updater shall start the verified installer and close the app. On macOS it shall open the verified disk image. | D | M | UC-17 |
| DSK-10 | The desktop app shall check quietly 5 s after start and every 4 hours. It shall show "Update available" with "Update now" and "Later". "Later" shall hide the notice until a newer build appears. | D | S | UC-17 |
| DSK-11 | The desktop Azure DevOps proxy shall accept a Discussion page token (`continuationToken`) on the read operation `comments` only: a non-empty text of at most 2,048 characters, sent URL-encoded in the query string of the fixed Comments address (the connection's host, `…/_apis/wit/workItems/{id}/comments`, api-version 7.1-preview.4); any other value, or a token on another operation, shall be refused before a request is sent. A 429 answer to a read shall be reported as "Azure DevOps is limiting requests (429)." with its Retry-After seconds (PIR-06). | T | M | UC-17, UC-40 |

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SMP-01 | "Load synthetic sample" shall replace the Time Writing cache and release source with a deterministic generated dataset, without reading from or writing to Azure DevOps, and shall audit "Load sample". | T | M | UC-18 |
| SMP-02 | When saved Azure data exists, loading the sample from Settings shall ask for confirmation first. | D | M | UC-18 |
| SMP-03 | In sample mode the app shall show "Showing a synthetic sample dataset, not Azure DevOps data. Refresh from Azure to replace it." | D | M | UC-18 |
| SMP-04 | The sample shall cover every model the pages use: people with profiles, streams, absence, holidays for three regions, the release source, and release scopes including a re-baseline. | T | S | UC-18 |
| SMP-05 | While the synthetic sample is loaded, **every** write to Azure DevOps — from any page: Feature and User Story creation, Time Writing Tasks and hours, edits, state and Sprint moves, comments — shall be refused before anything is sent, with "Synthetic sample data is loaded: nothing is written to Azure DevOps. Fetch your data first." | T | M | UC-18 |
| SMP-06 | **Reset synthetic MVP data** (Admin → History) shall ask for confirmation naming what is replaced (periods, capacity, resources, baselines, History; the fetched Azure DevOps data until the next fetch; connections and PATs kept), and shall record the reset in History. | T | M | UC-18 |

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DOC-01 | The Documentation page shall show, as tabs, every Markdown document in `docs/`: User guide (first, DOC-12), Validation package, Requirements, Traceability matrix, Test plan, Verification plan, UAT plan, Known issues, Software components, Security assessment, DFMEA, Development process, Azure DevOps setup, Project plan, CP release reporting brief, SWBOM, Release notes, Configuration management. | T | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-02 | The page shall show the documents bundled with the running build. | I | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-03 | The page shall render headings, paragraphs, nested lists, tables, rules, inline code, bold, italic and links as formatted text. It shall not inject HTML from the documents. | T, I | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-04 | A link to another bundled document shall open that document in the page, at the linked section when an anchor is given. A link to a section shall scroll to it. | T, D | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-05 | Only https links shall be clickable external links, opening in a new tab. Links of any other scheme shall be shown as plain text. | T | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-06 | The page shall show an "On this page" list of the document's level-2 sections, linking to each. | D | S | UC-20 |
| DOC-07 | "Download .md" shall download the shown document under its file name. | D | S | UC-20 |
| DOC-08 | The page shall remember the selected document in localStorage `demand-planning-docs-tab-v1`. | D | C | UC-20 |
| DOC-09 | The repository shall hold a **Validation Package** indexed by `docs/VALIDATION-PACKAGE.md`: the requirements at three levels (user, system, detailed), the generated traceability matrix, the test plan, the verification and UAT plans, the known-issues register (known bugs, open issues, accepted residual risks and limitations), the software components (in-house, third-party with versions and licences, platforms, known vulnerabilities), the security assessment (code analysis, penetration test, product security risk assessment, mitigations traced to requirements and test cases), the DFMEA (controls traced to requirements) and the development process mapped to ISO 13485 §7.3 / §4.1.6, IEC 62304 and the FDA CSA guidance. Every package document is bundled into the build and shown on the Documentation page. | I | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-10 | `node scripts/traceability.mjs` shall generate `docs/TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md` from the documents — use case → user requirement → system requirement → detailed requirement → test case → UAT day → automated test → software component, with a gap list — and `pnpm test` shall fail when the committed matrix differs from the generated one, a requirement in force has no test case, a test case names an unknown requirement, a UAT day names an unknown or retired test case, a use case has no user requirement, a user requirement no system requirement, a system requirement no detailed requirement, an ID is defined twice, or a source file is not listed as a software component. | T | M | UC-19, UC-20 |
| DOC-11 | `pnpm test` shall check the Validation Package documents: each has its required sections; finding, risk, mitigation, DFMEA and issue IDs are unique; every requirement, test case, mitigation, DFMEA row, finding, risk or issue ID cited in a package document exists in the document that defines it; the package index lists every package document and the app bundles them. | T | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-12 | In Web and Windows Desktop, Documentation shall offer a Tool Validation Package area listing and downloading supported files only from `tool-validation/demandplanning` and its defined category folders in the configured Git storage repository and branch. It shall use the configured PAT in authenticated GET headers; Contents: Read shall suffice. It shall display category, original format and draft status, provide search and explicit refresh, handle missing or denied access, and preserve binary bytes above 1 MB. Neither private files nor the PAT shall be bundled or persisted in app data/exports; PATs shall never occur in download URLs or errors. Blank templates shall be distinguished from draft records; downloads shall not imply approval or execution. | T, I | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-13 | The Documentation page shall offer a Design documents area that bundles every Markdown file under `docs/design/`, including nested folders, with the design index as its default entry. Users shall be able to navigate between bundled documents, open repository source links on GitHub and download the displayed document as Markdown. The area shall use the app’s existing Documentation access. | T, D | M | UC-20 |
| DOC-14 | The repository shall hold a **user guide**, `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, written for end users as short tasks with steps: getting started (browser and desktop, one PAT per server, Git storage, work email, the synthetic sample, the menu, Home, theme and zoom, Background jobs, "Newer data in Git"), roles and rights (the reason line, View as), daily work (with editing My dashboard, MYD-14), reports, Release Details, On-Demand pages (with Save view for everyone, UI-20…UI-23), Insights (the chart editor's tabs, CHT-70…CHT-73), On-Demand Dashboards, PDF and interactive HTML, Admin, and troubleshooting. Its Contents shall list every chapter. It shall describe only features of the build it ships with: no placeholder sections. The Documentation page shall show it as its first tab, and the profile menu shall offer **User guide**, which opens it. | T, I | S | UC-20 |
| DOC-15 | The user guide's screenshots (`![alt](user-guide/images/….png)`) and clips (a line `<video … src="user-guide/media/….webm" poster="….png" title="…"></video>`, WebM with a PNG poster, muted, loaded only when played) shall be bundled with the build and shown on the Documentation page — images and videos only from the app's own files (CSP `media-src 'self'`), never from another host, the video line read as plain values (src, poster, title), never as HTML. A picture that is not in the build shall show its text instead. Every clip shall have a caption, and its steps shall be written out next to it, so the guide is complete without the clips. Every picture the guide shows shall exist and every file in its folders be shown; the screenshots shall stay under 8 MB, the clips under 12 MB (each under 1.5 MB). | T | S | UC-20 |
| DOC-16 | `scripts/user-guide-shots.mjs` (`pnpm docs:shots`, `pnpm docs:videos`) shall make every screenshot and clip of the user guide from the built app, with **synthetic data only** — the synthetic sample and made-up pages, people and files served by a fake GitHub, Azure DevOps refused — and shall stop before a capture that shows a real server, organisation or repository name, a token, or an email outside `example.com`. | T, I | S | UC-20 |

| UI-05 | Every action button shall be clearly visible: raised (shadow, lifting slightly on hover) and coloured by what it does — solid indigo with white text for the main action, white with an indigo outline for secondary actions, a tinted pill for small inline actions, green for Save to Git, red for Delete / Remove — in light and dark theme; disabled buttons shall be flat and grey (not-allowed cursor). Toggle groups and filter chips keep their own look. | D | S | UC-01 |

The widths, order, hidden columns, wrap, density and sort are kept per table in the viewer's browser (the sort since v10.6; the widths and Wrap text, kept apart before, are folded in once); **Reset layout** returns to the view for everyone when one is saved (UI-20), else to the table's default. CSV cells stay formula-safe (CON-69). | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |

Azure times with a zone are written in the viewer's local time; numbers stay numbers. | T | M | UC-08 |

| UI-16 | The side menu shall fold to icons on wide screens with a **«** button in the menu header (**»** shows it with names again); folded, a page's name shows on hover, the zoom control is hidden (Ctrl keys still zoom) and the choice is kept per computer. Pop-ups without input (Get started, Download the desktop app, View as, Search settings) also close on a click beside them. | T | M | UC-01 |
| UI-20 | Every table built on the shared table component (UI-07, UI-13) shall offer, in its **Columns** panel, **Save view for everyone** and, once one is saved, **Clear the view for everyone** (confirmed in a pop-up) to a person with the right "Table, chart and page defaults" (`layout.defaults`: the Tool Owner, and the Admin by the starting matrix) with Git storage that can be written; fail closed — without the right, with a read-only token, with the synthetic sample or in View as the actions are not shown (hidden, not disabled, as the per-object ⋯ exception of UI-STANDARDS §3). The view is the column order, the shown and hidden columns, the widths, Wrap text, the density and the sort (column and direction; not for a table whose owner sorts the rows itself); it is saved once per table id in the shared views defaults in Git (`personal-views-defaults.json`, entry `table:<table id>`, with who saved it and when in `by:table:<table id>`), as Column options → Save for everyone (BKL-47): queued and merged per entry with a save by someone else in between (GIT-17), its result listed under Background jobs. The panel says which view is shown: "The view for everyone, saved by … on …", "Your own view" or "The built-in view". | T | S | UC-08, UC-14 |
| UI-21 | A table shall show the viewer's **own view** when they changed it, else the **view for everyone**, else the table's built-in view. A viewer who never changed a table sees the view for everyone ("first time") and a later one as soon as it is read (at start or Load from Git); the first change a viewer makes starts their own view from the view for everyone they see, which they then keep when the view for everyone changes. With their own view the Columns panel shows "Your own view · **Use the view for everyone**" (their own view removed); when a view for everyone was saved after their own view started (or their own view is older than any) it reads "A new view for everyone is available — **Use it**". After saving, the Tool Owner's own view is removed, so they see what everyone sees. | T | S | UC-08, UC-14 |
| UI-22 | A view for everyone (or an own view) shall be applied to the table's columns as they are: a column that no longer exists is ignored (order, hidden columns, widths, sort — a sort on it falls back to the table's default sort), a column the view does not know comes at the end and is shown. Views read from Git or the browser keep only known shapes (column ids of up to 200 characters, at most 300 columns, widths between 48 and 2,000 px, Compact or Comfortable, a sort as column and direction). | T | S | UC-08 |
| UI-23 | **Page filters** that a page remembers per person shall offer the same **Save view for everyone** / **Clear the view for everyone** beside the filters, with the same precedence (the person's own choice, else the view for everyone, else the page's start) and "Your own view · **Use the view for everyone**": the Team overview's period, filters and tab (`dashboard`), an On-Demand Dashboard page's period, filters and tab (`dashboard-page:<id>`), an Insights page's filter row (`insights-page:<id>`) and the People & roles → People filters (Region, Role, Access role, Status; from this version remembered per person with their views, MYD-08 — the search box is not remembered). The entry is the page's own remembered value with who and when beside it; the QMS-0025 column order (BKL-47) uses the same entries. | T | S | UC-14, UC-26 |
| UI-24 | Every table built on the shared table component (UI-07, UI-13) that is **wider than its box** shall keep a **horizontal scroll bar at the bottom of the screen, right above the app's sticky footer** (the footer's real height; the window's bottom edge where there is no sticky footer, e.g. on a phone), as wide as the table and in line with it, while the table is in view and its own bottom edge is below that line; once the table's bottom (with its own scroll bar) comes into view the sticky bar goes, so there are never two. The bar and the table scroll together both ways (the bar dragged; the table scrolled with a trackpad, Shift + wheel or the keyboard), and the bar follows every change of the table's size or place (columns shown or hidden, Wrap text, density, data loaded, the window resized, the side menu folded). It is hidden from screen readers and not in the Tab order (the table itself stays scrollable), follows the theme and high contrast, is not printed, and is not used for a table inside a pop-up or drawer (product owner, 2026-11-25). | T | S | UC-08, UC-09 |
| UI-25 | A table built on the shared table component (UI-07, UI-13) for which **Group by and Filter by column** are switched on (one option of the table, `groupFilter`; first the On-Demand Table pages, OND-132 — the other tables in KNOWN-ISSUES LIM-103) shall offer, in a row under its toolbar, **Group by** a column and, once grouped, **then by** for each further level — **up to four levels, outermost first**, a column used once, changing a level dropping the levels after it — as the retired QE Mapping did (QEM-06, QEM-11). Each **group row** shall show **▸ / ▾**, the **column, the value and the row count** ("3 rows"), indented by its level; groups in natural order (2 before 10, any case) with **"(blank)" last**; a value under two different parents is two groups. Grouping uses each value **as shown**: a Date as the table shows it, a Currency as its amount and currency ("1,200.00 EUR"); a column holding **several values** (Multi-select) puts the row **in the group of each of its values**, so the counts of one level can add up to more than the rows — the row is still one row (counted, selected and exported once). A column of one value per row (an ID, a key) is not offered. The rows inside a group keep the table's sort; **subtotals** per group are shown only on a table that shows a Total row (none of the tables with grouping today shows one). Everything else works on the grouped rows: a click or Enter opens the row (OND-27) with Previous / Next in the order shown, the selection's "every row shown", the row locks, "Showing the first N" / paging (counting data rows, not group rows), the sticky scroll bar (UI-24), and **Export**: the rows shown (UI-29), once each, in the order of the groups, with the columns shown — **no extra group columns**; a grouped-by column is exported even while hidden. About 2,000 rows stay quick to group and draw. | T | S | UC-08, UC-37 |
| UI-26 | Such a table shall offer **Filter by column** (a button in the same row; **Hide filters** clears the filters and hides the row): a **filter row** under the headings with a field per filterable column that keeps the rows whose value **as shown contains** the text, **in any case** (as the retired QEM-03); a column with a list of values (Dropdown, Multi-select) offers a **pick list** ("All" and its values) that keeps the rows **having that value** (any case; a Multi-select row having it among its values) — a text that is not one of the values (e.g. saved before the list changed) stays a "contains" filter, shown as "Contains “…”". The filters **add up**, and work **after the table's own search**; the table's count shows the rows left ("5 of 7 rows"), and while any filter is set the row says "**{n} of {m} rows match the column filters on {columns}** · **Clear column filters**", naming also a filtered column that is hidden, so that no row is hidden without saying so. With no row left the table shows "No row matches the column filters." and keeps its filter row. | T | S | UC-08, UC-37 |
| UI-27 | A grouped table shall let each group be **opened and closed on its own** (▸ / ▾, `aria-expanded`), and offer **Expand all** (every group at every level open) and **Collapse all** (every group at every level closed, so **only the outermost group rows** with their counts show), each **disabled when it has nothing to do** (as the retired QEM-10). Which groups are open is the viewer's **for this visit only** (not saved): the groups start **open**, or **closed** when the view for everyone says **Start collapsed** (UI-28); a new grouping, or Reset layout, starts again; a group stays open or closed while the filters change. | T | S | UC-08, UC-37 |
| UI-28 | The **grouping** (its levels) and the **column filters** shall be part of the table's view (UI-20…UI-23): the person's **own view** keeps them in their browser, and **Save view for everyone** saves them for everyone — on such a table Save view for everyone first opens a **pop-up** (eyebrow "VIEW FOR EVERYONE", title "Save view for everyone") naming what is saved (columns, order, widths, Wrap text, density and sort; "Group by: State → Assignee" or "no grouping"; the column filters or "none") with the option **Start collapsed** (off by default: the groups open expanded; disabled without grouping), then **Cancel** / **Save view for everyone**. Precedence as UI-21: a person who never changed the table sees the grouping, filters and Start collapsed of the view for everyone; their first change starts their own view from it, and **their own grouping and filters win** — "no grouping" and "no filters" included; **Start collapsed is always the view for everyone's** (a person opens and closes groups per visit, UI-27). **Reset layout** (and "Use the view for everyone") returns to the view for everyone. A view saved **before** these existed has no grouping or filters and keeps working: an own view without them takes them from the view for everyone. A grouped or filtered column that no longer exists is **ignored** (UI-22). Views read from Git or the browser keep only known shapes: at most four column ids for the grouping, filters as text of up to 200 characters per column id, Start collapsed only as true. | T | S | UC-08, UC-14, UC-37 |
| UI-29 | On a grouped table, **Export**, the selection's **"Select every row shown"** and **Previous / Next** in the row drawer — opened by a click or Enter on a row, or by a row's own button such as **Edit** — shall act only on the rows **actually visible**: the rows matching the search and column filters that are **not inside a closed group** (a group closed itself or under a closed group at any level), once each, in the order shown; a Multi-select row is visible while any of its groups is open (UI-25). **Opening a group brings its rows back** into all three. While rows are left out this way, the Export button's tooltip and the top of its menu say "**Exports the {n} rows shown ({m} rows in closed groups left out)**"; with every group open nothing is said. The table's count ("{n} of {m} rows") and the column-filter line still count every matching row, closed groups included (product owner, 2026-11-25: "Yes"). | T | S | UC-08, UC-37 |
| UI-30 | A pop-up for a **long list or a bulk action** — the DP and IOCC **Create Features** pop-ups (DPF-04, QV-59) — shall be **full size** but still a pop-up (`Modal` `full`, UI-STANDARDS §4): it fills the window but for a **20 px margin** in which the dimmed page shows, with **rounded corners**; its header (eyebrow, title, ×) stays at the top and its actions (Cancel, Create …) at the bottom, and **only the list between them scrolls** (its column headings stay at the top of the list). The keyboard and closing rules of every pop-up (UI-18, UI-19) and the dark theme are unchanged; at phone width (640 px or less) it takes the whole screen. At 1440 px the DP list with its date columns (DPF-14) fits without scrolling sideways. | T | M | UC-32 |
| UI-31 | The shared table selection (CAP-60's tick boxes, `DataTable` `selection`) shall let a table say **why a row cannot be ticked** (`blocked`): that row's tick box is **disabled**, with the reason as its tooltip and in its accessible name ("Select Feature 207 (not possible: …)"), and a row ticked earlier that became blocked is not part of the selection; the heading's **"Select every row shown"** ticks and unticks only the rows shown that can be ticked (UI-29: not those in closed groups). First used by My PI Review (PIR-36). | T | S | UC-40 |

### 5.21 Build and deployment (CI) — UC-19

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CI-01 | Every pull request to main shall run, on Windows: install with a frozen lockfile, lint, version check, unit tests, the web build, and the Windows installer package. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-02 | Every pull request to main shall build the macOS universal disk image. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-03 | A push to main shall publish the installers, their SHA256SUMS files and `latest.json` to the `desktop-latest` prerelease. | I, D | M | UC-19 |
| CI-04 | A push to main shall run the tests, build the web app, and deploy it to GitHub Pages. | I, D | M | UC-19 |
| CI-05 | The build shall fail when the package version is not `x.y.z`, or when a release tag does not equal the version. | T | M | UC-19 |
| CI-06 | An end-to-end suite (`pnpm test:e2e`: Playwright driving Chromium against the built web app served by `vite preview`, with no network — the synthetic sample and fixtures in `e2e/fixtures` as data) shall automate the UAT scenarios listed in SOFTWARE-COMPONENTS.md §1.10, each named after its test case; every pull request to main and every push to main shall run it and fail when a scenario fails. | T | M | UC-19 |
| CI-07 | CI jobs that run dependency code (install, lint, tests, build, packaging) shall hold a read-only token (`permissions: contents: read`) and shall not persist Git credentials in the checkout (`persist-credentials: false`); only publishing jobs that download already-built artefacts, and the job that writes the release commit (CI-11), which installs nothing and runs only the repository's own scripts with Node.js, shall hold `contents: write`, and workflow steps shall take their inputs from environment variables, not from `${{ }}` interpolation inside `run:`. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-08 | The repository shall keep a **Software Bill of Materials (SWBOM)** of every third-party component, generated from `pnpm-lock.yaml` by `pnpm swbom` (`scripts/swbom.mjs`) and never edited by hand: `docs/SWBOM.md` (on the Documentation page) and `docs/swbom.cdx.json` (CycloneDX 1.5 JSON). Each component has its exact version, licence, SHA-512 integrity hash (from the lockfile), whether package.json names it directly, and its scope — *runtime* (a dependency of package.json or anything it pulls in: shipped in the web and desktop app), *desktop runtime* (Electron) or *build / test* (not shipped). `pnpm test` shall fail when the SWBOM does not list exactly the lockfile's components and the product version; the daily package update regenerates it. | T | M | UC-20 |
| CI-09 | Every merged change shall get a semantic version — **major** for a change that breaks existing data, the shared setup files or the way users work, **minor** for a new or changed feature, **patch** for a fix, a dependency update or documentation only — and its entry (version, date, the changes with their requirement IDs and pull requests) at the top of `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md`. The pull request shall state these in its own change note `docs/changes/<name>.md` (front matter `bump:`, then the release-notes text) and shall not change the version in `package.json` itself: CI shall fail a pull request (other than Dependabot's) without a change note or with a changed version, and `pnpm test` shall fail on a malformed note, or when the newest version in the release notes is not the package version or the versions are not unique and in descending order with an entry each. The version and the entry are written by the release commit (CI-11); each version is tagged and released by CI (CI-10). Configuration items, identification, baselines, change control, release, status accounting and audits are described in `docs/CONFIGURATION-MANAGEMENT.md`. | T | M | UC-19, UC-20 |
| CI-10 | Every push to main whose Windows, macOS and end-to-end jobs pass shall tag the built release commit (CI-11) `vX.Y.Z` (the `package.json` version) and publish a GitHub release of that version with the Windows installer, the macOS disk image, their SHA256SUMS files and the version's entry from `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md` as its notes, without anyone tagging by hand; when the release of that version already exists it shall change nothing; a `vX.Y.Z` tag pushed by hand shall run the same job and fail when it does not equal the package version. The job that writes the release runs no dependency code (CI-07). | T, I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-11 | After every push to main, before anything is built, a job *Write release commit* shall take the change notes waiting in `docs/changes/` on the newest `main`: the highest bump gives the next version, which it writes to `package.json`; their texts, each followed by "PR #N." from the squash commit that added it, become that version's entry at the top of `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md`; it removes the notes, regenerates `docs/SWBOM.md`, `docs/swbom.cdx.json` and `docs/TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md`, and pushes one release commit to `main` (retrying when `main` moved meanwhile). Without notes it commits only regenerated documents that changed, or nothing. The other jobs of the run shall build, test, tag and publish that commit, and their tests shall fail when the committed matrix or SWBOM is not the generated one; a pull request need not commit them. Runs on `main` shall not be cancelled by a later merge. The job shall hold `contents: write` only for this push, install nothing and take its inputs from environment variables (CI-07). | T, I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-12 | Every pull request to main and every push to main shall also run the end-to-end suite (CI-06) with the page on a CPU four times slower (`E2E_CPU_SLOWDOWN=4`: Chromium's CPU throttling for every page the scenarios open), in its own job *End-to-end tests (slow machine)* with a read-only token, and fail when a scenario fails, so a step that passes only because the app is fast (a race between rendering, focus, loading and saving) is caught before users meet it. | T, I | S | UC-19 |

### 5.22 QE Mapping (QEM) — UC-23

*Retired:* the QE Mapping page was removed with its data (product owner, 25 Nov 2026) — see NAV-54, CON-117, MYD-30 and GIT-28; the QE of bulk Create Features comes from an On-Demand Table page (DPF-02, EPS-09). The rows are kept for the record.

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QEM-01 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* Data & governance → QE Mapping shall hold the QE Mapping as a shared table in Git (qe-mapping.json next to the planning data, one commit per save, conflicts refused). Access roles treat it as its own area (default: Admin, Product Owner, People Manager). Anyone whose Git token can save (not with sample data) may upload and change it; others see it read-only, and without Git the mapping last loaded in this browser is shown. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-02 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* "Upload Excel…" shall read an .xlsx workbook (or a CSV, comma / semicolon / tab separated) as it is, without the columns being known in advance: every non-empty sheet becomes a tab, its first non-empty row the column names (an empty name becomes "Column N", a repeated one "Name (2)"), every value text — shared, rich and inline strings, numbers, TRUE / FALSE, formula results, and date-formatted cells as YYYY-MM-DD (with HH:MM when they have a time); empty rows and trailing empty columns are dropped. An old .xls file is refused with "Old .xls files cannot be read …"; replacing an existing mapping asks first. The upload is a draft until Save to Git. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-03 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* The table shall be filterable: a search over all shown columns and, with "Filter by column", a filter per column (contains, any case); the count of matching rows is shown. At most 300 rows are rendered at a time, with "Show 300 more". | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-04 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* With the right to change it, a cell shall be edited by clicking it (Enter or leaving the field keeps it, Escape cancels); "＋ Add row" adds an empty row at the top; rows are selected with check boxes (or all shown rows) and "Delete n rows" removes them after confirmation. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-05 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* Each column heading shall have a menu: Rename…, Hide, Move left / right, Group by this / Ungroup and Delete column… (with its values, after confirmation); "＋ Add column" adds a named column; names must be unique per tab. Rename, move, add and delete change the shared mapping; hiding is each viewer's own choice, kept in this browser per tab ("Columns (n of m)" lists every column to show or hide; at least one stays shown). | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-06 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* "Group by" (a column) shall show the rows in collapsible groups by that column's value ("(blank)" last), with each group's count; grouping and filters are for the current visit. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-07 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* "Export Excel" shall download every tab with all its columns and rows as .xlsx (header row bold); "Export CSV" the current tab's shown columns and filtered rows. A mapping over 900 KB is refused at Save to Git with a message, as GitHub reads larger files differently. Changes show the unsaved bar (description, Discard, Save to Git); "Reload from Git" asks before discarding them. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-08 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* The QE Mapping table shall be compact: tight cell padding and smaller text, headings on one line, columns as narrow as their content (long text wraps only with Wrap text on, QV-38), so that more columns fit on the screen. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-09 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* Each QE Mapping column shall be resizable by dragging the right edge of its heading (double-click resets that column); the widths shall be kept per tab and column in this browser, and "Reset widths" shall return every column to its automatic width. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-10 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* When the QE Mapping is grouped into two or more groups, "Collapse all" shall close every group at every level (only the outermost group rows with their counts stay) and "Expand all" shall open them all; each button shall be disabled when it has nothing to do. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-11 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* Grouping shall be hierarchical: up to four columns, outermost first ("Group by", then "then by" for each further level, or "Then group by this" / "Remove from grouping" in a column's menu). Each group shall show its column, value and row count, indented by its level, and be collapsible on its own; a value under two different parents is two groups. Changing a level drops the levels after it. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-12 | *(Retired — see NAV-54)* **＋ Add row** shall open a **pop-up** with one field per column of the tab; **Save** shall add the row (first in the table) and **save the QE Mapping to Git at once** (with any other unsaved change, which the pop-up says), with a commit message naming the row's QE; if Git refuses, the row stays as an unsaved change with the reason. The **QE Name** column (a column named "QE Name", "QE", or a QE person / owner / resource column) shall be chosen from the **Resources list** (everyone with Time Writing Tasks and everyone with a resource profile, Settings → Resources), in the pop-up and when editing its cells (a value outside the list is kept). | T | M | UC-11 |
| QEM-13 | *(Retired — see NAV-54; Replace… stays on the On-Demand pages, OND-100)* **Replace…** in the QE Mapping toolbar shall open a **pop-up** (UI-STANDARDS §4, eyebrow "QE MAPPING · {TAB}", title "Replace a value") that replaces one value of one column with another in **every row of the tab** (also rows the search or filters hide): **Column** — a select of the tab's columns, preselected with the column named "QE Mapping column with the QE" in an Epic's Feature setup (EPS) when the tab has it, else none; **Find** — a select of the column's distinct values with their row counts (most rows first; spellings that differ only in case or surrounding spaces counted as one), plus **Another value (type it)…**; **Replace with** — free text (required) with the column's values and the Resources list as suggestions; a cell **matches when it is the whole value, ignoring case and surrounding spaces**, and a cell already holding exactly the new value is not changed. A **preview** shall say "{N} rows will change" and list the first 10 (row number with the first other value, old value, new value); **Replace** (primary, disabled with the reason when nothing changes) and **Cancel**. Replace shall **save the QE Mapping to Git at once** (QEM-01's save, with any other unsaved change, which the pop-up says) with the commit "QE Mapping: replace “{X}” with “{Y}” in {column} ({tab}, {N} rows)" — the Git history is the QE Mapping's audit trail — and show the result as a notice with the commit; a refused save keeps the pop-up open with the reason. Replace… needs the right that edits the QE Mapping ("Edit the QE Mapping", Git storage that can save); it shall be **disabled with the reason** without Git storage, with a read-only token, with the synthetic sample, while **View as** is on, before the roles are loaded, without the right, or when the tab has no rows. | T | S | UC-23 |

### 5.23 Monthly pack (PCK) — UC-24

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PCK-01 | Pulse Check shall have a **Monthly pack** tab for the month before the header month (the month presented in the following month's call), showing the two slides as boxes, in this order: the **Performance Pulse** (Summary, Overall status, Key takeaways, Key next steps, Decision required, Risks, Proposed mitigation, Leadership support requested, KPI Pulse) and the **2x2** (Actions/Deliverables Realized, Observations/Challenges & Risks, Focus Areas for Next Months, Support Requested / Topics to Highlight). | D | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-02 | Settings → **Monthly pack** (shared settings) shall keep the pack setup in Git as monthly-pack-setup.json: the slide lines in order, each with a label, the app streams that feed it (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, S&S IT) and the Epics whose Features closed in the month count for it; and the KPI Pulse rows, each with a label and what it reads — an Epic metric (open items, items closed in the month, items closed this year, closed share), the actual utilisation of the month or the year, or a value typed per month. Without the file the default lines (Serviceability = S&S IT, Digital Platform = DP, Clinical Platform = CP, IOCC, PPU, Content, Tool validation) and KPI rows apply. Unknown metrics, duplicate ids and invalid Epic ids are dropped on reading. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-03 | Actions/Deliverables Realized shall list, per slide line with at least one entry, the line's releases of the month ("Line - release, release"): the completed releases of its streams (DP and IOCC per QV-32 / QV-36, the S&S IT export's releases of the month, the other streams' releases with documents approved in the month) and the titles of the Features closed in the month under its Epics. Key takeaways shall list the supported count per line ("Line = n"), where a stream's count is its Supported Releases (DP, IOCC) else its releases and an Epic's count its closed Features. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-04 | KPI Pulse shall show each row's value: for an Epic metric the count over every item below the Epic except Tasks, Epics and Removed items (closed = Closed Date, else a finished state by its last change); the utilisation rows as the actual utilisation (booked hours over available hours of the people with bookings) of the month or of January to the month; a typed row its value for the month, editable in the tab. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-05 | The pack shall generate: the capacity line "Capacity: n% utilization (Mon) – QE – n% (Mon)" (QE = people with the role QE) in Observations and, as "Stretched Resources", in Support Requested; the Summary sentence with the month's utilisation; the year-on-year line "YoY support change of ±n% overall – with ±n% in Line, … (Mon YTD vs. last year)" from the supported counts of January to the month against the same months of the previous year (left out when last year has no data); and, when the month's utilisation is 100% or more, the Risks line "Overload: n% actual utilization (QE n%)". | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-06 | Every other box shall be text typed per month, one line per bullet, kept in Git as monthly-pack/<YYYY-MM>.json with the typed KPI values — blank until typed, saved with "Save to Git" (a Git token that can write; refused in sample mode; a concurrent change is refused), and read when the month is chosen. Each box and each slide shall be copyable as text (generated lines first, then the typed lines, "• " bullets), and **Download .pptx** shall write both slides as a 16:9 PowerPoint file (one box per slide area, heading and bullets, no library) named pulse-check-<month>.pptx. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-07 | Every KPI Pulse row shall take a value typed for the month, also a calculated one (utilisation, Epic counts): the typed value overrides the calculation on the slide and in the .pptx, the calculated value stays shown next to it, and "Use calculated" clears the override. A typed utilisation (a number, with or without %) shall also replace the calculated one in the generated lines (Summary, the capacity line, Risks). The override is kept with the month's typed KPI values (PCK-06). | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-08 | Settings → Monthly pack shall take the team's PowerPoint template (.pptx, at most 10 MB, with a Performance Pulse slide holding KPI PULSE and a 2x2 slide holding "Actions/Deliverables Realized"; a file without them is refused, and boxes the template lacks are named) and keep it only in the Git data repository (Storage) as monthly-pack-template.pptx, never in the app's source; replace and remove it there (queued, GIT-17: a template cannot be merged, so the latest save by its time wins and an older replace or remove never overwrites a newer template — the panel says "Not replaced: a newer template saved by someone else is in Storage and is kept."); and a **Department name** (shared setup). Download .pptx shall then fill that template — page 1 the Performance Pulse, page 2 the 2x2 — changing only the text of its boxes, found by their labels and positions: title "<Department>\| Performance Pulse update" and "<Month YYYY> Update", Summary, Overall status (On Track / At Risk / Off Track chosen per month; **Not set** until one is chosen — the word "Not set", the symbol – and a grey circle, never a green On Track nobody judged (owner decision 3); chosen: the word, the symbol ✓ / ! / ✕ and the circle green / amber / red), Key takeaways, Key next steps, Decision required (first line as the headline, the rest below), Risks and Proposed mitigation, Leadership support requested, the KPI PULSE rows in order, and the four 2x2 boxes; text longer than its box shall shrink to fit; every other part of the file is kept, and the file properties shall name nobody. Without a template the plain layout is used, in the same page order. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-09 | Typing in a Monthly pack box shall not recount the data: the releases per line and month, the YoY figures and the calculated KPIs shall be kept while only the typed text or KPI values change, and the boxes shall take the keystrokes at once while the generated lines follow. | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-10 | Every typed box of the Monthly pack — the 2x2 (Actions/Deliverables Realized included) and the Performance Pulse, except Overall status and KPI Pulse — shall show its text as **bullets**: each generated bullet (from the data) and each typed bullet has a **+** that adds a new typed bullet **right below it**, and **+ Add bullet** adds one at the end of the box; a typed bullet is edited in place — its text wraps and the field grows with it, one line per bullet (v7.2.1) — **Enter** adds the next one below it, **✕** (or Backspace on an empty one) removes it; the new bullet takes the focus. The slides (.pptx, Copy) show the typed bullets in that place; a bullet placed below a generated bullet that is no longer there goes to the end of the box. The bullets are saved with the month's text in the Git data repository (monthly-pack/<month>.json; the place as `anchors`, only for boxes with a bullet below a generated one) on **Save to Git**; empty bullets are not saved; files without anchors keep their typed lines at the end. Without a Git token that can save, the bullets are read-only (no + and no fields). | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-11 | Actions/Deliverables Realized shall end with a **Capabilities (Projects & Services)** bullet, "<label> [n]: Feature, Feature, …": the **unique Features** under the Epics chosen in Settings → Monthly pack (Capabilities bullet: the label and several Epics, shared in Git with the pack setup) that have **actual hours in the month** — a month Time Writing Task of that month under the Feature with Completed Work above 0, from anyone; each Feature once, titles in order. No Epics chosen, or no Feature with hours: no bullet. Typed bullets can be added around it (PCK-10); it is in the .pptx and Copy like the other lines. | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-12 | **Key takeaways** shall show the Capabilities count (PCK-11) in its "Supported:" line, after the release lines, as "<label> = n" (e.g. "Supported: Content = 3; Capabilities (Projects & Services) = 5"), n being the number of unique Features in the Capabilities bullet; no Epics chosen or no Feature with hours: no count. It is in the .pptx and Copy like the other lines. | T | S | UC-24 |

### 5.24 Insights workspace (WSP) — UC-25

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WSP-01 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* The menu shall have an **Insights workspace** page under Data Insights, beside the Dashboard, which stays unchanged. The page shall read the header month and offer the Dashboard's Region, Role, Team member and Stream filters (people and hours; releases, baselines and the timeline are not filtered and say so) and two tabs, **Overview** and **Insights & monthly pack**, the tab remembered in the browser. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-02 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Overview shall show four KPI tiles with the change against the previous month: the planned utilisation of the month (Settings → Capacity bands, with planned and capacity hours and the actual utilisation), the releases completed in the month (every default stream, counted as in Pulse Check), the number of exceptions (per kind), and the **data completeness** — the share of the month's planned Tasks (Original Estimate > 0, outside absence, shared time and NPRA) that carry a recorded value, with the people without a region or role — followed by the workload and capacity trend of the twelve months to the header month and the completed releases cumulative from January, this year against the prior year (prior year left out when it has no data). | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-03 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* The **exception queue** shall list, high severity first, then baseline, capacity and missing kinds, then by impact: **Over 25% baseline** — a release whose booked hours exceed its current baseline (Baselines) by more than 25% (high at 50%); **Missing baseline** — an open release under a stream with an estimation rule but no scope; **Capacity risk** — a person planned at or above the overloaded band in the month (high at 20 points above it); each row with its stream, region, impact, severity, owner and target date, and chips to show one kind. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-04 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* **Timeline health** shall count the Features (not Removed) whose Target Date lies between the first day of the current month and six months ahead, per Epic and in total: completed (finished state), overdue (open, date gone), at risk (open, due within 30 days) and planned, with a stacked bar per Epic. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-05 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Insights & monthly pack shall show **evidence-based insight cards** computed by rules, not a language model, each with a tone (as expected, worth a look, needs attention, no data), a sentence, the filters and what was checked, a small bar chart and the source rows on demand: planned utilisation against the previous month and the same month last year; people planned over capacity; planned Tasks without recorded hours (elapsed and partial months); releases over their baseline; releases completed against the previous month and last year; streams resting on one person (80% or more of the year's recorded hours); Features past or near their target date. A card without data says so or is left out. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-06 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Each card with a finding shall offer **Add to monthly pack**: the line joins an insights queue kept in the browser per month, with removal; **Save to monthly pack** shall append the queued lines to the Key takeaways of the header month's Monthly pack in Git (PCK-06 rules: a writable token, refused in sample mode) and clear the queue. The tab shall also show a preview of the month's pack (key figures, the generated capacity, supported and YoY lines, the saved Key takeaways, next steps and observations) and the mock-up's suggested questions as buttons that open the section holding the evidence; there is no free-text question (LIM-09). | T | S | UC-25 |
| WSP-07 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* For now the Insights workspace shall be hidden: the menu shall not offer it (Data Insights keeps the Dashboard, Pulse Check and PI Review). Its code, rules and saved queue stay in the build so it can be shown again without rework; WSP-01…06 apply when it is shown. | T | S | UC-25 |

### 5.26 Historical hours (HIS) — UC-27

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HIS-01 | Settings shall have a **Historical hours** page for roles with "Manage shared settings": what is imported (source file, sheet, date and importer), the cut-off month, the rows, people and recorded hours counted, and how many Azure DevOps Time Writing Tasks before the cut-off are left out with their hours; **Upload Excel…** / **Replace from Excel…** and **Remove history** need Git storage and a token that can save. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-02 | An uploaded .xlsx (at most 30 MB, read in the browser) shall be read from its **TransformedData** sheet, else the first sheet whose heading row (within the first 10 rows) has a person (QA/QE), a **Month(Date)** and an **Expected** or **Actual** column; per row: person, month (a date or YYYY-MM), Epic No, Stream(Features), Project / Release Title, Service/Project/Release, Expected (planned hours) and Actual (recorded hours), headings matched in any case and extra columns ignored. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-03 | Only rows before the cut-off month (default **October 2026**) shall be imported; every other row shall be listed with its spreadsheet row number and reason — no person (#N/A, TBD, blank), no month, a month from the cut-off on, or no hours — never dropped silently; rows left out by design (a month from the cut-off on, no hours in the month) shall be counted apart from the **rows to check** (no person, no month, an unknown group), which are listed open. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-04 | The import shall be saved to the Git data repository as `history-hours.json` (never in the app's source; nothing written to Azure DevOps) and read from it by everyone with Git storage; read back, only known shapes are kept (person, month YYYY-MM, positive Epic ID, hours 0–1000, at most 20,000 rows) and an unreadable file counts as no history. Saving is queued (GIT-17): when someone else saved the file after it was read here, the import is merged into the file in Git by row (person, month, Feature, Epic, stream, activity, type and tag), the name choices by name and the import details (cut-off, source, sheet, who, when) as one entry — both imports are kept, the later one wins for a row or detail both changed, and rows from the resulting cut-off month on are left out; a newer file is never written over. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-05 | The report pages — My dashboard, the Team overview, Pulse Check and the Monthly pack — shall count, before the cut-off month, the imported hours only (Azure DevOps Time Writing Tasks of those months are left out, they stay in Azure DevOps) and, from the cut-off month on, Azure DevOps only; the imported rows count as Time Writing Tasks of their month (Expected = Original Estimate, Actual = Completed Work) under a Feature per activity and the row's Epic. Pages that write to Azure DevOps never see them. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-06 | Before saving, each name in the file shall be shown with its report name: matched automatically when exactly one name known from Azure DevOps or Settings → Resources has the same words in any order ("Ana Costa" = "Costa, Ana"), else kept as written (people who have left); each can be changed in a list. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-07 | Rows without an Epic No shall be listed per Stream name with their row count, and each stream can be assigned an Epic from the fetched data; an unassigned stream counts under its own name, an Epic No not in the fetched data under "Epic <number>". | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-08 | A new upload shall keep the earlier name and stream choices for the names and streams still in the file; replacing or removing the history shall be a single commit in Git (its history linked from the page). | T | C | UC-27 |
| HIS-09 | People who have left shall appear by name in the reports for the months of their history; their capacity comes from Settings → Resources (region, role, Joined / Left on) like everyone else's. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-10 | A **Feature ID** column (optional) shall place a row under that Feature when it is in the fetched Time Writing data, counting for the Feature's Epic whatever the Epic No says; rows whose Feature ID is not in the data, and rows whose Feature ID and Epic No disagree, shall be listed before saving, and a row without a usable Feature ID is placed by its Epic No, else its stream (HIS-07). | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-11 | Besides an upload from the computer, the page shall offer **From Git storage…**: the .xlsx files in the data repository's folder and its `history/` folder, read through Git storage without downloading them first (no other path is read). | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-12 | Imported holidays, leave and trainings shall count like Azure DevOps ones under the Feature or Epic a row is placed under (leave as absence, trainings as NPRA under the absence Epic), but each row only for its own person: a row under a holiday or common-activity Feature (shared time, CAP) adds its hours to that person's public holidays or reserved time, never summed and spread over everyone in the Feature's scope. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-13 | A group in the person column shall apply the row to each person in the group, like a regional holiday Feature (shared time): **All** — everyone; **All-BLR**, **All-NL**, **All-US** (also written with India, Netherlands, USA) — everyone in that region of Settings → Resources. Under the absence Epic (or a mapped holiday Feature) the hours are a public holiday, otherwise reserved common time (trainings included); a group is never listed as a person, and an unknown group ("All-XYZ") is listed as not imported. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-14 | Epic No and Feature No (also Feature ID) cells shall be read as Excel writes them — "37753", "37753.0", "#37753", or several separated by , ; / & (the first counts); empty, 0, TBD and N/A are none; any other text shall be listed with its row and column before saving. The streams still needing an Epic shall say why: rows whose Feature No is not in the fetched data, or rows with neither a Feature No nor an Epic No. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-15 | The file shall be read with every month; the cut-off month on the import check shall apply at once to every count and list, so a later cut-off (e.g. November) brings the rows of the months before it (October) back without uploading again; only rows before the cut-off are saved. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-16 | An optional **Tag** column shall give a row's tags (several separated by ";" or ",") to its Task like Azure DevOps tags, so "Training" or "NPRA" counts it as training / NPRA (CAP-32) — also under the absence Epic, where the other rows count as leave; the app's own tags ("History import", "Shared: …") cannot be set from it, and it is kept in history-hours.json (at most 80 characters). | T | M | UC-27 |

### 5.27 Time Writing and Testing (ACT) — UC-28

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### 5.28 Planning Overview (OVW) — UC-29

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OVW-01 | Under the **Testing** heading (ACT-01, Admin role only), a **Planning Overview** page shall show the header month for the team, read only, with **Region**, **Epic** (the Active Epics, without the absence Epic, with their IDs) and **Owner** filters applying to every figure, and **Clear filters**. | T | M | UC-29 |
| OVW-02 | **Planned utilization** shall be planned hours ÷ capacity of the people in scope for the month (the capacity model of CAP), with the change in percentage points against the month before. | T | M | UC-29 |
| OVW-03 | **Delivery** shall be the share of Features (not under the absence Epic) whose Target date falls in the month that are done (Closed, Resolved, Done or Completed), with "n of m Features due this month done" and the change against the month before. | T | M | UC-29 |
| OVW-04 | **Baseline exceptions** shall count the releases whose booked hours exceed their current baseline by more than 25% (BAS), with the number of open releases under a rule without a baseline; **Capacity risk** shall count the people planned over the overload threshold (Settings → Capacity) in the month. | T | M | UC-29 |
| OVW-05 | **Workload and capacity** shall chart, for the 12 months up to the header month, planned and actual hours (bars) against capacity (line) on one hours axis, with a legend, the month's figures on hover and the same figures as a table. | T | S | UC-29 |
| OVW-06 | The **Exception queue** shall list every exception — *Over 25% baseline* (impact = % over), *Missing baseline* and *Capacity risk* (impact = hours over capacity) — with item (ID linking to Azure DevOps), Epic, region, owner and Target date, filterable by type with counts. | T | M | UC-29 |
| OVW-07 | **Delivery health** shall show, for the six months from the header month, the Features by Target date per Epic — completed, open with the date ahead, overdue (open, date passed) — as totals and a bar per Epic with a legend. | T | S | UC-29 |
| OVW-08 | **Generate Monthly Pack** shall open Settings → Monthly pack (MPK), where the pack is set up and downloaded. | T | C | UC-29 |

| CVA-02 | Four tiles shall show **Capacity** (hours after leave and holidays, CAP), **Actual** (Completed Work booked), **Actual utilization** (actual ÷ capacity, with how many people are over, on track and under) and **Headroom** (capacity − actual; **Over capacity** when negative), each with the change against the month before. | T | M | UC-30 |

| CVA-04 | **By person** shall list everyone with capacity or hours in the month — region and role, capacity, actual, planned, % used with a bar and a status **Over** (above the overload threshold), **On track** or **Under** (below the available threshold, Settings → Capacity) — most used first. | T | M | UC-30 |
| CVA-05 | **Where the hours went** shall show the month's actual hours per Epic, largest first (up to ten). | T | S | UC-30 |
