### 5.30 KPI tracker (KPT) — UC-31

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KPT-01 | *(Hidden from the menu since v7.11.0 — the page stays in the build, not offered in the menu or Customize menu, until it is shown again.)* Under **Data Insights** (area Team insights), a **KPI tracker** page shall show the team's KPI tracker workbook read only: one sheet at a time (a Sheet choice when there are several), as a table that follows the table default (UI-12), with an export; above it where the data comes from — "Read live from SharePoint at …" or "Showing the copy saved in Git: read from SharePoint at … by …" — and the file's last change in SharePoint when known. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-02 | In the **desktop app**, the page shall take the SharePoint link of the .xlsx file (Copy path or a "/:x:/r/" link; https, a site under /sites/ or /teams/, no user name or password) and read it **read only**: one GET to SharePoint's REST API (…/_api/web/GetFileByServerRelativeUrl('…')/$value) in the app's own SharePoint session, at most 20 MB, the answer accepted only when it is an Excel file. **Sign in to SharePoint** opens the site in a sandboxed sign-in window (https only, no pop-ups) where the company sign-in is completed; it closes once the file can be read. No tenant or client ID and no password are kept by the app; **Sign out** clears the session. A sign-in page, a refusal (401 / 403), a missing file (404) or an unreachable host is reported with what to do. Nothing is ever written to SharePoint. In the browser the page says that reading live needs the desktop app. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-03 | A read by a role with **Manage shared settings** and a Git token that can write shall update the copy in the Git data repository (**kpi-tracker.json**, one commit per read, never a credential; queued and merged by sheet name with a read saved by someone else in between, the later read winning for a sheet both have — GIT-17): the link, the file name, when and by whom it was read, the file's last change, and every sheet with data as text cells — trailing empty rows and columns dropped, at most 20 sheets, 2,000 rows, 100 columns and 1,000 characters a cell (more is cut off and said so). Other people's reads are shown but not saved. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-04 | The page shall show the **copy in Git** whenever no live read was made in this visit — in the browser, without SharePoint access, or when SharePoint cannot be reached — checked when read (schema 1, link checked, cells as text within the limits); a sheet's first row with text is its header (blank headings become "Column N", repeated ones get " (2)"). Without Git storage it says to connect it. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-05 | A newer copy saved by someone else while the page is open shall be named "KPI tracker" in the **Newer data in Git** notice on the KPI tracker page (GIT-18); Load the newer data reads it again. | T | C | UC-31 |

### 5.31 DP releases: QE and bulk Features (DPF) — UC-32

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DPF-01 | *(Retired — see DPF-09)* The DP table (Release Details → DP) shall show each release’s **Release type** from its version x.y.z.a (the Release field, else the Title’s version; a leading "v" ignored, missing parts 0): **X.0.0.0 Major** and **x.y.0.0 Minor** use the **Release** set, **x.y.z.0 MLD** and **x.y.z.a Platform Update (PU)** use the **PU** set; any other version has no type. The sets are part of the Feature setup of **Epic 13946** (Settings → Feature setup), each a Feature DoD and default User Stories, built in as: Release — DoD "IQ/OQ is complete and Checklist is uploaded into the feature.", User Stories PRD, SFV, DT, IQ/OQ; PU — DoD "DT is finished.", User Stories SFV, DT, IQ/OQ (each "<milestone> : Review of Deliverables ({service} {version})"); editable and saved with the setup in Git. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-02 | The DP table shall show each release’s **QE** from the **QE page** — an On-Demand Table page and two of its columns chosen in the Feature setup of the DP bulk creation Epic (EPS-09; QV-59; the QE Mapping it replaced was removed, NAV-54): the row (not hidden) whose **Platform / Product** column names the release’s product (its PsName, else its Program) — exactly (ignoring case, spaces and punctuation), else as whole words either way — and its **QE** column as shown (a Text or Dropdown value; a built-in list value by the name the list has now, LST-09). IOCC uses the same rule on the release name (QV-59). Without the page and both columns, or when the page is not there, the column is empty and its tooltip says what to set. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-03 | The DP table shall show the release’s **Possible QEs**: the people assigned to the earlier releases of the same product (same PsName, lower version) in the DP data, newest release first, without the mapped QE and without the release’s own Assigned To, at most five. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-04 | Releases shall be ticked in the DP table (Select all shown, Clear) and **Create Features (n)** — for roles that may create work items for others — shall list for each its release name, version and Release Classification (DPF-08, in that order, DPF-12), Feature title (Program / Service / Release), User Stories, QE and email, or why it is skipped (a Feature on QMS-0025 matches it 100 %, DPF-05; the bulk Epic has no Feature setup, the QE page columns are not set or the page is not there, no QE on the QE page, or no email found for the QE on QMS-0025), with the PI / Sprint (chosen when several are current). Create Features shall be disabled with the reason while no DP bulk creation Epic is mapped (QV-59; none is built in). Create shall make, one after another as a background job, each Feature under the DP bulk creation Epic (QV-59) with the DoD and User Stories of its Release Classification value, else of the Epic's setup (DPF-07, DPF-09), the Time Writing story and the month Task, all assigned to the QE; the result names each Feature made and each one not made with the reason; a stop part-way keeps what was made (Complete children adds the rest). Writes go to dev.azure.com only, as every Create Feature. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-05 | In every bulk Create Features (DP, DPF-04; Content, CNB-01), a release whose QMS-0025 Feature matches it **100 %** (the board match of the release, QV-46 / dpBoardMatch) shall not be selectable — its tick is disabled, with a tooltip naming the Feature — and is left out of "Select all"; a Feature that matches above the match threshold but below 100 % shall be named in the pop-up ("a similar Feature #… (n %) exists") and the release is still created. | T | S | UC-32, UC-33 |
| DPF-06 | Every bulk **Create Features** pop-up (DP, IOCC — QV-59 — and Content, CNB-01) shall offer, on each release that will be created, a **drop-down of the person** it is assigned to (everyone with an email on the loaded QMS-0025 board), **prefilled** — DP and IOCC from the QE page (DPF-02; the name on the page shown under it), Content from "Assign all the Features to" (CNB-02) — and changeable per row. A release skipped only because the QE page gave no QE (or no email for it, or its columns are not set) shall be created once a person is picked; any other reason still skips it. The result lists each Feature with its person. | T | M | UC-32, UC-33 |
| DPF-07 | The Feature setup of every Epic shall keep, besides its default DoD and User Stories, **one set per Release Classification value** — a Definition of Done and User Stories — for the values of the Epic's own **Release Classification** List field (EPS-07); an Epic without one has no value tabs (DPF-09). Admin → Epic & Feature setup → Setup per Epic shall show the values as tabs after **Epic default** (a value with its own set marked ●), each with a switch "Own DoD and User Stories for {value}" (on: edit its DoD — empty uses the Epic's DoD — and stories; off: the Epic default), and the preview shall show the value chosen; the fixed Release / PU tabs are no longer shown. The value chosen when a Feature is created picks the set — its own, else the Epic's default (DPF-09: old Release / PU sets are no longer used) — in New Feature (changing the value offers the new set's DoD and stories) and in bulk creation (DPF-08). There are no built-in Release / PU sets (DPF-09). My own version without sets uses the shared ones. | T | M | UC-32, UC-07 |
| DPF-08 | The DP and IOCC bulk **Create Features** pop-ups shall show, on every release row, a **Release Classification drop-down** with the values of the bulk Epic's list (DPF-07), **prefilled** with the value detected from the release number — by the field's rules (DPF-10, DPF-11), else (no rules saved) the value named like the type read from the number (Major, Minor, MLD, Platform Update) when the list has it — changeable per row; the chosen value picks the row's DoD and User Stories (shown in the row) and is written into the Epic's own Release Classification field (filling a Block field). An Epic without its own field shows no drop-down and writes no Release Classification (DPF-09); a release is never skipped for want of a type. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-09 | There shall be **no built-in Release Classification and no built-in Release / PU (MLD) lists** anywhere in the app: no Release Classification drop-down with built-in values (New Feature — BKL-33 — and the DP / IOCC bulk pop-ups offer only the Epic's own list, else none); no **Release type** column on Release Details → DP; no built-in Release (PRD, SFV, DT, IQ/OQ) or PU (SFV, DT, IQ/OQ) sets for DP or IOCC bulk creation — a bulk Epic without a Feature setup skips its releases with "the … bulk Epic has no Feature setup"; the Release / PU sets saved in a Feature setup are no longer read (and are dropped at its next save); New Feature for an Epic without a setup offers no PRD / SFV / DT / IQOQ milestone stories and no PU / Full Release DoD (the DoD is typed; the Review of Deliverables story and Your own User Story remain); Content bulk writes no "Release Classification : NA" line. The type read from a release number only pre-fills a bulk row's value when the Epic's own list has that name (DPF-08), or by the rules a Tool Owner sets on the Epic's own field (DPF-10); the **Release Classification (detected)** column of the DP and IOCC release tables (DPF-12) shows only a value of the bulk Epic's own list, never a built-in type. | T | M | UC-32, UC-07 |
| DPF-10 | Admin → Epic & Feature setup → Setup per Epic shall offer, under an Epic's **Release Classification** List field, **Detect the Release Classification from the release number**: for **each value** of its list, ticks for the part of the release number that changes for it to apply — **x.0 — first number only** (x.0, x.0.0, x.0.0.0), **x.y — second number** (x.y, x.y.0, x.y.0.0), **x.y.z — third number**, **x.y.z.w — fourth number** — the deepest non-zero part deciding (e.g. Release ← x.0 and x.y, MLD ← x.y.z, PU ← x.y.z.w). A part may be given to **one value only**: a part ticked for two values is named ("x.y is given to Release and Minor: a part of the release number may decide one value only.") and the setup is not saved until fixed. **Fill with the usual rules** proposes x.0 → Major, x.y → Minor, x.y.z → MLD, x.y.z.w → Platform Update (or PU) for the values named so (disabled with the reason when none is); **Remove the rules** clears them. The rules are saved with the field in the shared epic-setup.json (`classRules`: value and parts) with one Save to Git, by roles allowed "Edit the Epic setup"; on read they are checked strictly (known parts only, each part for one value — the first keeps it — a value once, text capped, at most 50; only on a List field named Release Classification) and a file without them reads as before. **Without rules nothing changes**: the value named like the release type is pre-filled, as before (DPF-08). | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-11 | The DP and IOCC **Create Features** pop-ups (DPF-08) shall pre-fill each row's Release Classification with the value the bulk Epic's rules (DPF-10) give for the release number (DP: the Release field, else the Title's version; IOCC: the release number), with a leading "v" ignored and missing parts read as 0; a number that is not 1 to 4 numbers, a part without a rule, or a rule whose value is no longer in the list gives **no value** (the Epic's default set). Under the drop-down a hint says what was detected — "detected from 5.2.1", or "MLD detected from 5.2.1" once the person picked another value; the person can still change it per row. Without rules saved, the value named like the release type is pre-filled, as before. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-12 | **Release name → Version → Release Classification** shall be the order of these fields wherever they appear together: (a) **Setup per Epic → Fields & title** lists the Epic's fields with Release name fields (Release Name, Name) first, then Version fields (Version, Release Version, Release Number, Release), then the Release Classification, then the others as saved (the order shown; the Title and Description keep the saved order); (b) the DP and IOCC **Create Features** pop-ups show the columns **Release name, Version, Release Classification**, then Feature title, User Stories, QE and Result (DP: Start date, Target date and Time Writing Tasks after User Stories, DPF-14, DPF-16); (c) the DP and IOCC **release tables** show, after Select, **Release name, Version, Release Classification (detected)** as their first columns — DP: the PsName (else the Title without its version) and the Release field (else the Title's version); IOCC: the Release column is named Version — the classification detected by the rules of the mapped DP / IOCC bulk Epic (DPF-11; its tooltip says from which Epic), **—** without a bulk Epic or a value. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-13 | **DP bulk creation dates** (Admin → Stream options → Bulk creation Epics, under the bulk creation Epics) shall set which **date column of the DP query** gives a Feature made by DP **Create Features** its **Start date** — **"Start date from"**, default the **DRPE Date** (RPE, Release Planning Event, QV-26) — and its **Target date** — **"Target date from"**, default the **DDT Date** (DT, Design Transfer). Each offers the DP query's columns whose field name ends in "Date" (the fetched data's columns, else the query's SELECT) and always the four DP milestone dates (DRPE, DRPC, DRfH, DDT Date), shown by their field and team names; a column is kept by its field name and found with any prefix (Custom.DRPEDate), as QV-24 / QV-26 find the milestones. The panel shows the two columns in use ("— default" when so) and **Edit dates…** opens a pop-up with the two drop-downs and **Save to Git**. The setting is shared with the bulk creation Epics in planning-data.json (`releaseSourceSettings.dpBulkDates`), changed by the same right (*Stream options*), committed to Git straight away like a shared bulk Epic (QV-59) and audited; without a writable Git connection Edit dates… is disabled with the reason. A default is not stored; files without the setting, and values that are not field names (letters, digits, _ and ., up to 120), use the defaults. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-14 | The DP **Create Features** pop-up (DPF-04) shall read each release's **Start date** and **Target date** from the columns of DPF-13 as **dates only** — a plain YYYY-MM-DD as it is, an ISO date and time (Z or an offset) as its **UTC day**; any other text is not a date — and show them as the columns **Start date** and **Target date** of its list (with the column each comes from as their tooltip and in the text above the list). A release without the date, or with a value that is not a date, is still created, **without that date**, and its Result says so ("no DDT Date — Target date left empty", "DRPE Date “TBD” is not a date — Start date left empty"). Create sets the dates on the Feature (Azure DevOps Start Date and Target Date, as New Feature does). | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-15 | A release whose Start date (DPF-14) is **after** its Target date shall be **skipped** with the reason — "the Start date 2027-05-01 (DRPE Date) is after the Target date 2027-03-01 (DDT Date)" — and not created; the same day is allowed. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-16 | The DP **Create Features** pop-up shall offer one optional tick box, **not ticked by default**: **"Create Time Writing month Tasks from the Start date to the Target date"** (the product owner: "Create Month tasks based on the start date — but optional"). An optional tick box left unticked keeps the behaviour before, so no choice is pre-selected for the person (UI-STANDARDS §12). **Not ticked**: one month Task per Feature, for the current month, as before. **Ticked**: one month Task for every month from the **Start date's month** to the **Target date's month** — counted from the Start date, not from the current month. A month before the current one cannot get a new Task (New Feature's rule, BKL-31; its planning is past), so a row whose Start date is in an earlier month starts at the current month and says so in its **Time Writing Tasks** column ("from Nov 2026 (Start Sep 2026 is before this month)"); a row without a Start date starts at the current month ("from Nov 2026 (no Start date)"); without a Target date, or with one already past, only the first month (said on the row). The column shows each row's months ("3: December 2026 – February 2027"), a row with more than 12 is marked "n month Tasks: check the Target date" (no upper limit, LIM-141), and the total of Tasks is shown under the tick box. The Tasks are made by New Feature's path — the Feature, its stories and the first month's Task, then one Task per later month (each tagged ad-hoc when its planning has locked, its Original Estimate the setup's default) — and a failure part-way keeps what was made and says how to add the missing months; the result names the month Tasks per Feature. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-17 | **Create Feature on one DP release** (Release Details → DP, the row's QMS-0025 match column; the product owner: "It is not auto populating" — "It is applicable even for Create Feature, not just Bulk Create") shall open the New Feature form **prefilled by the rules of DP Create Features** (DPF-04): the **Parent Epic** is the **DP bulk creation Epic** (QV-59) when it is on the loaded board, with the line "The DP bulk creation Epic, as Create Features uses it." (else blank with the reason: none set, or not on the board) — an exception to BKL-23 for DP rows only; the **title** bulk gives (Program \| Service \| Release, or the Epic setup's title pattern, EPS-03); the **Release Classification** detected from the release number (DPF-11) and with it the DoD and User Stories of that value (DPF-07; the shared setup first, as bulk; the Review of Deliverables story not ticked, as bulk creates none); **Assigned to** the **QE from the QE page** (DPF-02) when it names someone on the loaded board — it wins over "Me" — else me with bulk's reason under the field (e.g. "No QE on the QE page for this product: assigned to you unless you choose"); the **Start date** and **Target date** from the DP date columns of DPF-13 read as in DPF-14 (UTC day, ISO only, a prefix allowed), a missing or unreadable date left empty with a short note under the field ("No DDT Date on this release"); and the **Time Writing months** by DPF-16 with the same optional tick box, **not ticked by default** (the current month only; ticked, every month from the Start date's month to the Target date's month, with bulk's notes on the months line). Every value can still be changed. A Start date after the Target date is prefilled as it is and the form's own check says what is wrong ("The Target date is before the Start Date.", the Target date's earliest day is the Start date) — the form's equivalent of bulk skipping the row (DPF-15); a release whose QMS-0025 Feature matches it 100 % (DPF-05) has no Create Feature button, and the form names a Feature bulk would name or skip. Only DP has the date columns, the QE page rule and the bulk plan: Create Feature on IOCC, CP, Content and Windchill releases is unchanged. | T | M | UC-32, UC-07 |
| DPF-18 | DP **Create Features** (bulk) and **Create Feature** on one DP release (DPF-17) shall follow **one set of rules**, kept in one place (the DP create plan used by both): the same DP release shall give the same Feature either way — title, parent Epic, Start and Target dates, assignee, Release Classification with its DoD and User Stories, Time Writing month Tasks and each User Story's Sprint (EPS-10) — when the person keeps what is prefilled. | T | M | UC-32 |

### 5.32 Content releases: bulk Features (CNB) — UC-33

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CNB-01 | The Content releases table (Release Details → Content) shall offer ticks (Select all shown, Clear) and **Create Features (n)** — for roles that may create work items for others — making for each selected release, one after another as a background job, a Feature titled like its Create Feature ("Content \| <Title>") under the **Content bulk creation Epic** (QV-59; none built in — without one mapped Create Features is disabled with the reason) (no Release Classification line, DPF-09), with the DoD **"All Documents are approved"**, one User Story **"Review of Deliverables - <Feature title>"** in the current Sprint, and the Time Writing story with the month Task of **4 h** Original Estimate; a release whose Feature on QMS-0025 matches it 100 % cannot be selected (DPF-05), one with no title or a title over 255 characters is skipped with the reason; the result names each Feature made and each one not made. Writes go to dev.azure.com only, as every Create Feature. | T | S | UC-33 |
| CNB-02 | The Create Features pop-up shall ask for **one person** all the Features, stories and Tasks are assigned to — a name from the loaded QMS-0025 board (offered as you type), "Name <email>" or an email — and show the email it resolves to; Create stays disabled without one. The PI / Sprint is the current one (chosen when several are current). | T | S | UC-33 |

### 5.33 Epic setup (EPS) — UC-34

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EPS-01 | **Admin → Epic setup** (replacing Master data, whose old links open it) shall keep, per Epic of the QMS-0025 board, the **Feature fields** of its new Features — each with a label (its placeholder {label}), a **type** (Text, List, Number, Date), for a List the shared **value list** it offers, **If empty** (Optional, Warn or Block), where it **goes** (Description, Title, or both) and an optional **on-prem field** — up to 12, in an order that can be changed, and an optional **title pattern** of the fields' placeholders (and {feature}). It shall keep **value lists** shared by every Epic (one value per line); a list used by a field cannot be removed. The setup is one file in Git (epic-setup.json), shared with everyone, saved in one commit with a description; the last one read is kept in the browser. Without Git storage, or for people without the permission, it is shown read-only. A preview shows the Title and Description of an example Feature. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-02 | **New Feature** under an Epic with an Epic setup shall ask for its fields in place of the Feature setup's title fields (with or without a Feature setup): a List field as a drop-down of its list, a Date as a date and a Number as a number, required fields marked. The Title shall follow the title pattern, else the Title fields joined with " \| ", else stay as typed when every field goes into the Description; the Description gets "Label : value" for each field that goes into it. An empty **Block** field shall stop the Feature being created, naming it; an empty **Warn** field shall be shown, and the Feature is created; a value not in its list is flagged. Admin → Feature setup shall say that the Epic setup's fields apply in place of its title fields. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-03 | **Bulk creation** (DP, IOCC, Content) shall apply the bulk Epic's Epic setup to each release: values from the release's on-prem items (each field's on-prem field); an empty **Block** field skips the release (shown before creating, with the field); an empty **Warn** field creates it and lists the field in the pop-up and the result; a title pattern sets the Title, with {feature} as the title the stream gives (without one, the stream's title is kept); the Description gets the field lines. | T | M | UC-34, UC-32, UC-33 |
| EPS-04 | Two permissions of Role rights (CON-94) shall control the Epic setup: **Edit the Epic setup** (fields, title pattern, Copy to Epics; by default Tool Owner, Admin and Scrum Master) and **Edit Epic setup value lists** (by default Tool Owner and Admin). An access.json saved before these existed gives them as by default; an older one to the roles that managed shared settings. | T | M | UC-34, UC-22 |
| EPS-05 | **Copy to Epics** shall give the chosen Epics the fields and title pattern of the Epic shown, replacing theirs (e.g. one stream's Epics split by leader), as part of the unsaved draft. | T | S | UC-34 |
| EPS-06 | A **Text** field of the Epic setup that names an **on-prem field** shall be a **drop-down** in New Feature of the distinct values of that field in the fetched on-prem data (the release source and every fetched view; case ignored, sorted, at most 500), prefilled from the release when created from one; a value already set but not in the data stays selectable. Until such data is fetched the field stays a text box saying so. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-07 | The Feature fields are set only in the Epic setup: the **title fields of the Feature setup** (CON-75, BKL-18) are no longer read, shown or saved — Admin → Feature setup shows the Epic setup's fields instead and offers **Remove the old title fields from Git**, which saves the shared setup (and the person's own) again without them. When an Epic's setup has a field named **Release Classification**, it is the only Release Classification New Feature asks for (there is no built-in picker, BKL-33). | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-08 | *(Retired — see CON-97)* Epic setup and Feature setup shall be **one Admin page, Epic & Feature setup**, with the sections **All Epic setups**, Feature fields per Epic, Value lists and Feature setup; older links to Feature setup or Epic setup open the right section. **All Epic setups** shall list every Epic with Feature fields or a Feature setup (shared or mine): the Epic (title from the board), its fields (with list / on-prem and Block / Warn), its title pattern and its Feature setup (Shared, Mine, number of User Stories, DoD), searchable, each with **Fields** and **Feature setup** opening that Epic in the section. | T | S | UC-34 |
| EPS-09 | **QE page** (QE Mapping removed, NAV-54): Setup per Epic shall show, for the **DP and IOCC bulk creation Epics** (QV-59), **QE from page** — a select of the On-Demand **Table** pages (not the dashboards) — **Column with the Platform / Product** (a Text, Long text, Dropdown or Multi-select column of that page) and **Column with the QE** (a Text or Dropdown column, also a Dropdown with a built-in list, LST), in place of the two QE Mapping columns. They are saved additively in the bulk Epic's Feature setup as `qePage: { pageId, matchColumn, qeColumn }` — **ids, not names**, so renaming a page column keeps the choice; all three are needed. No column is built in. While the setup names no page, the page named **Resource Mapping** (if there is one) is preselected and marked as the **default** ("Default: the page named “Resource Mapping”. Choose its columns to use it."). A setup written by an earlier version with only the QE Mapping column names (`qeColumns`) is read and **kept as written** (an older app version still uses it while `qe-mapping.json` exists, LIM-130); it is **not guessed** onto the page: a line says "The old QE Mapping columns (“…”, “…”) need choosing again on the QE page." | T | M | UC-34, UC-32 |
| EPS-10 | **Sprint from** (the product owner: "Set the Sprints for the User Stories based on the RPE, PRD, SFV, DT dates"): each User Story of an Epic's Feature setup (Setup per Epic → User Stories, the Epic default, each Release Classification value and All Epics) shall offer **Sprint from** — **None — the Sprint chosen** (the default) or a DP date column: **DRPE Date (RPE)**, **DRPC Date (PRD)**, **DRfH Date (SFV)**, **DDT Date (DT)** and the DP query's other …Date columns (DPF-13's list and matching: by field name, a prefix allowed). It is saved additively with the story in the Feature setup as `sprintFrom` (a field name, by its last part); an older setup without it is None; anything else is left out. It is edited with the Feature setup's own Save and rights. When a DP release's Feature is made — DP Create Features and Create Feature on a DP row (DPF-17, DPF-18) — a story with Sprint from is created in the **Sprint whose start and finish dates hold that date of the release** (UTC days, both days included), searched in the **whole iteration tree** (any PI) among the Sprints the form and bulk may choose — current or future, team Sprints first (the PI / Sprint rule of New Feature) — so a story is **never put in a finished Sprint**. Without the date, with a date that is not one, with a date in a finished Sprint or in no Sprint, the story keeps the **default Sprint** (the PI / Sprint chosen) and the note says why: "SFV: no DRfH Date — default Sprint", "PRD 12 Mar 2026 is in a past Sprint — default Sprint", "… no Sprint holds it — default Sprint". The bulk pop-up shows each row's story Sprints in its User Stories column ("SFV → Sprint 3 · PRD → default Sprint") with the notes; the New Feature form shows each story's Sprint or note next to it. The Feature keeps its PI and the Time Writing story and Tasks are unchanged; IOCC, Content and the New Feature form outside a DP row ignore Sprint from. | T | M | UC-34, UC-32 |

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |

| ADM-01 | **Admin → Overview** (first in the Admin menu, for everyone) shall list the setup health: the open steps of Get started (required ones as To do, optional ones as Optional) and the shared-setup checks — shared changes not yet in Git, a Git token that cannot save, no access roles, no Tool Owner, no IOCC bulk creation Epic, no Epic setup, no shared Feature setup — required first, each with a link that opens the page and section that fixes it; nothing is changed from the Overview itself. | T | S | UC-35 |
| ADM-02 | Shared planning-data changes not yet saved to Git shall be shown on the Overview with **Save to Git** (the publish tray) and as a count next to the **ADMIN** heading of the menu until saved; shared changes keep saving as today. | T | S | UC-35 |

It shall also list for **Attention** (not Optional: that stream's Create Features is disabled) each bulk stream — DP, IOCC and Content — that has **no bulk creation Epic** mapped (none is built in, QV-59), as “{stream} bulk creation Epic is not set”, linking to Admin → Stream options → Bulk creation Epics. Only the shared file is judged: a person's own setup does not hide a gap others have. | T | S | UC-35 |

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |

It shall be searchable, show where each template is linked in the saved Feature setup, and add, edit and remove templates (a linked one after confirmation) for roles allowed "Edit Epic setup value lists and User Story templates" with a Git token that can save; others see it read only. | T | M | UC-36 |
| STT-02 | In Setup per Epic, the User Stories (of the Epic default, a Release Classification value — DPF-07 — or All Epics) shall offer **＋ Add from templates**: first the templates whose tags fit the Epic and the value shown (Epic tags empty or naming the Epic; value tags empty, or naming the value on a value tab), then the other templates. A picked story stays **linked**: it is shown with a Template mark and its description, and New Feature (ticked by default) and the DP / IOCC bulk creation use the template's **current** title and description; **Unlink** keeps a copy that no longer follows it; a removed template leaves the copy saved with the story. **Save as template** on a story of the setup makes it a template (tagged with the Epic and the value shown; none on All Epics) and links the story. A story's description can be edited in the setup. | T | M | UC-36, UC-07 |
| STT-03 | The User Story templates shall be shown as a **list** (the shared table, UI-STANDARDS §5), not as cards: one row per template with **Title**, **Description** (two lines at most), **Epics** ("Any Epic" without a tag), **Release Classification** ("Any value"), **Linked in** (how many places of the saved Feature setup link it) and, for those who may edit, **Actions** — **Edit** and **Remove**. Search, sort, the row count and Export come with the table. **Edit** and **＋ New template** open the template in a pop-up (title required; description optional; Epic and Release Classification tags), whose **Save** puts it in the unsaved changes that **Save to Git** then commits. | T | S | UC-36 |

### 5.36 On-Demand pages (OND) — UC-37

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OND-01 | The menu shall have a group **On-Demand** that lists every On-Demand page placed there by its name (in the order of the menu layout, then in name order, PGB-04; a page may be placed in another group, PGB-02), for viewers whose roles include the menu area **On-Demand pages** (CON-103); Home shall have an **On-Demand** tile with a link to each page. The top bar shall show the open page's name, with its description as the title's tooltip. The last page opened is remembered in this browser. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-02 | **CoNQ** shall exist from the start: while the Git data repository has no On-Demand page (and none was read here before), the menu shall offer **CoNQ** with the description **Cost of Non-Quality**, saying "CoNQ is not in Git yet: it is created with its first save to Git."; its first save (its columns, OND-30, or a row, OND-28) creates it. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-03 | A page shall be a work table in the shared table (`DataTable` in a `TablePanel`, UI-STANDARDS §5): one column per configured column in their order, then **Actions** (**Edit**, which opens the row drawer for editing, OND-27, OND-29; **Hide** / **Show**, saved at once, OND-39); a search over all columns; sort by any column (a Number column as numbers, right-aligned); the count "{shown} of {total} rows"; the viewer's own column choice, order and widths; and **Export** (Excel or CSV, with "Export tables"). A page without columns shows "No columns yet." and what to do. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-04 | *(Retired — see OND-27, OND-29)* Rows shall be added and edited in a **row pop-up** (one way for every row, UI-STANDARDS §3/§4): **＋ Add row** and **Edit** open a field per column by its type — Text (one line, up to 2,000 characters), Long text (a paragraph, OND-22), Number, Currency (an amount and its currency, OND-23), Date, Dropdown (a list of its options) and Multi-select (a tick per option), each with its column's description under it (OND-25); a required column must be filled, the others are labelled "(optional)"; a Number or Date value that is not one is refused, and a row with no value at all is refused, with the reason above the buttons (**Cancel**, **Add row** / **Save**). **Remove** takes a row out. Each change is an unsaved change of the page (OND-09). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-05 | **Set up columns…** (with "Set up On-Demand pages") shall open a pop-up to **add, rename, reorder (↑ ↓) and remove** columns and set each column's **type** — Text, Long text, Number, Currency (with its default currency), Date, Dropdown (one value), Multi-select — whether it is **Required** and its optional **Description** (OND-25); a Dropdown or Multi-select column has an **option list** whose options are added, renamed, reordered and removed there. Column names must be unique (any case) and given; a Dropdown or Multi-select needs at least one option, without blanks or repeats; at most 60 columns and 200 options a column. The column list is a shared editor (`ColumnListEditor`) that the PI Review form setup uses too (PIR-22). Two more types take their choices from a built-in list instead of options of their own: **Dropdown (built-in list)** and **Multi-select (built-in list)** (LST-08). | T | M | UC-37, UC-41 |
| OND-06 | A change of the columns shall not silently destroy values. When it touches values already in the rows, **Save** first lists what happens and asks (**Back**, **Save columns**): a **type change keeps the values** (a Multi-select value of several choices becomes one text "a, b"; a text becomes a one-choice list), and values that do not fit the new type are kept and shown as **"not a number"**, **"not a date"** or **"not in the list"**; a **renamed option** is renamed in every row; a **removed option**'s values are kept and shown as "not in the list"; a **removed column** goes with its values (named with their number). A kept value that does not fit stays marked in the table and in the row drawer until it is changed. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-07 | Everyone whose roles include the area sees the pages. Changing rows (＋ Add row, Edit in the row drawer, Hide / Show, OND-39) needs **"Edit On-Demand data (rows)"**; Set up columns… needs **"Set up On-Demand pages (pages, columns, options)"** (CON-103) — or a co-ownership of the page or its group (PGB-09, PGB-10) — and ＋ Add page…, Rename page…, Hide page / Show page (PGB-19) and Delete page… (the Tool Owner only, PGB-20) are changes of the menu (PGB-05, PGB-07: a Tool Owner, a co-owner of the page's group, or in the On-Demand group the setup right); both also need Git storage with a token that can save and no synthetic sample ("Synthetic sample data is loaded. Saving to Git is disabled."). Without them the buttons are **disabled, not hidden**, with the reason as a visible line above the table (and as each button's tooltip): the missing permission and where an Admin changes it, or, before the roles file is loaded (CON-101), "Read only: the access roles are not loaded yet, so nothing can be changed on this page."; or the Git reason as a notice. | T | M | UC-37, UC-22 |
| OND-08 | Each page shall be **one JSON file** in the Git data repository, `on-demand/<page name>--<page-id>.json` next to the planning data (a page created from this version on, named after the page, OND-120; a page that existed before keeps `on-demand/<page-id>.json` for good, also when renamed, OND-133) (schema 1, kind `on-demand-page`: id, name, description, columns with id, name, type, options and required, rows with id and the values by column id — a Multi-select value a list); the menu lists the folder (only files named `<page-id>.json` or `<name>--<page-id>.json`, a page id of lower-case letters, digits and hyphens, OND-122). Everything read back shall be validated: a file that is not a page is reported and not shown; an unknown type becomes Text; columns and rows without a valid or with a repeated id, values of unknown columns and values that are not text are dropped; sizes are capped (60 columns, 200 options, 5,000 rows, 2,000 characters a value); the id in the file name wins over the id inside. A page over 900 KB is not saved (a page converted to row files, OND-31, has no such limit). The pages last read are kept in this browser and shown while Git storage is not connected. No token is stored; the only personal data beyond what users type into the table are the work emails (or names) of the people who added and changed rows (OND-20, OND-21). Before any page save or file move, GitHub must confirm the repository is private. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-09 | *(Retired — see OND-28, OND-30)* Changes shall be saved with an explicit **Save to Git**, as on QE Mapping: an **unsaved-changes bar** per page ("Unsaved changes to {page} — not yet in Git", an optional description, **Discard**, **Save to Git**); the unsaved changes of each page are kept while the viewer moves between pages. Saves are the **queued, merged saves** of GIT-17: queued per file with their time (to the second), the file read again before each write and, when someone else saved it meanwhile, **merged** — the name and description as one entry, **columns and rows by their id**; where both saves changed the same entry the **later save wins**, also between a change and a removal (a removed row stays removed unless it was changed later); the column order follows the save that changed it; columns new on either side are kept. The result ("Saved to Git." / "… merged with a change by someone else.") is a **Background jobs** entry (UI-STANDARDS §8); a failed save keeps the changes, says why in the bar and in Background jobs, and a save kept in the queue offers Retry. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-10 | With "Set up On-Demand pages" (in the On-Demand group; elsewhere as PGB-05…PGB-07): **＋ Add page…** opens the Add page pop-up of PGB-05 (type, name, optional description, icon, group) and creates the blank page in Git at once (its id made from the name, unique), then opens it; **Rename page…** changes the name and description, saved to Git at once (**Save to Git**, OND-30); **Hide page** / **Show page** (PGB-19) takes it out of the menu and back; **Delete page…** — the **Tool Owner only** (PGB-20) — asks first in a pop-up naming the page, how many rows and columns go with it and that its file (a row-file page: its folder, OND-31) is deleted from Git (its history stays in GitHub), then deletes it. While no page is left, CoNQ is offered again (OND-02). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-11 | Every On-Demand page shall have two tabs, **Data** (the table of OND-03…OND-07, unchanged) and **Insights ({n})**, in the shared result tabs (`ResultTabs`, UI-STANDARDS §7), the chosen tab remembered per page in this browser tab. The Insights tab shall show a heading panel (**Insights**, ⓘ with how many charts and tables and rows, **Filters…** and **＋ Add chart / table** on the same line, the save state of the page of OND-30) and then each insight in its own panel in the order set: the **title** and ⓘ (no type above it since CHT-39; the type is ⓘ's first line) — which lists what is measured and how it is split ("Sum of Cost (EUR) by Category, stacked by Sites"), its own filters ("Category: Scrap, Rework · Found on: Jan 2026 – Jun 2026", or "All rows"), the filter row's effect, "{used} of {all} rows counted" (with how many match the filters and how many were left out for lack of a number or date), the currency and its counting rules (CHT-25) — then the chart or table. A page without rows says "No rows to show." | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-12 | Four kinds of insight: **Bar / column** — horizontal bars, a value per category, optionally **stacked** by a Dropdown or Multi-select column (at most 5 stack colours, the rest as "Other"); **Line over time** — a value per **month** of a Date column, every month from the first to the last (also months without rows), optionally one line per value of a Dropdown or Multi-select column (5 + "Other"); **Pie / donut** — each category's share of the sum of the slices, with more than 5 categories the 5 largest and **"Other"** (with "(none)"), so at most 6 slices; **Summary table** (called **Pivot table** since CHT-74, which adds nested rows and columns) — a pivot: rows by one field, optional columns by another, cells the measure, a **Total** column and row. Charts shall be drawn in the app's chart frame (`ChartFrame`, UI-STANDARDS §9) with legend, **Data labels** box, **Table / Chart** toggle (the Table view lists categories × series with totals; a pie also the share in %) and axis names; the value axis starts at zero (to scale; below zero shown when a sum is negative); the colours are the `--viz-series-*` tokens in light and dark mode, "(none)" and "Other" the neutral `--viz-other`; a keyboard and pointer readout per category or month. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-13 | The measure of an insight shall be **Count of rows**, **Sum** or **Average** of a Number column. Categories: a **Dropdown** by its options, a **Multi-select** by each of its values — **a row counts once in each of its values** — a **Text** by its text, a **Date** by month. A value that is empty, **"not in the list"** or not a valid date goes to **"(none)"**; a Sum or Average leaves out rows without a number, and a line rows without a date, and says how many. Totals (of a category, a series and overall) take **each row once**, so with a Multi-select field the parts can add up to more than the total — stated in ⓘ and under a summary table. An Average is the average of the rows of the cell (an empty cell "—"); Average is not offered for a pie or a stacked bar ("Stacked bars add their parts up…"). Order: **largest first** by default for bars, pies and tables, or by option order / month / name; a line always by time; "(none)" and "Other" last. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-14 | Each insight shall have **filters** of its own: on a Dropdown or Multi-select column a set of its values (and "(none)"), a row passing with any of them; on a Date column a range **from / to month** (either open), a row without a valid date failing it; all filters apply together. The filters shall be stated under the insight's title. A filter with nothing chosen, or ending before it starts, is refused in the pop-up with the reason. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-15 | **＋ Add chart / table** and **Edit** shall open a pop-up (UI-STANDARDS §4, eyebrow "ON-DEMAND · {PAGE}") with **Title** (required), **Type**, the category field ("Categories by" / "Months of" / "Rows by", only columns that fit), the optional second field ("Stacked by" / "One line per" / "Columns by"), **Measure** and its Number column, **Sort** and **Filters** (＋ Add filter, ✕), and a **live preview** of the chart or table from the page's rows; **Add chart** / **Save** saves it to Git at once, as do ↑ ↓ and Remove (OND-30). Each insight has a **⋯** menu with **Move up**, **Move down**, **Edit…**, **Size…** and **Remove…** (which asks first in a pop-up naming the chart) (OND-61). Adding, editing, reordering, sizing and removing need **"Set up On-Demand pages"** (CON-103) and Git storage that can save; everyone with the area sees the insights. Without them **＋ Add chart / table** and **Filters…** are disabled, not hidden, with the reason as a visible line on the Insights tab and as the button's tooltip (CON-102); the charts show no ⋯ menu (OND-61, decided by the product owner). A page without columns says to set them up first. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-16 | The insights shall be saved **in the page's file**, `on-demand/<page-id>.json`, as a list `insights` (each: id, title, type `bar` / `line` / `pie` / `table`, category column id, optional second column id, measure `count` / `sum` / `average` with its Number column id, filters with column id and values or from / to month, sort `value` / `label`), written only when the page has insights (the file format otherwise unchanged, schema 1). Read back, they shall be validated: an unknown type, a bad or repeated id is dropped; an unknown measure becomes Count; bad months and empty filters are dropped; titles, values and sizes are capped (40 insights, 10 filters); a reference to a column that is not on the page is **kept** and shown as broken (OND-17). Two saves shall be merged as the rest of the page (OND-28, GIT-17): **insights by id**, the later save winning for an insight both changed (also a change against a removal), their order following the save that changed it. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-17 | A column used by an insight is referred to by its id: a **renamed** column is followed (the new name shows); when a column is **removed**, or its type no longer fits (e.g. the stack column made Text), the insight shall show **"This chart cannot be shown:"** with what is wrong ("The column for the categories is no longer on the page (removed).", "“Sites” for the stacks is now a Text column; it needs Dropdown or Multi-select.") and that it can be edited or removed, instead of failing; the other insights and the page still work. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-18 | Every row of an On-Demand page shall have a **key**, made in the browser the moment the row is added (when the row drawer saves it, OND-28, before the file is read again and written) and never changed: the page's **key prefix**, the date (UTC, yymmdd) and **4 random characters** from 31 that cannot be mistaken for each other, e.g. `CONQ-261123-K7Q2`. It shall be unique within the page although rows are added by several people saving on their own (asynchronously): a new key is checked against the page's keys, and a key that still appears twice after two saves are merged is replaced for the second row on the next save. The key is the first column (**Key**), searchable, sortable and exported, and shown in the row drawer (OND-27). The **key prefix** is set per page in **Set up columns…** (1–8 letters or digits; CoNQ starts as `CONQ`, a new page from its name) and is fixed once a row has a key. Rows added before keys get theirs, in page order, on the next save of the page. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-19 | Every row of an On-Demand page shall have a readable **ID**, the page's key prefix and a **sequential number** of at least 4 digits, e.g. `CONQ-0001`, `CONQ-0002` (more digits after 9999). The number is **given when the row is saved to Git** (OND-28) — a row from before IDs shows "on save" until its page is next saved — as the **next free number** (the page's highest + 1, in row order), and **never changes** afterwards; a removed row's number is not given again. It shall stay unique when several people add rows and save on their own (GIT-17): numbers are given when the file is written (also the merged file written after the re-read), a number already in Git keeps its row, and a number that a merge still leaves on two rows is dropped from the second when the file is read and that row gets the next free number on the next save. Rows saved before IDs get theirs, in page order, on the next save. The ID is the first column (**ID**, sorted by number, searchable, exported) and is shown in the row drawer (its title, OND-27); the random key of OND-18 stays as the row's instant unique key. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-20 | When a row is added, it shall record **Added by** — the viewer's work email (CON-82), else their name, else "unknown" — and **Added on**, the time in UTC (ISO); both are set once and never changed, and shown as the columns **Added by** and **Added on** (the time in the viewer's time zone, NAV-51) and in the row drawer ("Added {time} by {email}", OND-27). Rows added before keep them empty ("—"). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-21 | Every row shall keep an **update log**: adding the row is the first entry ("Added", with its values), and **every saved edit that changes a value** adds an entry automatically — **who** (as OND-20), **when** (UTC) and **what changed**, per field its old → new value — with no comment to type (owner decision). Saving the row without a change adds nothing (and writes nothing). The log shall be shown as **History** in the row drawer, newest first (OND-27) ("Changed · {time} · {email}", "Cost: 1,200.00 USD → 1,500.00 EUR"); the columns **Last updated by** and **Last updated on** (the newest entry) are offered hidden at first in the table's Columns list and are always exported. The log keeps the **last 100 entries** of a row, each old / new value cut to 500 characters, and is not part of the table export. When two saves change the same row, the later save wins for the values (OND-28) and the **logs are merged** (the union of both by entry id, in time order) so no entry is lost. A `comment` found on an entry in the file is read and shown; the app writes one only for **Replace…** ("Replaced “X” with “Y” in {column}", OND-101). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-22 | A column may be of type **Long text**: a paragraph of up to **10,000 characters** (line breaks kept), entered in a text area in the row drawer with the count of characters, shown in the table cut to **3 lines** with the full text as the cell's tooltip and in full in the row drawer, and exported in full. Every value is read back up to 10,000 characters, so a Long text column changed to Text keeps its text and marks a value longer than a Text allows ("longer than a Text", OND-06). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-23 | A column may be of type **Currency** with a **default currency**: an ISO 4217 code chosen from a list (EUR, USD, GBP, INR, CHF, JPY, CNY …) or another 3-letter code typed in (**Other code…**). Each cell holds an **amount and its currency**, the column's default to start with, entered as an amount field and a currency list in the row drawer; it is kept as "1200.5 EUR" (an amount without a code counts in the column's default), shown and exported as **"1,200.00 EUR"** (the currency's decimals) and right-aligned, sorted by amount. A value that is not an amount is refused in the row drawer and, after a type change, kept and shown "not an amount"; type changes to and from Currency follow OND-06. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-24 | In the Insights (OND-11…17), **Sum** and **Average** shall also take a Currency column, **without exchange-rate conversion**: a chart or table shows **one currency at a time** — the currency of most rows first and, when the rows hold several, a **Currency** choice above the chart (for the viewer, not saved) — and leaves the rows in other currencies out; the measure names the currency ("Sum of Cost (EUR)") and the "rows counted" line says which currencies were found and how many rows each ("Currencies found: EUR (3 rows), USD (1 row); shown: EUR — amounts in different currencies are not added up."), and how many rows in other currencies were left out. Count of rows is not split. (One currency at a time rather than a series per currency, so that no bar stack, pie or total ever adds amounts of different currencies.) | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-25 | Each column may have an optional **Description** — how or what to fill, up to **300 characters** — edited in **Set up columns…**, shown as help under the field — and under the column's name when the row is viewed — in the row drawer (UI-STANDARDS §3) and behind **ⓘ** next to the column's heading in the table. | T | S | UC-37 |
| OND-26 | The page file (OND-08) shall stay **schema 1** and readable by and from older files: the new fields are optional — a column's `description` and, for a Currency column, `currency`; a row's `seq`, `addedBy`, `addedAt` and `log` (entries `{id, at, by, added?, comment?, changes: [{column, from, to}]}`) — and are validated when read (a number that is not a whole number 1…9,999,999 or repeats is dropped; a time that is not an ISO time is dropped; log entries without a valid id or time, or repeated, are dropped; texts and sizes capped). Changing the columns keeps every row's key, ID, Added by / on and log. An app version from before these fields drops them when it saves the page (LIM-19). | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-27 | A click on a row of an On-Demand page's table (CoNQ, every Table and Table + Insights page) — or **Enter** / **Space** on the row, which can be reached with Tab — shall open the row in a **drawer from the right** (a shared `Drawer`, about 520 px wide; the full width on a phone): its title is the row's readable **ID** (OND-19; "New row" for ＋ Add row), with the page as eyebrow, **‹ ›** (**Previous row** / **Next row**, in the table's current sort and search order, with "{n} of {m}") and **×**; its body shows the row's **ID**, **Key**, **Added** (time and who, OND-20), then every column's **name**, its **description** (OND-25) and the **value** as in the table (a Long text in full, a value that no longer fits marked, "—" when empty), then the row's **History** (OND-21, newest first); its footer stays at the bottom with **Hide row** / **Show row** (OND-39; **Delete row…** for the Tool Owner on a hidden row, OND-40) and **Edit**. A click on a button inside the row (Edit, Hide) does not open it. The drawer is a dialog named by its title: the focus moves into it and stays inside while it is open (Tab, Shift+Tab), **Escape**, **×** and a click on the overlay beside it close it, and the focus goes back to the row; it follows the light and dark theme and shows without sliding when the system asks for reduced motion. Without the right to edit rows (OND-07, PGB-09, PGB-10), before the roles are loaded (CON-101), or without Git storage that can save, **Edit** and **Hide row** are disabled, not hidden, with the reason as their tooltip and as a visible line in the footer (CON-102); the row is still shown. | T | M | UC-37, UC-22 |
| OND-28 | A row shall be **saved to Git at once**: the drawer's **Save to Git** (OND-29) writes the row's change — added or changed, with its log entry (OND-21) and, for a new row, who added it and when (OND-20) — to the page's file as one commit, "{page}: add row {ID} ({key})" / "{page}: update row {ID}"; a row is no longer removed by its editors: **Hide row** / **Show row** (OND-39) commits "{page}: hide row {ID}" / "{page}: show row {ID}", and only the Tool Owner deletes a hidden row ("{page}: delete row {ID}", OND-40). On a page of row files (OND-31) the same changes are written as OND-33 says. Each save is the **queued, merged save** of GIT-17, applied to the page as last read from or saved to Git: the file is read again before the write and, when someone else saved it meanwhile, merged — the name and description as one entry, **columns and rows by their id**; where both saves changed the same entry the **later save wins** (for a row its values; also between a change and a removal: a removed row stays removed unless it was changed later); the column order follows the save that changed it; columns new on either side are kept; the logs of a row are united (OND-21); the **sequential number** of a new row is given at the write, after the re-read (OND-19), so the row shows its ID once saved — and a save waiting behind another save of the same browser, or retried later, is merged from the version it was made from (KB-80). A conflict at write time reads, merges and tries again (at most 3 times, GIT-17). Progress and result are a **Background jobs** entry ("Save to Git · {page} · row {ID}": "Saving to Git…", then "Saved to Git. on-demand/{page}.json — updated {ID}" or the reason, UI-STANDARDS §8); while a save runs the drawer's buttons are disabled and it does not close. A failed save keeps the edit in the drawer with the reason and **Retry** (a save kept in the GIT-17 queue is retried from there); nothing typed is lost. A save without a change writes nothing. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-29 | With the right to edit rows, **Edit** in the row drawer shall turn the values into fields **in place** — a field per column by its type: Text (one line, up to 2,000 characters), Long text (a paragraph, OND-22), Number, Currency (an amount and its currency, OND-23), Date, Dropdown (a list of its options) and Multi-select (a tick per option), each with its column's description under it (OND-25); a required column must be filled, the others are labelled "(optional)"; a Number or Date value that is not one is refused, and a row with no value at all is refused, with the reason above the buttons — then **Cancel** (back to the row as saved) and **Save to Git** (OND-28). **＋ Add row** opens the drawer empty, for editing ("New row"; its ID and key are given when it is saved). Closing the drawer (×, Escape, a click beside it) or moving to another row (‹ ›) with **unsaved edits** asks first in a pop-up, "Discard your changes?" (**Keep editing**, **Discard changes**). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-30 | The other changes of a page shall also be **saved to Git at once**, each as its own queued, merged save (OND-28, GIT-17) with its Background jobs entry: **Set up columns…** on **Save** / **Save columns** (OND-05, OND-06; a failed save keeps the pop-up open with the reason), **Rename page…** on **Save to Git** (OND-10) and every **Insights** change — add, edit, ↑ ↓, remove (OND-15), shown at once while it is saved. There is **no unsaved-changes bar and no separate Save to Git step** for a page any more. Under the table and on the Insights tab the page shows its **save state**: "Waiting to save (N in queue)" while more than one save of the page waits, and a save kept after the retries with **Retry** (GIT-17); an Insights change whose save failed before it could be queued (e.g. no network) is **kept** in this browser and still shown — "Not saved to Git: the change to the charts and tables of {page}, kept here — {reason}" with **Discard** (asks first) and **Retry** — and **↻ Reload from Git** asks before discarding it. (Builds before this one kept a page's unsaved changes in memory only, never in the browser's storage, so no older draft is left to carry over.) | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-31 | A table page (types **Table** and **Table + Insights**, one predicate `hasTable`) may be kept as **one file per row** ("row files") in the page's folder, named as its file (OND-120: `on-demand/<name>--<page-id>/` for a page created from this version on, `on-demand/<page-id>/` for a page from before, OND-133; `<page-id>` below stands for either): `on-demand/<page-id>/page.json` — the page without rows (name, description, key prefix, columns, insights, colours; kind `on-demand-page`, `storage: "rows"`) — `on-demand/<page-id>/rows/<row-key>.json` — one row (id, key, ID number, added by / on, log, hidden, values) named by its key (OND-18) — and `on-demand/<page-id>/counter.json` — the next readable ID. A page is row files when its folder holds page.json; the single file of OND-08 stays readable. A row key is used as a file name **only when it is a key of OND-18** (capital letters, digits, two hyphens: no path outside the page's folder, SM-106); a file in the folder not named like a key is ignored and the file name wins over the key inside. **Reading**: the page's folder is listed (one call), page.json and the rows folder's entries are read side by side (one call each), and only the row files not read before in this visit (by their version) are fetched — in batches of 100 through GitHub's GraphQL API, 4 at a time, each one alone through the REST API where GraphQL is refused. Measured against a mocked GitHub with 40 ms a call: 500 rows open in about 0.2 s in 8 calls (one file: 0.08 s in 2 calls), 2,000 rows in about 0.35 s in 23 calls. A page of row files has no 900 KB limit (5,000 rows at most, as OND-08). The open-app check (GIT-18) watches page.json and the rows folder by their versions (one listing of the page's folder), never each row file. GitHub must confirm private repository visibility before writing row files or creating Git objects from them. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-32 | **Convert to row files…** — on the page and as **Convert…** in Admin → Pages & menu, for a table page still in one file — shall be offered to the **Tool Owner** only (disabled with the reason for others who may set the page up), ask first in a pop-up naming the page, its **rows** and that older app builds cannot open it afterwards (**Cancel**, **Convert {n} rows**), and write **one commit** (GitHub's Git Data API: the files in one tree, a commit on the branch's latest commit, the branch moved without force; when someone committed meanwhile it starts again from their commit, at most 3 times): page.json, a file per row, counter.json (the highest ID + 1) and, in place of `on-demand/<page-id>.json`, a marker `{kind: "on-demand-moved", moved: "on-demand/<page-id>/"}` that an older app reports as "is not an On-Demand page" and never parses or overwrites with rows. Keys, IDs, values, logs, columns, insights and colours stay identical; a row without a key or ID gets one, as its next save would. The page is then read from its folder; the result is a Background jobs entry. There is no automatic conversion and no way back in the app; a large page is suggested for it (OND-62). | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-33 | On a page of row files every change shall be written as what it is, queued per page (GIT-17) and stamped `[saved …]`: a **new row** takes its ID from counter.json with a version-checked update (refused when another person took a number meanwhile: read again and retried, up to 6 times — two rows added at the same moment never share an ID, an ID never changes, the counter only goes up and is never below the page's highest ID + 1) and is **created** as its own file (refused when the file exists — a key clash: the row gets a new key); a **changed row** writes only its own file (merged as OND-34); a row **hidden or shown** (OND-39) is a change of its file; a row **deleted** (OND-40) deletes its file; a change of the name, description, columns or charts writes page.json (merged as OND-09); a change that touches **many rows** (a renamed option or a type change of Set up columns…) is **one commit** of page.json and every changed row file, applied to the rows as they are at that commit. After each save the page is read again (only changed rows). Adding a row takes no lock. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-34 | Two saves of the **same row** of a page of row files shall be merged **field by field**: a value only one save changed takes that change; a value both changed takes the later save's (also a value one of them cleared); whether the row is hidden is one more field; the log is the union of both logs by entry id (OND-21); the key, ID and who added it come from the row in Git. With row locks (OND-35) this happens only when a lock expired or was released while someone was still editing (LIM-24). | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-35 | On a page of row files, **Edit** on a row shall take the row's **lock**: a file `locks/<page-id>/<row-key>.json` `{by, name, at, expires, session}` on the branch **`locks`** of the data repository (made from the data branch on the first Edit; where the token or the repository's rules refuse a new branch, in the folder `locks/` of the data branch, and the page says so). Creating the file is refused when it exists: when it is someone else's lock that has not expired, Edit stays disabled and the drawer says **"Being edited by {email} since {time}"** with the reason "{email} is editing {ID} since {time}. You can view it; Edit is free again when they save or cancel, or {n} minutes after their last activity." (CON-102); the table marks the row (✎ and the editor's name in its ID cell) and disables Edit and Hide for it. One's own lock (another window) and an expired lock are taken over with a version-checked update (two people cannot both win). After taking the lock Edit reads the page again so the edit starts from the row as it is in Git. Viewing, the other rows, **＋ Add row**, Insights, dashboards and exports never wait for a lock. The page's locks are listed once when the page opens (one folder listing), when the drawer opens a row and on the open-app check of GIT-18 — no polling of their own. Lock commits never raise "Newer data in Git" (GIT-18). Edit needs the viewer's work email (it names the editor); View as, a read-only token and the synthetic sample never take a lock. GitHub must confirm private repository visibility before a lock or lock branch mutation. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-36 | A lock shall be **renewed** while its holder is active (every 2 minutes, only when there was input in the drawer since the last renewal) and **released** on Save to Git, Cancel, closing the drawer or moving to another row and — best effort — when the page closes; its expiry covers a crash. After the **inactivity timeout** — **15 minutes** unless changed — a warning shows in the drawer 2 minutes before (half the timeout when it is shorter than 4 minutes), as a polite live region: "No activity: your changes to {ID} will be discarded in m:ss and kept as a draft in this browser for one day." with **Keep editing**; at the timeout the edit closes, the lock is released and the drawer says "Your edit of {ID} was closed after {n} minutes without activity, and the row is free for others again." (with whether a draft was kept, OND-37). The timeout is a **shared setting** in `row-locks.json` next to the planning data (whole minutes, 1 to 240), changed in Admin → Pages & menu → Row locks → **Change…** by the **Admin** role ("Shared setup in Git, master data, audit, KPI tracker") and the **Tool Owner**, nobody without the roles file (CON-101); a newer file is loaded with Load the newer data on Pages & menu. When the lock is lost while editing (released by an owner, or taken over after expiring) the edit closes the same way at the next renewal. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-37 | At the timeout (or when the lock is lost) the row's **unsaved values** shall be kept in this browser's storage as a **draft for one day** — the repository, page, row key, the editor's work email, the values typed and the row's values when the edit started, nothing else (no token) — and deleted after a day whenever drafts are read, when it is restored or discarded, and when the row is saved. The next **Edit** of that row by the same person in the same browser shows "**Restore your draft from {time}?**" naming the fields it changes and, when the row changed in Git meanwhile, each changed field (old → new, and where the draft changed it too, that the draft's value replaces it when restored), with **Discard draft** and **Restore draft** (the fields the draft changed take its values, the others keep the row's current values). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-38 | A **stuck lock** (someone else's, not expired) shall be released by the **Tool Owner** and the page's **co-owners** — also a co-owner of its group, who holds the page's rights (PGB-09, PGB-10) — with **Release lock** in the drawer, after a pop-up naming who holds it and since when (**Cancel**, **Release lock**); the result is a Background jobs entry and the row can be edited again; everyone else sees no Release lock (fail closed: nobody without the roles file). | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| OND-39 | **Hide row** / **Show row** (drawer, and **Hide** / **Show** in the table) shall replace Remove row for everyone allowed to edit rows (OND-07; blocked like Edit while someone else holds the row's lock, OND-35): the row gets `hidden: {by, at}` and a History entry "Hidden" / "Shown again" with who and when, saved at once like any row change (OND-28, OND-33); the row and its ID are never removed by it. A hidden row is left out of the **Data table, the Insights, their filters and drill-downs, the exports and the On-Demand Dashboards** for everyone. The Data tab offers **Show hidden rows (n)** (per viewer and page, remembered in this browser) listing them greyed with a **Hidden** mark; the drawer of a hidden row says who hid it and when. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-40 | A row shall be **deleted** for good only by the **Tool Owner**, from a **hidden** row (**Delete row…** in its drawer), after a pop-up naming the row's ID and that its number is never given again (**Cancel**, **Delete {ID}**); "{page}: delete row {ID}". Readable IDs of hidden or deleted rows are **never given again**: a page file keeps the highest number given (`lastSeq`, written once a row has been deleted), a page of row files its counter (OND-33). Everyone else cannot delete a row. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-50 | The Insights tab of a table page (Table + Insights, CoNQ) shall have an optional **filter row** for its viewers, set up by the page's owners (OND-51): when set up it is shown above the charts (under the Insights heading panel) and applies to **every chart and summary table** of the tab — KPI, bar, line, pie, Pareto, summary table, each chart's **Table** view, its "rows counted" line and its **drill-down** ("Rows behind …", CHT-10) — on top of each chart's own fixed filters (OND-14), which stay as they are. The filter row is the Insights tab's own: the On-Demand Dashboard pages (DSH-01…DSH-12) are not changed by it, and a Table page (no Insights tab) has none. | T | S | UC-37 |

A column that is not (or no longer) on the page, or whose type no longer fits (the period's not a Date; a filter's a Long text), stays in the file but is **left out of the filter row with a note** on the Insights tab ("Filter row: A filter's column is no longer on the page (removed): the filter is left out."); a renamed column is followed. Two saves shall be merged as the rest of the page (GIT-17, OND-28): the field is **one entry**, the later save winning where both changed it. ### 5.37 Custom streams (STR) — UC-02, UC-08, UC-37

*Group rationale: the Tool Owner asked to add a Release page for a new stream without a code change. A custom stream reuses one of the release rules the app already has; it is kept next to the stream mapping in Git and found by its name, as the built-in streams are.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STR-01 | Settings → Streams → Stream mapping shall show a panel **Custom streams** listing each custom stream (name, source, release rule, Feature matching, earlier names, Epics, **Edit** and **Remove**) with **＋ Add stream…**, a pop-up (SETTINGS · STREAMS) with **Name** (2 to 40 characters with a letter or digit), **Source** (Azure DevOps query connection, or Windchill export), **Release rule** (STR-03, each with what it gives, an example and what it counts), **Fuzzy Feature matching** (Azure DevOps only), **Earlier names (optional)** (up to 5, comma-separated) and **Epics to match Features in (optional)** (QMS-0025 Epic IDs, for the rules like DP, IOCC and Content; empty: the whole board). **Add stream** shall save the list at once to the shared setup in Git as `custom-streams.json` (next to `azure-views.json`; commit "Add stream: <name> (<rule>)"), record the result in Background jobs and say to map a connection or export named like the stream. The built-in streams (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, S&S IT, ECP) are not listed there and cannot be changed or removed. | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-02 | Every place that lists or looks up streams shall use the **built-in streams followed by the custom streams** (one list, `allStreams`): the Stream mapping rows (with a **Custom** mark and the rule's plain name; **Add query** opens the connection form named like the stream), the Release Details menu (one list A–Z, NAV-52; ＋ when not set up yet), the Home tile Release Details, the views that are a stream's (not listed under Other views), Pulse Check, the Dashboard delivery counts, the Monthly pack setup's stream ticks and figures, My dashboard's signed documents, Release naming patterns (not for streams like DP) and My views' name check. A connection or export belongs to the stream whose name — or an earlier name — it is or starts with as whole words; where several fit, the **longest name wins** (a custom "DP Labels" keeps its connection from DP). The Get started step "TFS APAC settings", the Admin-entered release counts (CP, DP, IOCC) and the Feature matching Epics of the shared settings stay with the built-in streams. | T | M | UC-08 |
| STR-03 | The release rules offered shall be the app's own, by plain names with an example: **Like DP — release from Area/Iteration path** (name from the Area Path, number from the Iteration Path; DP's milestones and data sets are DP's own and not used; counts Releases in the month); **Like IOCC — grouped by area, name from title** (Type of Release from the Area Path segment after the stream's own name or an earlier name, else the last Area Path segment; Completed and Supported as IOCC counts them; bulk Create Features); **Like Content — each document is a release by its title** (or by the stream's naming patterns; bulk Create Features); **Like Tool Validations — one per tool** (Releases and Supported as Tool Validations); **Like S&S IT — Windchill path**; **Like ECP — Windchill projects** (each export named after the stream read by project, on its own, ECP logic by default on import). CP's release-folder tree rule is not offered. A custom stream's Release page shall have the parts of UI-STANDARDS §10 as its rule gives them: source bar, counts with "What was counted", Releases \| Source data tabs, the common columns with Time Writing Feature, Planned (h), Booked (h) (the QMS-0025 match) and Create Feature, and Select / Create Features where the rule has bulk creation. | T | M | UC-08 |
| STR-04 | A custom stream's **Fuzzy Feature matching** shall decide its Time Writing Feature as for DP and IOCC (a percentage, at the Stream mapping threshold) when on, and the exact title match when off; its **Epics** shall limit the QMS-0025 match (board column, Create Feature's existing-match check) as QV-57 does for the built-in streams; its **earlier names** shall keep connections and exports named after them with the stream. | T | S | UC-08 |
| STR-05 | `custom-streams.json` read from Git shall be **validated**: a file that is not JSON or not a custom-streams file (schema 1) is refused as a whole and the streams last read stay in use, with a warning; each stream is checked on its own — an **unknown rule**, CP's tree rule, a name or earlier name that is a **built-in stream** (or its earlier name QRE), a data set (Pedestal, DHFI) or a reserved page name, or that **clashes with an earlier custom stream** is refused with a clear message (listed in the panel as "Not used — custom-streams.json in Git: <name>: <reason>") while the others are used; at most 30 streams, aliases cleaned, Epic IDs positive whole numbers. The last good file is kept in this browser so the menu and Release pages of custom streams are there at the next start before Git answers. | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-06 | **Edit** shall change a custom stream and **rename** it, keeping the old name as an earlier name so its connection or export and their data stay with it; **Remove** shall ask first in a pop-up that names what changes (its Release page and lines leave the menu, Pulse Check, the Dashboard and the Monthly pack setup) and that its connection or export and their data are **kept** (then listed under Other views); both are saved to Git at once ("Rename stream: <old> → <new>", "Change stream: …", "Remove stream: <name>"). | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-07 | Adding, changing and removing custom streams shall need **Change the stream mapping** (`streams.manage`, CON-93) and fail closed (CON-101): for anyone else — and while the roles are not loaded, Git storage is not connected or read-only, the synthetic sample is loaded, or the file has not been read yet — **＋ Add stream…**, **Edit** and **Remove** are disabled and a visible line says why (CON-102: "Your role does not allow “Change the stream mapping …”. An Admin can change this in Admin → People & roles → Role rights."); the custom streams are still shown to everyone and work for everyone. | T | M | UC-22 |
