The types shall be one list in the code (`pageKinds`), so that further types are added as further kinds — **On-Demand Dashboard** (DSH-01) is one; a Release page for a new stream comes later; a type in the file that this build does not know is kept as written and the page shown as Table + Insights. Changing a page to Table hides the Insights tab and keeps its charts in the page file. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-02 | The **menu groups** shall be the built-in ones — Data Insights, My Work, Testing, I&D Board (no page of its own, NAV-53: shown only while it holds an added page), Release Details, Other views, On-Demand, Data & governance (the Admin group is not configurable) — which keep their name and place and **cannot be renamed or removed, only configured**, and **groups of your own**, shown after On-Demand in their order. Every group has the settings **Accepts added pages** (on / off; by default on for On-Demand and for a new group of your own, off for the other built-in groups) and its **co-owners** (work emails, several per group). | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-03 | The menu layout shall be **one shared file**, `menu-layout.json` in the Git data repository next to the planning data (not in `on-demand/`, whose every file is a page, OND-08): schema 1, kind `menu-layout`, `groups` by id (a group of your own: `g-<name>` with name and order; a built-in group by its fixed id, e.g. `on-demand`, `data-insights`) with `acceptsPages` and `owners`, and `pages` by page id with `type`, `group`, `order`, `icon` and `owners` (and, for a page that is a tab of Tool Validations, `ownersFromList`: the co-owners written there from the list's co-owners, a subset of `owners`, merged as a set as the co-owners are — TVL-25). It shall be **validated when read** (not a layout: refused and reported, the layout last read kept; bad ids, a group of your own without a name, emails that are not emails dropped; an unknown icon the default; at most 30 groups of your own, 50 co-owners each), cached in the browser for the menu while Git is not connected, and saved with **queued, merged saves** (GIT-17): merged by group and page id, each field of an entry three ways (a field one save changed takes that change, one both changed the later save's), the **co-owners as sets** — an email either save added stays, an email either save removed (the other leaving it as it was) goes — and an entry one save removed and the other changed: the later save wins (removed and untouched: removed). Before any layout save, GitHub must confirm the repository is private. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-04 | The **sidebar, Home and the top bar** shall follow the menu layout: an added page is listed in its group (after the group's own entries; On-Demand and groups of your own hold only added pages) by its order, then by name, with its icon; a group of your own is a heading of its own and a Home tile with its pages, and a page in a built-in group is also on that area's Home tile; the top bar shows the page's name, with its description (else "{page}, in {group}") as the tooltip. A page whose group **no longer exists** shall fall back to **On-Demand**. | PGB-05 | **＋ Add page** shall open a pop-up (UI-STANDARDS §3/§4, eyebrow "PAGES & MENU") from **Admin → Pages & menu** (page action, and **＋ Page** in a group's row), from **⋯** next to a group's heading in the menu (shown only on groups the viewer may add to) and from **＋ Add page…** on a page: **Page type** (Table, Table + Insights), **Page name** (required), **Description** (optional), **Icon** (a small fixed set: ▦ ▤ ◫ ◉ ◇ ◈ ◎ ◔ ⚑ ★ ✎ ☰), **Menu group** — the groups that accept added pages and the viewer may add to, or, for a Tool Owner, **＋ New group…** with its name — and **Page co-owners** (optional). **Add page** creates the blank page file in Git at once (`on-demand/<page-id>.json`), then saves its place in the menu layout (and the new group), each a queued save, and opens the page; the result goes to Background jobs. | T | M | UC-38 |

A group that stops accepting added pages gives its co-owners nothing until it accepts them again. | T | M | UC-38 |

A page keeps its family: **Edit** offers On-Demand Dashboard only for a dashboard and Table / Table + Insights only for a table page, the other choice disabled with the reason ("A table page cannot become an On-Demand Dashboard: its rows would be hidden …"). **Remove page…** (on the page or in Admin → Pages & menu) asks first naming the page and its blocks and deletes its page file and its dashboard file; the data sources are not changed. The only write actions are the owners' building actions. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-03 | A block's **data source** shall be the **rows of a table page** — any On-Demand, Table or Table + Insights page except On-Demand Dashboards and the page itself, as saved in Git (not a viewer's unsaved changes), read from Git as those pages are (OND-08), offered by name — or the **Time Writing hours** (DSH-16), offered after the table pages, or the **Tool Validations master list** and **expiry list** (TVL-18), offered after them, or an **Azure DevOps query** — one the app already reads (DSH-17) or a saved query the owners added to the dashboard (DSH-18) — offered last. A block whose page is no longer there (removed, or not visible) says so ("The table page “…” is no longer there (removed), or you cannot see it.") instead of drawing. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-04 | Table pages (`page:<page-id>`), the **Time Writing hours** (`hours:time-writing`, DSH-16) the **Tool Validations master list and expiry list** (`tools:master`, `tools:expiry`, TVL-18) and the **Azure DevOps queries** (`azv:<connection id>` and, for a My view, `azm:<connection id>`, DSH-17; `azq:<reference id>`, DSH-18) shall be the **only kinds of data source**: a block or filter naming another kind of source in the file is dropped with the reason (DSH-08). | DSH-05 | A **block** shall be a **KPI tile**, a **Bar chart**, a **Line chart**, a **Pie chart** or a **Summary table** (called **Pivot table** since CHT-74, with nested rows and columns), each with a title (up to 120 characters), a data source, a **measure** — Count of rows, Sum or Average of a Number or Currency column (a Currency column one currency at a time, the one of most rows first, never added across currencies, OND-24) — for charts and tables a **group-by** field (and, except a pie, an optional second field), optional **block filters** (OND-14), the **Date column the period applies to** (by default the source's first Date column; none: "Not filtered by the period") and, with tabs, its tab. The aggregation, the "(none)" and "Other" rules and the reference checks are those of the On-Demand insights (OND-13, OND-17). **＋ Add block…** opens a pop-up (eyebrow "DASHBOARD · BLOCK") with a live preview over the current period; the main action (**Add block** / **Save to Git**) is refused with the reason while a field is missing or does not fit. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-06 | Charts shall be drawn in the shared chart frame (UI-STANDARDS §9: legend, **Data labels on** to start, **Table / Chart**, axis names); a **summary table** in the shared table (§5): sorted **largest first** ("(none)" and "Other" last), the **first 10 rows** with **Show all (+N more)**, a Total row, the row count and **Export** (Excel / CSV through the neutralising CSV helper, CON-94; the export ends with the Total row, DSH-14). **Arrange blocks…** lists the blocks in order with **Edit**, **↑ ↓** and **Remove** (after a pop-up naming the block); **Tabs and filters…** adds, renames, reorders and removes up to 10 **tabs** (a removed tab's blocks go to the first tab) and up to 6 **filters of the filter row**. KPI tiles are shown above the tabs; with tabs each tile has **Open {tab} →**. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-07 | The **filter row** shall offer **Year → Quarter → Month** as dependent multi-selects (`MultiSelect`; `Period`, `periodMonths`, `narrowPeriod`, offered quarters and months as Team overview, OV-15, OV-16): nothing ticked in Year is the **current year**, nothing ticked in Quarter or Month every one of the years or quarters counted; a block keeps the rows whose month in its Date column is in the period (rows without that date left out and counted). Each **owner-defined filter** lets the viewer tick values of a Dropdown or Multi-select column of one data source (nothing ticked = all) and narrows the blocks over that source. **Reset filters** goes back to the current year with no filter. The years offered are the current year, those found in the blocks' dates and any chosen. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-08 | The dashboard shall be **one file per page**, `dashboards/<page-id>.json` in the Git data repository — named as the page's own file, `dashboards/<name>--<page-id>.json`, for a Dashboard page created from the version of OND-134 on (schema 1, kind `dashboard`: `tabs`, `filters`, `blocks` by id, and the saved Azure queries' references `queries` by id, DSH-18), created with the first block and saved at once with the **queued, merged save** (GIT-17): merged by block, tab and filter id, each field three ways (a field one save changed takes that change; both changed: the later save's), an entry one save removed and the other changed: the later save wins (removed and untouched: removed), the order following the save that changed it. **Validated when read**: a file that is not a dashboard is refused whole (reported, the one last read kept); a bad block — unknown type, bad or repeated id, no title, unknown data source — is **dropped with a message** on the page; at most **40 blocks**, 10 tabs, 6 filters, 10 filters per block, 200 KB; column references are kept as written and checked when drawn (DSH-10). | DSH-12 | The results of the building actions (add, edit, move, remove a block; tabs and filters; rename and remove the page) shall go to **Background jobs** (UI-STANDARDS §8) naming the page and the file; a save that fails is also kept above the page and in the save queue with Retry (GIT-17); warnings about the data shown (a dashboard file refused or blocks dropped) stay on the page. | T | M | UC-38 |

The copy gets a **new id** and keeps every setting and its data source; on another dashboard it goes on the tab of the **same name** (case-insensitive), else on its **first tab** — and the result says so ("… has no tab named “…”: the block is on its first tab, …"). It is saved to the target's file (`dashboards/<page-id>.json`) at once with the **queued, merged save** (GIT-17); a target with 40 blocks refuses it with the reason; the result goes to **Background jobs** (DSH-12). | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-16 | **Time Writing hours** shall be a block data source next to the table pages: the **planned** and **booked** hours of the Time Writing data **as Team overview counts them** (the same month Tasks, people, regions, roles and calculation profile, OV-11) as rows by **Team member**, **Region**, **Role**, **Stream** (Epic), **Feature** and **Month** — the hours of a stream on no Feature as "(no Feature)", a person's other counted time (trainings, other NPRA, common activities) as the stream "Other time (trainings, NPRA, activities)" — so that the totals per person and month and per stream equal Team overview's; Team overview itself is not changed. A block over it measures the **Sum** or **Average** of **Planned hours** or **Booked hours** (a new block: Sum of Planned hours; Count and the other measures are refused with the reason), groups by those fields (Month by month) and takes the dashboard's period by **Month**; the filter row may filter its Team member, Region, Role or Stream. Only the block's settings are saved in the dashboard file, never hours. Viewing follows who may see **Team overview** (the menu area "Team insights", CON-94): for anyone else such a block shows **"This block cannot be shown: Not available for your role."** while the other blocks are drawn, and no hours are worked out for them. | T | S | UC-38, UC-22 |
| DSH-17 | The **Azure DevOps queries the app already reads** shall be block data sources with **all their columns as fields** (DSH-20), one source per query, from the data the app has fetched (`state.azureSources`, the same data as the pages that show them — no read of their own): each **Release Details stream's query** (the connections of Stream mapping, named by the stream, or by the connection's view name when a stream has several; kind "Release Details stream query"), the **Tool Validations Tool List** and **Validation Documents** queries (named "Tool Validations · Tool List" / "· Validation Documents" as their tabs; kind "Tool Validations query") and the viewer's own **My views** (SET-17; named by the view; kind "My view"). A query not fetched yet says so ("Not fetched yet: fetch … from Azure DevOps (↻ Refresh on the top bar) to see this."); the block's ⓘ and the Data source line say when it was fetched. Only the source key (`azv:<connection id>`, `azm:<connection id>`) is saved in the dashboard file. Rights: DSH-23. | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-18 | A dashboard's **builders** (DSH-09: "Build On-Demand Dashboards" or a co-owner of the page or its group) shall add **any other saved Azure DevOps query** as a data source with **＋ Add Azure query…** in the page's ⋯ (a pop-up, eyebrow "DASHBOARD · DATA SOURCE"): **Pick a saved query of a connection** — a server and project of the app's connections, then one of the project's saved queries listed with the builder's own PAT (Shared Queries and My Queries, two folder levels) — or **Paste the query's address** (`…/<project>/_queries/query/<id>/`, query-edit or `?id=`), accepted only for a **server the app already reads** (a connection's or My settings' server), and a **name** (unique on the dashboard, at most 80 characters). Only the **reference** is saved in the dashboard file in Git — `queries: [{ id, name, server, project, queryId }]`, merged by id (GIT-17) — **never a PAT, never rows, never the query text**; the reader keeps only these keys and drops an invalid one with the reason (DSH-08). At most **10** per dashboard; **Remove Azure query…** removes one that no block or filter reads (else it says which). A block copied to another dashboard (DSH-15) takes the reference along (the target's own when it has the same query). Everyone else sees both actions disabled with the reason (CON-102). | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-19 | A saved query (DSH-18) shall be **read with each viewer's own PAT** for its server (one PAT per server, My settings → Servers and PATs; never the builder's) when the dashboard opens — only the queries a block or a filter uses, all of them while a block pop-up or Tabs and filters… is open — **read only**: the saved query **by its id** (`GET …/_apis/wit/wiql/{id}`, at most 5,001 ids; a tree or links query: the work items at either end of its links), the project's **field list** (names and types) and the work items in batches of **200** — through the same transport as the other reads (browser, or the desktop proxy, which checks the id again, SM-200). Nothing is written to Azure DevOps (on-premises servers stay read-only). The rows stay in the page's memory only — never in Git, a backup, the browser's storage or a published interactive HTML file (whose note says such blocks are not published). | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-20 | A query's **columns** (its SELECT list in its order; the default columns when it selects only the ID) shall become the source's **fields** with chart types from Azure DevOps' field types: Integer and Double → **Number**; DateTime → **Date** (by day); picklists, identities, Boolean (Yes / No) and tree paths (Area / Iteration Path) → **Dropdown**-like categories; **Tags** (and any `….Tags` field) → **Multi-select** (as MYD-16's Tags); HTML, plain text, history and GUIDs → **Text** (HTML as its text); the **ID** → Text. Without Azure's types (DSH-17) the type comes from the reference name (State, Work Item Type, Assigned To, Area Path … → Dropdown) and the values (all dates → Date, all numbers → Number, repeating values → Dropdown, else Text). **Identities by display name, never an email**: an identity's display name, "Name <name@…>" as "Name", an email on its own as the part before "@" — in every field. Each row's ID opens the work item (https only) in the drill-down. Field ids are stable for every viewer (the reference name, shortened with a hash). | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-21 | **Caps**: a query source shall hold at most **5,000 rows** and **30 columns** (the first ones, in the query's order). A capped source says so — "Capped: only the first 5,000 rows are read (the query returns more); … — the figures are not complete. Narrow the query in Azure DevOps." — on the block (a **Capped** mark in its header with the reason, and the line above the chart or table), in its ⓘ, on the KPI tile, under the block pop-up's Data source, and in **every export** of the block (the summary or Pivot table's Export and **Rows behind**: file name prefixed "Capped-", the reason as the first line, OND-67's mechanism). | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-22 | A dashboard's Azure queries shall be **read when the dashboard opens** (and when a block or filter starts to use one) — **no background polling** — and again with **↻ Refresh Azure queries** in the page's ⋯ (for every viewer; disabled with "The Azure queries are being read." while reading; the rows already shown stay until the new ones are there). Each block over a saved query says in its ⓘ when it was read; the app's own queries (DSH-17) are as fresh as the app's last fetch. | T | S | UC-38 |
| DSH-23 | **Rights to read** an Azure query source shall follow the existing rights, **fail closed**, a block a viewer may not see showing the reason ("This block cannot be shown: …") while the other blocks are drawn: a Release Details **stream query** and the **Tool Validations** queries need the menu area **Release Details** (CON-94; "Not available for your role."); a **My view** is its **owner's only** — it is shown only in its owner's browser ("This is someone else's own My view: only its owner sees it …") and not while viewing as someone else; a **saved query** (DSH-18) needs the viewer's **own PAT** for its server ("Add your PAT for … in My settings → Servers and PATs to see this: …") and that PAT's **access in Azure DevOps** (a refusal shown as "Azure DevOps did not return the query with your PAT: …", the PAT never in the text), and is not read while **viewing as** someone else. The block pop-up offers a source the viewer cannot read as "(not available)". | T | S | UC-38, UC-22 |

### 5.40 Chart options (CHT) — UC-37, UC-38

The options are one implementation for the Insights tab of the table pages (OND-11…OND-17) and the blocks of the On-Demand Dashboard pages (DSH-05, DSH-06): the same file fields, validation, aggregation, drawing and editor.

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CHT-01 | A **bar chart** shall take an **Orientation**: **Horizontal bars** (the default, as before) or **Vertical columns**; with a second field (**Stacked by**, a Dropdown or Multi-select) the values are **stacked** in either orientation (at most 5 stack colours, the rest as "Other", OND-12); an Average, Minimum, Maximum or Distinct count cannot be stacked (OND-17). Vertical columns keep the chart frame (UI-STANDARDS §9: legend, Data labels, Table / Chart, axis names), the value axis from zero and the keyboard (←/→, Enter, Esc). | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-02 | The **Pie / donut** shall stay a donut with the total (or the slice pointed at) in its middle, in the Insights tab and as the Dashboard's Pie chart; a new Insights type **KPI (one number)** shall show the measure over the matched rows (each row once, one currency at a time, OND-24) in the shared tile (`Metric`, UI-STANDARDS §9), as the Dashboard's KPI tile (DSH-05) does. | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-03 | When the categories (or the second field) are a **Date** column, **Group dates by** shall offer **Day**, **Week (ISO, from Monday**; week 1 holds the year's first Thursday, labelled "W02 2026"), **Month** (the default, as before), **Quarter** ("Q1 2026") and **Year**; a line shows every period from the first to the last, also periods without rows; the filters' month ranges (OND-14) and the Dashboard period (DSH-07) still work by month. | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-04 | **Show** shall offer **Top 5 / 10 / 15 / 20 + Other** or **All categories** for bar, pie, Pareto and summary table: the N largest categories by the measure, the rest — with "(none)" — summed as **"Other"**, shown last; without a choice the type's limit as before (a pie 5, bars 20, a table all). | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-05 | **Running total** (bar and line, Count of rows or Sum only) shall show each category or period as its value plus all before it, in the order shown; the axis says "— running total". | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-06 | **Percent of total** (bar, line and summary table, Count of rows or Sum only) shall show every value as a share of the total (each row once) in % with one decimal; with a Multi-select the shares can add up to more than 100% (said in ⓘ). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-07 | The measures shall add **Minimum** and **Maximum** of a Number or Currency column (one currency at a time) and **Distinct count** of any column but Long text (each value of a Multi-select on its own); a pie or Pareto offers only Count of rows and Sum (their parts add up); a measure a column no longer fits is reported as OND-17. | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-08 | A **Target line** (optional) shall be a fixed number with an optional label (up to 40 characters) drawn as a dashed line on bar, line and Pareto charts, in the measure's unit (in % with Percent of total), named in the legend and in ⓘ; the value axis includes it. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-09 | **Colours per value**: the owners shall pin a colour of the chart palette (Colour 1…5, the `--viz-series-*` tokens, light and dark) to a value of the coloured Dropdown / Multi-select column (stacks, lines, a pie's slices); the pins are the page's (the Insights tab) or the dashboard's and apply to every chart there, for everyone; values not pinned take the palette colours not pinned, in order — without pins the palette order as before; "(none)" and "Other" stay neutral. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-10 | **Drill-down**: a click on a bar, a stack, a slice or a point — or **Enter** on the one highlighted with the arrow keys — shall open a read-only pop-up "Rows behind {category} · {series}" listing exactly the rows counted there ("Other" included; the period's own rows for a running total) in the shared table (ID and every column, Export), in the Insights tab and on the Dashboard; no write; not in an editor's preview. | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-11 | **Width**: each chart or table (a Dashboard block other than a KPI tile) shall be **Half width** (the default: two side by side on a wide screen, as before) or **Full width** (the whole row) — and, since CHT-15, **One third** or **Two thirds** of a 6-column grid; on a narrow screen every panel is full width. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-12 | **Pareto** shall be a chart type of the Insights tab and the Dashboard: columns sorted largest first ("(none)" and "Other" last, Top N as CHT-04), the **cumulative share** as a line on a **0–100% axis** on the right, a dashed **80%** line, an optional target line; its Table view lists each category's value, **Share (%)** and **Cumulative (%)**; no second field; Count of rows or Sum. | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-13 | The options shall be **optional fields** of an insight or block (`options`: `orientation`, `dateGroup`, `top`, `cumulative`, `percent`, `target`, `size`) and of the page or dashboard file (`colors`: value → 1…5), written only when set, so a chart without them is drawn, exported and written **exactly as before**; **validated when read**: an unknown type is dropped with its message as before, an option the type or measure does not take dropped, Top N clamped to 1…50, a target that is not a number dropped, a label capped, a colour outside 1…5 dropped; the colour pins are one entry in the merged save (GIT-17). An app version from before chart options drops Pareto and KPI insights, reads Minimum / Maximum / Distinct count as Count of rows and drops the options when it saves (LIM-23). | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-14 | The chart pop-ups (Insights and Dashboard block) shall spread the display options over the editor's tabs (CHT-70) — **Group dates by** and **Show** on **Data**, the rest on **Format**, the chart elements and axis names on **Elements** — showing only the options the chosen type takes (orientation for bars; Group dates by only with a Date column; Show for bar, pie, Pareto, table; Running total and Percent of total disabled with the reason for a measure that does not add up), usable at 390 px; the preview shows them at once. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-15 | **Size presets — width**: a chart or table panel shall take **One third (⅓)**, **Half (½)**, **Two thirds (⅔)** or **Full width** of the chart grid (since CHT-44: 4, 6, 8 or 12 columns of the 12-column grid; before: 2, 3, 4 or 6 of 6); ½ is CHT-11's Half width (the default) and Full its Full width — the values `half` and `full` of v10 are read as they are; below about **720 px** every chart is full width (its height still applies). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-16 | **Size presets — height**: a chart shall take **Small** (¾ of today's height), **Medium** (today's height, the default) or **Large** (1½ times): bars and rows, columns, lines, the Pareto and the donut redraw for the height and for the width they get (the drawing width taken from the panel, also for the fixed-width SVGs, so the page never scrolls sideways); a KPI tile and a summary table take the height of their content (Height disabled with the reason). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-17 | **The owners' size for everyone**: on the Insights tab the page's owners (who may set charts up, OND-15) set a chart's width and height in its **Edit** pop-up (**Format** tab, CHT-70: **Width**, **Height**), with **Size…** in its ⋯ menu, or with the corner handle in **Arrange charts** mode (CHT-47; a dashed frame, "Done arranging" to leave); on an On-Demand Dashboard its builders (DSH-09) do the same in the block pop-up, **Size…**, **Arrange blocks…** (Width and Height per block) and **Arrange on the page** (CHT-47); each change is saved to Git at once with the page or dashboard (OND-30, DSH-12) and shown to everyone who has not chosen their own size. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-18 | **Each viewer's own size**: anyone who sees a chart — read-only roles too — shall resize it **for themselves** with the corner handle (outside Arrange mode); the size is kept in their **personal views** (`chart-size:{chart id}`, in Git per person, MYD-06; in this browser without Git) and never written to the page or dashboard file; a chart with an own size shows **"Size: yours · Reset to default"**, and Reset to default (or resizing it back to the default) removes it. Precedence: **the viewer's own size > the owners' (or shared) size > the factory size**. **View as** is read only: a size chosen there lasts until the page is left and is not saved. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-19 | **Built-in charts**: the charts in the shared chart frame on the app's own pages — Team overview → Streams: **Epic mix by month** (`overview:epic-mix-by-month`), **Overrun hours** (`overview:overrun-hours`), **Ad-hoc hours** (`overview:adhoc-hours`) — shall be resizable by each viewer as CHT-18; with no owner per page, their default is their **factory size** (today's: full width, Medium — nothing changes until someone resizes) or the size a holder of **"Table, chart and page defaults"** (`layout.defaults`, the Tool Owner) saved with **Save size for everyone** — one entry of the shared views defaults (`personal-views-defaults.json`, as Column options → Save for everyone, BKL-47; with Git connected and not with the synthetic sample) — which **Use the factory size for everyone** clears; the result goes to Background jobs (UI-STANDARDS §8). | T | S | UC-09, UC-37 |
| CHT-20 | *(Retired — see CHT-43, CHT-44: since the 12-column grid every page uses the grid, laid out as before until someone resizes or moves a chart)* **Existing look kept**: a page whose charts nobody resized shall look exactly as before — the Insights and Dashboard grid of v10 (two half-width charts side by side, full width on its own row) and the built-in charts one under the other at full width; the **6-column grid** is used only once a chart takes ⅓ or ⅔ (a built-in chart anything but full width). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-21 | *(Retired — see CHT-49: the handle snaps to columns and rows instead of the presets)* **The resize handle**: a handle on the chart panel's **bottom-right corner** (not on a phone, < 720 px) shall resize by **pointer drag**, snapping to the presets of CHT-15 / CHT-16 with a **dashed outline of the size it will take** and its name ("width ⅔, height Large") while dragging, kept on release; and by **keyboard**: it is focusable, **→ / ←** step the width, **↓ / ↑** the height, **Enter** (or leaving it) keeps the size, **Escape** cancels; its accessible name is "Resize {chart}: width ½, height Medium" and a live region announces each new size. The outline never makes the page scroll sideways. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-22 | The sizes shall be **optional fields** of an insight or block (`options.size`: `third`, `half`, `twothirds` or `full`; `options.height`: `small` or `large`, Medium when absent; not for a KPI tile or a summary table), **written only when set** (a chart nobody resized is written byte for byte as before), validated when read (an unknown value dropped); a viewer's size and a shared default are validated the same way when read. An app version before CHT-15 reads `third` and `twothirds` as no size and ignores `height` — its viewers see the chart at the default size — and drops them when it saves the page or dashboard (LIM-36). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-23 | **Long labels fit their chart** (KB-83): a category label (bar row label, column and Pareto tick) shall wrap by words into up to **two lines** in the room it has at the chart's current size (one line when a row is too low), recomputed when the chart is resized and at 390 px; a word or line that still does not fit ends in "…", with the **full text as its tooltip** (`<title>`), in the chart's accessible name and in its Table view; the **axis names** end in "…" (full text as tooltip) instead of running out of the chart; legend entries wrap. A short label is drawn exactly as before. | T | M | UC-09, UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-24 | **Axis names set by the owners**: the chart pop-ups (Insights and Dashboard block, CHT-14) shall offer, on the **Elements** tab (CHT-70), an optional **X axis name** and **Y axis name** (up to 60 characters) for the charts with axes — bar (either orientation), line, Pareto; not for a pie, KPI or summary table — with today's automatic name as placeholder (e.g. "Category", "Sum of Cost (EUR)", "Month of Found on"); empty means automatic; saved with the chart (`options.xTitle`, `options.yTitle`, written only when set, trimmed and validated when read; an older app ignores them). The built-in charts keep their fixed names. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-25 | **A short chart header**: a chart or table panel on the Insights tab and on an On-Demand Dashboard shall show only the **title** and **ⓘ** (since CHT-39 without the type eyebrow) — which lists what it measures and how it is split, its own filters, the filter row's effect, its period (a Dashboard block), the rows counted and left out, the currency found and shown, and its counting rules — plus a neutral **"Filtered"** pill (its tooltip and accessible name naming the filters) only when its own filters or the filter row's choices narrow it; a warning that matters for the numbers stays visible on the chart (a column that is gone, "Several currencies: EUR shown — amounts in different currencies are not added up"). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-26 | **Chart elements — Chart heading** (Excel's Chart Title): the chart pop-ups (Insights and Dashboard block) shall have a **Chart elements** group on the **Elements** tab (CHT-14, CHT-70), modelled on Excel's Chart Elements and offering only what the chart type takes; **Chart heading** (on by default) shows or hides the chart's title in its panel header — the panel title *is* the chart title (no second title is drawn in the chart); hidden, it is still read by screen readers, kept in the chart's accessible name and its ⓘ and ⋯ stay. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-27 | **Axes and axis names** (Excel's Axis Titles; bar in either orientation, line, Pareto): **Horizontal axis** and **Vertical axis** show or hide the axis's tick or category labels (a Pareto's 0–100% axis with the vertical axis), and **Horizontal axis name** / **Vertical axis name** the axis titles (their text the X / Y axis names of CHT-24 or the automatic ones); each is on by default; an axis or title left out gives its room to the plot. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-28 | **Data labels**: the owners shall set data labels **On for everyone**, **Off for everyone** or leave them **as each viewer chooses** (the default, as before: UI-17's shared default); each viewer's own **Data labels** box still has the final say for themselves (kept per chart on their computer, UI-17). **Label position** as Excel per type — bar / column: **Center, Inside End, Inside Base, Outside End** (a stacked bar or column: Center, Inside End, Inside Base); line: **Center, Left, Right, Above, Below**; pie / donut: **Center, Inside End, Outside End, Best Fit**; **Label contains** (Excel's order): **Series name, Category name, Value, Percentage** (Percentage for a pie or with Percent of total, where Value is then the measured amount), joined with ", "; **Number format**: Automatic (as before, e.g. 12.3K) or thousand separators with **0, 1 or 2 decimals**, and the **currency code** for a Sum, Average, Minimum or Maximum of a Currency column. The defaults draw the labels exactly as before (bars: the total at the end; stacks: centred; columns: above; lines: above; pie: the share in the ring); a horizontal bar's end value stays as before unless the owner's labels replace it. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-29 | **Labels fit**: data labels shall never overlap each other or leave the chart: a label wraps into at most two lines (Outside End of a column) or stays on one, a category or series name in a label is cut to 18 characters with "…", a **number is never cut** — a label that does not fit is left out (its value stays in the tooltip, the Table view and the data table); **Outside End** labels of horizontal bars and two-line column labels make the plot smaller so they stay inside the chart; a line's Left / Right labels take room beside the plot; on a pie, **Best Fit** keeps a label inside its slice when it fits and puts it outside with a **leader line** otherwise, outside labels on each side are moved apart, and the drawing grows to hold them. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-30 | **Data table** (bar in either orientation, line, Pareto): the chart's numbers in a small table under it, as Excel's data table with legend keys — one column per category, one row per series with its colour key (a Pareto adds its cumulative share), in the chart's number format; a real table (caption, column and row headers) in a region that scrolls sideways and can be reached with the keyboard, so many categories never make the page scroll. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-31 | **Gridlines** (charts with axes): **Major horizontal**, **Major vertical**, **Minor horizontal** and **Minor vertical** — major lines at the value axis's ticks or between categories, minor lines at a fifth of a major step or at each category's centre; the default is as before (the value axis's major lines: vertical for horizontal bars, horizontal otherwise). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-32 | **Legend**: **None**, **Top** (the default for charts with axes: in the chart's toolbar, as before), **Bottom**, **Left** or **Right** (beside the plot, which gets narrower); a pie's legend list is beside it on the **Right** by default, or Left, Top, Bottom or None. A legend on the left comes before the chart in the reading and keyboard order, otherwise after it; below 720 px a side legend goes above or below the chart. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-33 | **Trendline** (bar in either orientation, column, line; not a Pareto or pie): **None** (default) or **Linear** — one straight least-squares line per series (a stacked chart: each series' own values; categories as 0, 1, 2 …; points without a value left out; at least two points), dotted in the series' colour, named **"Linear ({series})"** in the legend; the value axis includes its ends. No error bars. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-34 | **Who sets them**: the chart elements shall be set by the page's owners (OND-15) or the dashboard's builders (DSH-09) in the chart's pop-up, saved with the chart for everyone (OND-30, DSH-12), with the preview showing them at once; the same code draws them on the Insights tab and on On-Demand Dashboard blocks; the built-in charts of the app's own pages are not changed. A Dashboard block whose builders set data labels on or off no longer starts with its labels on (DSH-06), their choice being the default. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-35 | **Stored and validated**: the elements shall be an optional field `options.elements` of an insight or block (`title`, `axisX`, `axisY`, `titleX`, `titleY`: `false`; `labels`: true / false; `position`; `content`; `decimals`: 0–2; `code`; `dataTable`; `gridlines`; `legend`; `trendline`: `linear`), each **written only when it differs from its default** (a chart without changes is written, exported and drawn exactly as before); **validated when read**: unknown keys and values dropped, an element the type does not take dropped (no axes, gridlines, data table or trendline on a pie; no trendline on a Pareto; Percentage only for a pie or with Percent of total), a stacked chart's Outside End drawn as Center. An app version before chart elements ignores the field (its viewers see the chart as before) and drops it when it saves (LIM-38). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-36 | **KPI cards**: the KPI (one number) charts of an Insights tab shall be shown together as a **row of compact cards** above the other charts, in the charts' order, wrapping to further rows — the look of the Dashboard's KPI tiles (UI-STANDARDS §9): the title with ⓘ (what it measures, its filters, the filter row's effect, the rows counted, the currency), the number with its currency, the measure as a caption, **"Filtered"** when narrowed, and for the owners a **⋯** menu (**Move up**, **Move down** among the cards, **Edit…**, **Size…** (since CHT-52), **Remove…**); the filter row applies to them (OND-50) and a currency choice as OND-24. Existing KPI charts (since v9.14) become cards. | T | S | UC-37 |
| CHT-37 | **Show as**: a KPI's **Format** tab (CHT-14, CHT-70) shall offer **Show as: Card** (the default) or **Panel** (the chart panel of before, with its size); `options.view: "panel"` is written only for Panel and validated when read. A card is at least 180 px wide (since CHT-51 at its default 3 columns on a 12- or 6-column grid; a card made narrower is at least 2 columns), its **number is never cut** (it wraps before its currency code, and as a last resort between digits, with the full number as tooltip) and its caption takes at most two lines with "…" (full text as tooltip and in ⓘ) (KB-84). | T | S | UC-37 |

An **older app version** (before this one) reads such a file as the one-level table of `category` × `second` and **drops the three fields when it saves** that page or dashboard (its strict readers, `parseInsights` and `parseDashboard`, keep only the fields they know; LIM-104). | T | M | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-80 | **Rows behind and Export**: a click (or Enter) on **any number** — a cell, a subtotal, an outer column's Subtotal, the Total row or column — shall open **Rows behind {groups}** ("Rows behind Platform · Feb 2026") with every group's filter applied (row fields and column fields, "Other" for the values Show left out), the rows counted there, each once (CHT-10). **Export** (Excel / CSV, the formats of the table defaults and the "Export tables" right, CON-94; marked as sample data on a sample page, OND-67; through the neutralising CSV helper) writes a **flat table**: one column per row field, a **Line** column ("Subtotal" on a group's line, "Total" on the last), then one column per column-field combination ("Stream A · Jan 2026"), each outer **"… · Subtotal"** and **Total**, numbers to 2 decimals — every line, whatever is closed, cut or not shown. The pop-up preview and Export PDF… have no clicks and no Export. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |

Collapse groups, narrow the filters or use Export for the whole table."). Export is never cut. | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-82 | **One look** (UI-STANDARDS §5, §6, §9): the nested Pivot table is a matrix with the row fields in one indented first column (18 px a level) and its heading "Epic / State", numbers right-aligned, the subtotal lines and columns shaded and bold in the light and dark theme, the headings kept in view while it scrolls, the controls in the shared table toolbar (status left; Collapse all, Expand all and Export right); the symbols ＋ ↑ ↓ ✕ ▸ ▾ only. | T | C | UC-37, UC-38 |
| CHT-83 | **The value's own name** (product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "I changed the data labels but the table below still uses Row Count"): a chart's measured value shall be named by the owners' **Value name** (new, optional, on the Data tab, up to 60 characters, stored as the chart option `valueName`; an older app ignores it, LIM-104) or, when that is empty, by the **title they typed on the value axis** (Y of columns, a line or a Pareto; X of horizontal bars, CHT-24), else by the generated measure ("Count of rows", "Sum of Cost (EUR)") — the same name in the chart's **legend**, its **Table** view and **data table** (CHT-34), its **tooltips**, data labels showing the Series name, the Pivot table's measure heading, **Rows behind** ("… counted in X (Services)") and **Export** headings; with Running total or Percent of total the name is followed by "— running total" / "— % of total". The ⓘ keeps saying how it is counted ("Count of rows by …"). | T | S | UC-37, UC-38 |


### 5.41 Tool Validations master list (TVL) — UC-39

*Group rationale: the product owner asked (24 Nov 2026) for one master list of validated tools that keeps a tool once it is listed, fed from several sources and kept in Git, with each validation's expiry — the field names in Azure DevOps are named after the first export, so the mapping stays generic and is set on screen. Edit rights follow the page co-owner mechanism of the page builder (PGB-08…PGB-11).*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TVL-01 | The Tool Validations stream shall accept **several sources** of tools, listed in the **Sources** panel of its **Setup** tab (TVL-14): the **Tool Validations query** (the stream's own Azure DevOps connection, always there, never removed), **another Azure DevOps query** (any other connection set up in the app, cloud or on-premises, read only), an **imported Windchill export** and a **manual list** kept as a **Table page** of the app (On-Demand or any group, PGB-01). **＋ Add source…** (a pop-up: Kind, the connection, export or page, Name) adds one, **Remove** (asked first in a pop-up) removes it and its links — its tools stay. At most 20 sources. Nothing is written to Azure DevOps. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-02 | The **master list** shall be kept in the Git data repository next to the planning data as `tool-validations.json` — `schema` 1, `kind` "tool-validations", the team default `defaultDays`, the co-owners `owners` (work emails), the `sources` (id, kind, name, `ref` of the connection, export or page, the field `mapping`, when and by whom last merged) and the `tools` (name, version, validation date, validity months, due date, the fields changed by hand `manual`, notes, the `sources` each with `inSource`, added at / by, changed at / by), sorted by name — and, when there are some, the owners' **`tabs`** (TVL-16: page id, tab name and page type, in their order) and, once saved, the **`highlights`** (TVL-27: the due date highlight bands) — read by everyone with Git storage and **validated strictly** when read: a file that is not JSON or not this kind is refused whole (the list last read stays in use, with the reason; nothing can be changed then), each source and tool is checked on its own (an unknown kind, an id used twice, no name, a date that is not a date, a period outside 1…600 months, the same tool and version twice) and a bad one is listed as "Not used — tool-validations.json in Git: <name>: <reason>" while the others are used; at most 5,000 tools. The last good file read is kept in this browser. Saves are **queued and merged** (GIT-17: tools by name and version, sources by id, co-owners as sets, the owners' tabs by page id — TVL-16, the highlight bands as one value — TVL-27) with the `[saved …]` stamp and the user tag (GIT-22); a newer version saved elsewhere raises "Newer data in Git" on the Tool Validations page (GIT-18, GIT-20). GitHub must confirm the repository is private before each write. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-03 | The **same tool** from two sources (or a source and a tool added by hand) shall be **one entry**: the same **name**, compared ignoring letter case and every run of spaces and punctuation ("Lab-VIEW" = "labview"), and the same **version** (a leading "v" and trailing ".0" ignored: "v2020.0" = "2020"; no version is a version of its own). **Different versions are separate entries.** Each entry remembers its sources. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-04 | The tools of the **Tool Validations query** (with two queries: the **Tools** query, TVL-19) shall be **appended automatically** whenever its data is fetched (↻, Fetch, Load from Git): read with its field mapping (TVL-07; the validation date is the completion date unless the mapping names another, TVL-29), its documents grouped per tool and version (the latest dates), new tools added, the dates of known ones updated (for a tool of Azure DevOps the query's values replace a date or period changed by hand, TVL-30), and a tool linked to it that the fetched data **no longer holds** marked **Not in source now** — **never removed**: it stays, with its dates and notes, until someone removes it. Everyone sees the result at once (worked out in the browser from the data fetched); it is **saved to Git by the app of a Tool Owner or co-owner** (TVL-11) — once per fetch, as its own commit "Tool Validations: n new, n updated, n no longer in the source from the Tool Validations query", its result under Background jobs — and, until then, the Master list says how many tools of the latest fetch are not saved yet. A fetch without items changes nothing. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-05 | The **other sources** (another query, a Windchill export, a manual list) shall be read on **their own pages** (fetched, imported, edited there) and brought into the master list with **Merge into master list…** in the Sources panel or on the source's own tab (TVL-14) — a pop-up that shows, before anything is saved, how many rows were read and what changes ("1 new, 1 updated, 0 no longer in the source" with the names; a warning while the field mapping is still the guess) — then merged as TVL-04 merges (the source's whole data as read in this browser: new tools added, known ones linked and updated, tools gone from the source marked) and saved to Git with when and by whom; disabled with the reason while the source has no data in this browser. *(An explicit merge was chosen over a merge on every import: a wrong mapping or a half-imported file is seen in the preview before it reaches the shared list.)* | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-06 | Each entry shall have a **Validation date**, a **Validity period** in **months** ("Validity (months)"; a source's period read as a number in the unit set for the source — months or years — or as a text with its unit, "24 months", "2 years", "2y") and a **Due date**: an **explicit due date** from a source or entered by hand **wins** and is shown as such ("from the source", "entered by hand"); else the due date is the validation date **plus the period** in calendar months (31 Jan + 1 month = 28/29 Feb), shown as "validation date + validity period"; else, when the entry has a validation date but **no validity period**, the period is the **default of 24 months** (2 years): the due date is the validation date plus 24 calendar months (29 Feb 2024 → 28 Feb 2026), shown as "validation date + 24 months (default)", and the period is shown as **"24 (default)"** (exported so too) in the Validity (months) columns of the Expiry list, the Master list and each source's tab; the default is applied when the list is read and **never written** to `tool-validations.json`, so a period or due date given later (by a source or by hand) wins; **No due date** only without a validation date and without a due date. For a tool from an Azure DevOps source the validation date is the **completion date** (TVL-29), so its due date is shown as "completion date + 24 months (default)" (or "completion date + validity period"). The Add / Edit tool pop-up says "Leave empty for 24 months (default)" and previews the due date with it. Dates are read in the forms a source writes them (ISO, 27-Oct-2026, Oct 27, 2026, 27.10.2026, 10/27/2026) as UTC days (NAV-51). | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-07 | Each source shall have a **field mapping** set on screen (**Edit mapping…**, a pop-up: Tool name (required), Version, Validation date, Validity period, Due date, each optional, and "Validity period numbers are in Months / Years", with how many rows and tools the mapping gives) and **saved in the shared file**. Until it is saved the app's **guesses** are used and marked **Guessed** (in the Sources panel, the mapping pop-up and the merge pop-up): for an Azure DevOps source the name and version by the Tool Validations rule (the Area Path folder, else the Title; QV-34, the stream's naming patterns QV-55), the validation date from the **completion date** (TVL-29: the special field "Completion date", also read when no validation date is mapped; Author Approval Date can be chosen; a mapping **saved** before with Author Approval Date is **kept** — no automatic change: the owners pick Completion date in Setup → Edit mapping, TVL-29), the due date from Microsoft.VSTS.Scheduling.DueDate (else TargetDate) and the period from a field named like "validity" when the query has them; for a Windchill export or a page the columns whose names fit (Tool / Name, Version, Validation date, Validity / Period, Due / Expiry / Next validation). | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-08 | The **Expiry list** tab of the Tool Validations Release page (TVL-14, opened first) shall show the Validation expiry — shown also before a fetch and in a month without documents: on top three tiles — **Overdue** (due date passed), **Due in the next N days** (with the last day), **No due date** — then one table of the tools **overdue first**, then **due within N days** (soonest first), then **without a due date** (no validation date and no due date, TVL-06; never hidden; tools due later are left out and listed on the **Master list** tab) with Status, Tool, Version, Due date, **Days left** ("10 days overdue", "Due today", "30 days left"), Due date from, Validation date, Validity (months) (Sources and Notes in Columns); the due date, status and days left follow the due date of TVL-06, so a tool with only a validation date is overdue or due **24 months** after it (default); a filter bar with the window, Status (All / Overdue / Due soon / No due date, with counts), search and **Reset filters**; "{shown} of {total} tools"; **Export** (Excel or CSV, a value that could be read as a formula written as text, UI-11). Days are counted from today in UTC. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-09 | The window **N** shall be set **on the same screen** ("Expiring in the next [N] days", 1 to 730): a **team default of 60 days**, kept in the shared file and changed by those who may edit the list (**Setup → Team settings → Edit…**, TVL-14), and **each person's own** N, kept for them in their personal views (on every computer, MYD-06) as soon as it is typed, with **Use the team default (n)** to go back; the line under it says which applies. Next to them, **Set {N} days as team default** makes the window shown the team default **on the Expiry list itself** — saved to Git as Team settings saves it (the co-owners unchanged, the result under Background jobs, TVL-12; an error shown under the window) — for those who may change the list; for everyone else it is disabled with the reason shown next to it (TVL-11); it is disabled too while N already is the team default. Setup → Team settings → **Edit…** keeps its own way to change it. | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-10 | The **Master list** tab shall list — alone on the tab, without the expiry tiles or table (TVL-08, TVL-14) — every entry (Tool, Version, Validation date, Validity (months), Due date with where it comes from, **Status** — In source / Not in source now / Added by hand —, Sources, Notes; Added in Columns) with a Status filter (with counts), search, Reset filters, "{shown} of {total} tools" and Export, and offer **＋ Add tool…** (Tool name, Version, Validation date, Validity period in months, Due date, Notes) and per row **Edit** and **Remove** — for a tool from Azure DevOps disabled with the reason (TVL-30). A date or period **changed by hand is kept** when a source other than Azure DevOps is read again (marked "(entered)"); emptied, the sources fill it again; the name and version of a tool from a source are not changed (they are how it is matched). **Remove** asks first in a pop-up naming the tool, and warns when it is still in a source (it comes back the next time that source is read). | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-11 | Only the **Tool Owner** ("Manage pages and menu groups", CON-106) and the **co-owners of the list** (work emails in the shared file, named by a Tool Owner in **Setup → Team settings**, picked as page co-owners are, PGB-18) shall change the master list: add, correct and remove tools, set the team default, add and remove sources, edit their mapping and merge them; everyone else reads it. Fail closed: while the access roles are not loaded (CON-101), without Git storage, with a read-only token, with the synthetic sample, in View as, before the file is read or when it cannot be read, every change is **disabled with the reason** shown next to it (CON-102), and the automatic append of TVL-04 is not saved. | T | M | UC-22, UC-39 |
| TVL-12 | The results of the list's saves (added, changed, removed, merged, the team settings, the automatic append) shall go to **Background jobs** (UI-STANDARDS §8) as "Saved to Git." or the error; an error of a pop-up stays in the pop-up above its buttons; saves waiting or kept after a failure are shown with **Retry** (GIT-17). | T | S | UC-39 |
| TVL-13 | The master list shall **not change** the release counts of Tool Validations: its Release page tiles (QV-64), Pulse Check's Releases and Supported lines and the Monthly pack keep counting the tools of the **Tool Validations query** for the month, as before — with two queries, the Tools and the Signed documents queries as TVL-21 counts them. | T | M | UC-08, UC-39 |
| TVL-14 | The Tool Validations Release page shall have the tabs **Expiry list** — opened first: the three tiles on top, then the Validation expiry table with the window "Expiring in the next N days" and **Set {N} days as team default** (TVL-08, TVL-09), nothing else —, **Master list** — only the master list table with **＋ Add tool…** (TVL-10…TVL-12) and the notices of the list's file in Git, nothing stacked under or above it —, **Tool List [TFS]** — one table: the Tool Validations (Tools) query's rows with every column in its SELECT order and "{n} rows" (TVL-31); before a fetch the usual fetch empty state —, **Validation Documents [TFS]** — only with two queries (TVL-19): one table, every document of the Signed documents query with Tool, Parent ID and Tool List (Azure) before the query's columns (TVL-31); before that query is fetched its fetch empty state with **Fetch signed documents** —; there is **no Releases table** and no **Show: {month} / All months** (the month's tools are the master list's **Current Month Status**, TVL-35, for the header month) — and then **one tab per other source** of the master list (TVL-01), named after the source, in the order the sources were added, shown on need only — then **the owners' tabs** in their order (TVL-15, TVL-16), after the source tabs — and **Setup** last, shown to everyone: **Sources** (TVL-01, with **＋ Add source…** and per source **Edit mapping…**, **Merge into master list…** and **Remove**), under it **Tabs** (TVL-15, TVL-16), under it **Team settings** (the team default and the co-owners, **Edit…**; TVL-09) and under it **Due date highlights** (the bands, **Edit…**; TVL-27), read only with the reason for those who may not change them (TVL-11) and with the notices of the list's file in Git (not connected, not readable, its saves). A source added on Setup gets its tab at once (before Setup); Setup stays open. The tab of one source: the source's rows as read in this browser with its field mapping (Tool, Version, Validation date, Validity (months), Due date and **Master list**: Merged / Not merged yet) with "{n} rows · {n} tools" and Export, **Edit mapping…** and **Merge into master list…** (TVL-05, TVL-07; disabled with the reason as TVL-11), the warning while the mapping is still the guess and, while the source's data is not in this browser, what is missing and how to load it (fetch the query on its page, import the Windchill export or load it from Git, add rows to the table page); it says the source is set up under **Setup → Sources**. Removing a source removes its tab. There are no Releases or Source data tabs of their own; the tiles above the tabs, Pulse Check and the Monthly pack are unchanged (QV-64, TVL-13). The last tab chosen is kept per page for the browser tab (QV-47); a tab kept from before opens where its content is now (Releases, Source data or the **Azure** tab of v10.13…v10.22 → **Tool List [TFS]**; Validation expiry → **Expiry list**; Master list and Setup → themselves), and a tab that no longer exists (a removed source; Validation Documents [TFS] with one query) or an unknown one opens the **Expiry list**. *(Product owner, 24 Nov 2026: the master list is what the team works from; the query is one source among several. Product owner, 24 Nov 2026, v10.15: the Master list tab stacked five sections — Sources and Team settings move to a Setup tab, last. Product owner, 24 Nov 2026, v10.16: the Master list tab still stacked the expiry tiles and table above the master list — the expiry view gets its own tab, **Expiry list**, first, with the team default set there too. Product owner, 24 Nov 2026, v10.23: "1 Tab for Validation Documents [TFS], 1 Tab for Tools List [TFS]. And whatever is present in Releases — remove it." — the Azure tab and its Releases table give way to the two query tabs.)* | T | M | UC-39 |
| TVL-15 | The Tool Owner and the list's co-owners (TVL-11) shall add **tabs of their own** to the Tool Validations page with **＋ Add tab…** in the **Tabs** panel of **Setup** (under Sources; the same place as ＋ Add source…, so the tab row stays the shared `ResultTabs`): a pop-up with the **Page type** — **Table**, **Table + Insights** or **On-Demand Dashboard** (PGB-01, DSH-01) — the **name** (of the tab and its page, up to 40 characters, not the name of another tab of the page nor of another page, PGB-16) and an optional description. Each tab is a **new On-Demand page** created with the page builder's own steps (PGB-05: `on-demand/<page-id>.json` in Git at once, a dashboard's blocks in `dashboards/<page-id>.json` with its first block) and placed **hidden** in `menu-layout.json` (PGB-19) — so it is **not in the menu, on Home, in search or in the menu editor**, and Admin → Pages & menu lists it as Hidden, marked **Tab of Tool Validations** — and added to `tool-validations.json` (TVL-16) as the last of the owners' tabs. The tabs are shown **after the source tabs and before Setup**; Setup stays open after adding one. On its tab the page is **the same page type as anywhere else**: a Table its table (Set up columns…, ＋ Add row, the row drawer, row files and locks, hidden rows, Export), Table + Insights its Data and Insights tabs, a dashboard its filter row, tiles, tabs and blocks (DSH-02…DSH-16) — without a page frame and without the menu's items (＋ Add page…, Rename page…, Hide page, Delete page…), which the Tabs panel replaces. At most 10 tabs. *(Product owner, 24 Nov 2026: "Add provision to add a Tab (can be Table or Insights or Dashboard)".)* | T | S | UC-39 |
| TVL-16 | The owners' tabs shall be kept in `tool-validations.json` as **`tabs`** — each `{ pageId, name, kind }` (the page id as On-Demand page ids, a name of 1 to 40 characters, the page type `table`, `table-insights` or `dashboard`), in their order, written only when there are some — **validated strictly** when read (a bad entry, a page that is a tab twice, a name used twice or more than 10 tabs refused with the reason in "Not used — tool-validations.json in Git: …", TVL-02) and **merged** in the queued save of GIT-17 by page id (a name both saves changed: the later save's; the order of the save that changed it, both: the later save's; a tab missing from that order after the others; a tab one save removed stays removed). In the **Tabs** panel each tab has **←** and **→** (move it one tab left or right among the owners' tabs, saved at once), **Rename…** (the tab and its page, saved to Git) and **Remove…**, which **asks first** in a pop-up saying what happens to the page: **Kept in Git, hidden** (the default; its data stays, a Tool Owner can open, show or delete it in Admin → Pages & menu, the co-owners the list wrote to it are taken off (TVL-25), and it can be made a tab again with ＋ Add existing page as tab…, TVL-24) or **Deleted with its data** (`on-demand/<page-id>.json`, a dashboard's `dashboards/<page-id>.json` and its place in the menu layout; the history stays in GitHub) — deleting is the **Tool Owner's only** (PGB-20), for a co-owner it is disabled with the reason, so a co-owner only removes the tab and the page stays hidden. A tab whose page is no longer in Git says so. The results go to Background jobs (TVL-12). | T | S | UC-39 |

(fail closed, MYD-15); without Git storage they say to connect it. Stream mapping shows a second row **Tool Validations · Signed documents** (named **Tool Validations · Validation Documents** since TVL-33), whose **Add query** creates the connection "Tool Validations Signed documents" (not shown in the menu). The Signed documents connection is the one chosen in **Stream mapping → Tool Validations queries → Signed documents query** (by its view name or name, case and punctuation ignored); on **Automatic** (the default) the connection whose view name or name carries "documents", "docs", "signed" or "signatures" besides the stream's name, else the one whose query selects [System.Parent] and an ApproverName column; the Tools query is the first other connection (the shared setup's first). The panel lists each connection of the stream with its role — **Tools**, **Signed documents** or **Not used** — its rows fetched and how they were told apart. **Backwards compatible**: with one connection, with **None — one query** chosen, or without a Signed documents connection found, the stream counts as before (QV-64, one query). | TVL-20 | The **completion date** that makes a tool Completed shall be a field of the Tools query — **Changed Date** (System.ChangedDate, the "last updated date") **by default** — chosen in **Stream mapping → Tool Validations queries → Completion date (Completed)** — also the validation date of the master list's tools from Azure DevOps (TVL-29) —: a picker offering Changed Date first, then the query's date columns (a reference or display name with "date", or a column whose first value is a date) by name, with the note that **Changed Date moves on any edit** of the tool's work item. The choice and the Signed documents query (TVL-19) are kept in the **shared settings** (`releaseSourceSettings.toolValidations`: `completionField`, a field reference name; `documentsQuery`, a connection name or `@none`) in the planning data in Git (GIT-14), like the other release source settings — set by the Tool Owner (the Stream mapping right, CON-93), read only for everyone else — and **validated when read**: a field reference of letters, digits, _ and . up to 120 characters and a name up to 120 characters; anything else is dropped and the defaults apply. | T | M | UC-08 |

A document whose Parent is not a tool of the Tools query (or that has none) is **Not in Tool List (Azure)** (e.g. a retired tool; until TVL-31 "Not linked to a tool"): listed in Validation Documents (TVL-31), never counted. Both data sets are read **once per fetch into maps by work item ID and month** — a month is a lookup, never a scan of the rows per month or per tool — so a Tools query and some 20,000 documents stay quick. | T | M | UC-08 |

### 5.42 PI Review (PIR) — UC-40

*Group rationale: the UI/UX review (UX-81) found PI Review in the menu as a placeholder; the PI Review plan (24 Nov 2026) delivers it in small releases. This first release reads only: the PI's Features and their Discussions, nothing classified, stored or decided — later releases add the classification, the Git storage and the Move to next PI decision. The second release (PI Review columns, product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "Extract the latest Discussion Comment along with Date. And Split the Learnings, Struggles, Move to Next PI into separate Columns") shows each Feature's newest comment split by the headings the team writes in it — still read only and in memory. The third release (the review form, product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "Going forward for PI 26.4 .. I want this to be updated through the hub where we give dedicated Paragraphs for these fields and ppl fill it in and it gets added to the discussion in Azure") adds the one write: the review, posted as a new comment by the Feature's assignee or a lead (CON-113), in a format the split reads back (PIR-13…PIR-18). The fourth release (the form setup, product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "Make the form editable.. Say in future if something changes, I can tweak it from the UI itself directly"; the editors are the Scrum Master and the Tool Owner) makes the form's fields a shared setup in Git, edited in the column editor the On-Demand pages already have (PIR-19…PIR-23, CON-114). The PI Review start date (product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "Let's do the read discussions from 26.4, older PIs don't show [as an] option. Date filter is set to 21-September-2026") keeps older PIs and comments out of PI Review (PIR-33), and **Edit review…** ("if it is already written, give me a provision to edit it. In My PI Review") lets the author change a review in place instead of posting another (PIR-34). **Write one review for several Features** (26 Nov 2026: "Select multiple and update in 1 go"; one form, the same for all; my existing review of the chosen PI replaced) ticks rows in My PI Review and writes them in one background job, re-checking each Feature before it is written (PIR-36, PIR-37).*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PIR-01 | **Data Insights → PI Review** (Team insights area, page `pireview`) shall replace the placeholder with the PI Review page: the PI picker (PIR-02), the **Features** table (PIR-03, PIR-04), the Feature drawer with its **Discussion** (PIR-05) and **↻ Read all Discussions** (PIR-06). The page shall write nothing to the Git data repository or this browser's database and, to Azure DevOps, only a review posted with **Write review…** (PIR-13) — a new comment in the Feature's Discussion; the Discussions read shall be kept in the page's memory for this page load only, and only the chosen PI in this browser's local storage. | T | M | UC-40 |
| PIR-02 | The **PI** control (one single-select in the table's filter bar) shall offer the PIs of the Time Writing connection's iteration tree (team iterations and the project's iteration tree, `readAzureIterations`) and of the fetched QMS-0025 items (so it works without a connection), each the Iteration Path up to the segment named PI or Program Increment (ATM-30), merged ignoring letter case and sorted **newest first** by the PI's name and numbers. It shall preselect the **current PI** — the PI of the Sprint whose start and finish dates cover today in UTC (NAV-51) — marked "(current)" and with a **Current PI** chip; when two PIs are current at once it shall preselect nothing and say so ("2 PIs are current at the same time (…): choose the PI to review."); when the iterations cannot be read (no PAT, sample data, an error) or no Sprint covers today it shall preselect the latest PI with a Feature and say why in a warning. The viewer's choice shall be remembered in this browser and used while that PI is offered; reading or writing the remembered choice shall never stop the page. **PIR-33**: the list leaves out the PIs that ended before "PI Review starts from", so a remembered choice of such a PI — and a default before it — falls back to the current PI. | T | M | UC-40 |
| PIR-03 | The Features of the chosen PI shall be the fetched work items of type **Feature** whose Iteration Path is in that PI (ignoring letter case), without **Removed** Features and without those under the **absence Epic** (the same rule as the state checks, ATM-07); a toggle **"Also Features with User Stories in this PI"**, **off** by default, shall add the Features with a User Story (not Removed) in the PI. | T | M | UC-40 |

| PIR-25 | **The next PI from today's PI by the Azure DevOps dates** (product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "the next PI should pick from Azure - Next PI based on the dates"; **changed** the same day: "The suggested next PI must ALWAYS be based on TODAY's date, not on the reviewed PI") — **Moves to** in Write review… (PI Review, My PI Review) and in the Close pop-up (PIR-18) shall be worked out from the Time Writing connection's **iteration tree** (`readAzureIterations`, the same read as PIR-02; the Close pop-up reads it when the review is asked), **from today** (UTC; the date is passed in, so it can be tested): (1) **today's PI** is the one dated PI whose period holds today — a PI's period is that of its PI-level iteration, else the span of the dated iterations under it; every PI of the tree counts, future PIs included; the next PI is the dated PI whose **start is the earliest after today's PI's start** (the first one after it ends, or the overlapping one); the same start: the lower PI number; the tree's order and letter case do not matter. **The reviewed PI does not decide the next PI** (a Feature of PI 26.4 reviewed on 10 Jan 2027, in PI 27.1, gets PI 27.2; reviewed during PI 26.4, PI 27.1). Today's PI without a later dated PI: the next PI **by number among the undated PIs of the tree** after it, else its number with the **year rollover**. (2) When **no dated PI holds today, or two do**, the app shall not fall back silently: the next undated PI by number in the tree after the **latest dated PI that ended before today**, else the year rollover from it; with no such PI (an undated tree) or without the iterations (no PAT, sample data, a failed or unfinished read), the next PI by number after the **reviewed PI** — the only PI then known — in the tree and the PI list, else its rollover. The **year rollover**: the PIs in a year are the highest last number of any known "year.number" PI, at least **4**; a PI whose last number reaches it moves to the next year's .1 in the same year format (PI 26.4 → PI 27.1, PI 2026.4 → PI 2027.1), any other adds one (PI 26.2 → PI 26.3); a PI with one number adds one. **PI names** (corrected after the product owner's photo of real data, 25 Nov 2026) are read as "PI 26.4", "PI 2026.4", "PI26.4", "PI-26.4", "PI.26.4", "PI_26.4" or "Program Increment 26.4", with any Unicode space (non-breaking, narrow, thin), zero-width characters, full-width digits or another dot character, and with text before or after the number ("Release PI 25.1", "PI 25.1 (Jan–Mar)", "PI 25.1 - Q1" are PI 25.1); a two-digit year is read as 20xx, so PI 26.4 and PI 2026.4 are the same PI; a **year node** above the PIs ("2026\PI 26.4") is not a PI; the **same PI in several subtrees** (per team or area) is one PI, its dates merged (earliest start, latest finish), its own dates winning over its Sprints'; a "**PI 26.2.1**"-style iteration is a Sprint of PI 26.2, never a PI of its own. A name without a PI number ("PI Planning") is never chosen; the note names such names once each, **at most five**, then "and n more" — never a name that has a PI number. The form shall show **one short line** under Moves to — "From the Azure DevOps dates." or, when a fallback was used, a **warning** ("PI dates missing in Azure DevOps — check this PI.", "No later PI has dates in Azure DevOps — by the PI number; check it.", "PI dates still being read — by the PI number for now.") — and the full note in its ⓘ ("Where the next PI comes from", PIR-32): "From Azure DevOps iteration dates: today (25 Nov 2026) is in PI 2026.4 (5 Oct 2026 – 3 Jan 2027); the next PI is PI 2027.1, starting 4 Jan 2027." (or "… while PI 2026.4 runs until … (the PIs overlap)"), "…, but no later PI has dates: PI 2027.1 is the next PI by number in the Azure DevOps iteration tree (it has no dates).", "The PI dates are missing: no PI in Azure DevOps has dates covering today (…). Used instead: PI 2026.3, the latest PI that ended before today (…) — …", "The PI dates do not decide: 2 PIs have dates covering today (…) — PI 26.4, PI 26.9. Used instead: …", "The PI dates are missing: no PI in the Azure DevOps iteration tree has dates. Used instead: PI 26.4, the reviewed PI — …", or, when Moves to comes from the latest review comment, that and the next PI — and the person can still change it. The PIs-per-year inference and the name rules apply throughout. The form setup preview (PIR-22) keeps its sample PI. **PIR-33**: a next PI that ended before "PI Review starts from" (only possible by the number fallbacks, e.g. the Close pop-up of an old Feature) is never suggested: Moves to is left empty with the warning "The next PI would be before PI Review starts — choose the PI." and the reason in its ⓘ. | T | M | UC-40 |
| PIR-26 | **When a review counts as Reviewed** (product owner, 25 Nov 2026: "My Review should fall either when the Feature was Closed or should lie in the … PI period. If it is from last PI don't consider it as Reviewed"; corrected the same day: "Use the Chosen PI instead of Current PI") — in My PI Review (PIR-17, PIR-24) a review comment shall count for the **PI chosen in the PI filter** when (a) its headings name that PI (PIR-28), or — without a PI in its headings — its date lies within the chosen PI's **date span** from Azure DevOps: its PI-level iteration's dates, else the span of its Sprints' dates (the iteration read and `piDates` of PIR-25); or (b) the Feature is **Closed** now and the comment is dated **on or after its Closed Date** (`Microsoft.VSTS.Common.ClosedDate`, already fetched; the review of the Close pop-up, PIR-18, is posted seconds before the Feature closes, so the **UTC days** are compared). Dates shall be compared as **UTC days**, as the PIs are. A review that meets neither (from an earlier or a later PI) shall be **Not reviewed**, with the hint "Last review {date} ({PI})" — the PI its headings name, else the PI whose dates cover its date, else "another PI"; the count "{n} of {m} reviewed" counts only the reviews that count; **changing the PI filter recomputes** the statuses. **Fallback**: when the chosen PI has no dates (neither its own nor its Sprints') or the iterations are not read (no PAT, sample data, a failed read), every review shall count, as before, and the page shall say so in a warning above the table; while the iterations are being read, a review shows Reading…. **Write review…** keeps the latest review as its prefill and, when that review does not count for the reviewed PI, says "written for {PI}: check it before posting" (also in the Close pop-up). PI Review shows no reviewed status or KPI, so only its Write review… note applies there. **PIR-33**: only the comments on or after "PI Review starts from" are considered (for Reviewed, the hint and the prefill); a comment's date is its **createdDate** — an edit in Azure DevOps (PIR-34) keeps it, so an edited review counts for the same PI. | T | M | UC-40 |
